### MF-070 — Run the 350M scale check and decide whether to attempt 500M

- **Priority / state:** P2 / In progress
- **Depends on:** MF-068, MF-069
- **Deliverable:** measured 350M Modern memory/throughput/stability profile, explicit 500M go/no-go record, and—only if approved—a bounded 500M stretch run.
- **Real plan confirmed (2026-09-12), if the scale check is a go**: train the 350M model for real on the full frozen 3B-token budget, using this session's finalized mixture/tokenizer/architecture decisions (`general-purpose-optimized`, [[MF-095]]; the tokenizer decision pending the stage-2 gpt4+digit-split comparison currently running; [[MF-107]] full RoPE; [[MF-121]]'s expanded GitHub-code allowlist) -- not just the profiling-scale pass the task's own original scope describes. For that real run's data prep, use `scripts/prepare_data.py --export-parquet-dir` ([[MF-125]]) on each of the 5 mixture sources, then `scripts/build_dataset_card.py` ([[MF-126]]) to generate a real dataset card (one Hugging Face dataset repo, each source its own named config), then publish manually via the Hub's own web upload UI (the user's stated preference, 2026-09-12) or `huggingface-cli upload ... --repo-type dataset` -- no custom upload code either way, see MF-126. Ebooks ([[MF-124]]) stay excluded by default from this real run; that source remains a separate, future, not-yet-sourced candidate.
- **Token-budget plan discussed (2026-09-13), real open question, not yet resolved: 3B tokens vs. a larger (Chinchilla-closer) budget for 350M.** `AGENTS.md`'s own frozen 3B-token target (`MF-063`) sits almost exactly at the classic ~20-tokens/parameter Chinchilla ratio *for 150M* (`150M x 20 ~= 3B`) -- reusing the same 3B budget unchanged for 350M (this task's own real-plan note above) would put 350M at only ~8.6 tokens/param, well under its own ~7B Chinchilla-optimal point. Not obviously a mistake either way: naive Chinchilla-optimal ratios optimize *training* compute only, and every well-regarded recent small model (SmolLM2, Qwen2.5, Llama 3's small variants) deliberately trains well past that ratio because a small model's real value is cheap, repeated *inference* -- a consideration this project's own consumer-hardware-usable-model goal is well aligned with, not against. **Real decision needed once this task's own scale-check produces real 350M throughput numbers**: keep 3B (matches the already-frozen protocol, cheaper, directly comparable to the 150M runs) or scale up toward ~7B (closer to compute-optimal for 350M specifically, real added GPU cost).
  **A real, code-verified gap found while discussing this, relevant to whichever budget is chosen: 3B-then-maybe-extend-to-7B is not cleanly supported today.** Checked `train/pretrain.py` directly: `--resume` (line 344) requires the freshly-built `TrainingConfig` -- including `max_updates`, set from `--updates` -- to exactly match what the checkpoint stored, raising `ValueError` otherwise; a checkpoint configured for a 3B-token `--updates` cannot be resumed with a larger value. Separately, WSD's own decay window (`wsd_decay_start_update`, `training.py`) is defined as the last `wsd_decay_fraction` (default 20%) of `max_updates` -- reaching the full 3B tokens under a 3B-configured run means the LR has already been annealed to its minimum, so even a code change permitting a larger `--updates` on resume would still be extending training from an already-bottomed-out LR, not a clean continuation. **The WSD-correct way to preserve real flexibility** is to configure `max_updates` for the larger possible budget (e.g. 7B) from the start and simply stop early at 3B if that proves sufficient -- at that point the run is still in the flat "stable" phase (LR at plateau, undecayed), exactly what WSD's stable phase is for. **But there is currently no mechanism in this codebase to manually trigger "decay to completion now" on a stopped-early stable-phase checkpoint** -- checked, does not exist. Real, small follow-on feature needed before this specific flexibility is actually usable, not just a config choice; not yet scoped as its own task.
- **Post-pretrain sequencing recorded (2026-09-13), directly answering the user's own question, per [[MF-098]]'s already-recorded pipeline shape.** Base pretrain (this task) is completely independent of, and unaffected by, whatever happens after it -- nothing about the 350M run needs to prepare for a later distillation/SFT/DPO stage. The real order, once a trained 350M checkpoint exists: base pretrain (this task) -> [[MF-098]]'s distillation (if greenlit -- still gated on its own unresolved RL-adjacency and teacher-output-provenance decisions, not yet scoped as real work) -> focused SFT ([[MF-123]]'s reasoning-demonstration sourcing feeds this) -> preference optimization (DPO-style, also part of [[MF-098]]'s scope). **User's own framing, worth keeping verbatim: "we will see where the big ROI [is] for those with this model"** -- i.e. this ordering is not a commitment to build all three stages, only the sequence if some subset is pursued; which stage(s) are actually worth the real effort is an open, deliberately-deferred call to make once a real 350M checkpoint exists to evaluate against. **User's own plan for a distillation teacher, recorded**: a quantized (Q4-family GGUF) teacher run locally via `llama.cpp`, "probably later" -- consistent with [[MF-098]]'s own already-recorded hardware caveat (most real strong-teacher candidates need ~17-22GB even at Q4_K_M, more than this project's own 8GB reference card) but plausibly workable via `llama.cpp`'s real CPU/GPU-split inference (slower, not requiring the whole model to fit in VRAM) rather than requiring a separate, more capable machine as that note assumed.
- **Plan recorded (2026-09-13), user decision, for the scale-check step itself: test `head_dim=96` (the now-adopted Modern default, [[MF-108]]) at 350M scale; if VRAM is too tight there, fall back to checking `head_dim=64` at 350M too, rather than assuming either transfers unchecked from 150M.** Directly closes the real, honest caveat [[MF-108]]'s own entry already carried forward: "50M/350M/500M inherit `head_dim=96` by architectural consistency, not independent per-scale verification -- 350M... has less VRAM headroom to begin with and is untested at head_dim=96." The scale-check's own real VRAM/throughput measurement (this task's core deliverable) is the natural place to resolve this, not a separate task.
- **Real result (2026-09-14): decisive, and bigger than the head_dim question alone -- `configs/350m-modern.toml` real-tested at its own actual declared config and found not viable on this local 8GB card, regardless of head_dim.** Full real investigation, in order:
  - **A real methodology mistake caught and corrected before it produced a false-positive result**: the first scale-check attempt reused the stale `data/shards/mf064-150m-train` pool, packed at `sequence_length=1024` -- not the config's own declared `max_seq_len=2048`. Caught directly (user correction) before drawing any conclusion from it; a real, fresh shard pool was built instead (`data/shards/mf070-350m-scale-check-2048`, real FineWeb-Edu, 3,000 documents, packed at the actual declared `sequence_length=2048`, 3,100,672 real train tokens) so the real measurement is of the config this project actually ships, not a shorter, easier one. This is the same real gap [[MF-101]]'s own entry above documents generally (every VRAM number on record before this was measured at half the declared context) -- here it was caught in the act, not after the fact.
  - **`head_dim=96` (the 150M-adopted default) at the real declared `seq_len=2048`: a genuine, confirmed VRAM-paging collapse.** Real diagnostic (`train/pretrain.py`, `batch_size=2`, `--progress-interval 2`): **~70 tok/s**, VRAM **7,906 MiB / 8,192 MiB (96.5%)**, 100% reported utilization -- the same real signature as [[MF-108]]'s own `head_dim=128`-at-150M collapse, not merely "slower."
  - **`head_dim=64` (reverted) at the same real `seq_len=2048`: still a real, stable, near-identical collapse -- confirming the problem is not head_dim.** ~92-93 tok/s, stable across four real consecutive progress checkpoints (updates 2/4/6/8, not a one-off early reading), VRAM 7,933 MiB -- essentially the same footprint as the `head_dim=96` arm despite the parameter-count difference. This directly disproves the working assumption that reverting head_dim alone would fix it.
  - **`--loss-chunk-size 256` added on top of `head_dim=64`: no improvement (~93-94 tok/s).** [[MF-101]]'s own newly-documented gap (this exact lever never having been tried in any VRAM measurement on record) was closed here directly -- and it does not help at this scale. The `[B, S, vocab_size]` logits tensor this flag removes is a comparatively small fraction of an already-near-8GB footprint dominated by something else (350M's own parameters/optimizer state/activations at real `seq_len=2048`, across 28 layers).
  - **`--activation-checkpointing` added on top of `head_dim=64`: a real, meaningful improvement, but not a fix.** Ran a full real 10-update diagnostic to completion: **155.6 tok/s** (stabilized after an initial higher reading before the recompute-vs-store tradeoff's own steady state kicked in) -- a genuine ~1.7x win over the ~92 tok/s baseline, confirming this specific lever (trading compute for memory across all layers, a different mechanism from chunked loss) does help here. VRAM stayed essentially unchanged (~7,900 MiB) despite the throughput win, consistent with this project's own established understanding that `nvidia-smi`'s reading reflects the allocator's reserved pool, not necessarily the instantaneous peak a checkpointing-reduced run actually needs.
  - **Real, decisive wall-clock implication: even at the best local configuration found (155.6 tok/s), a real 3B-token run would take ~223 days.** Not a viable local option at any reasonable token budget for this preset's real release run.
  - **Decision (2026-09-14), user-confirmed: recorded as a real NO-GO for training 350M's real release run on this local 8GB reference card.** `configs/350m-modern.toml` reverted to its own derived `head_dim=64` (the `head_dim_override=96` line removed, replaced with a comment explaining why -- 150M keeps `head_dim=96` unchanged, this reversion is 350M-specific, not a retraction of [[MF-108]]'s own 150M result). The real 350M/3B-token release run is deferred to rented hardware with real VRAM headroom, matching [[MF-117]]'s own already-recorded pre-rental-hardware-revalidation plan -- every one of this local card's own hardware-specific findings (`batch_size=2` ceiling, `head_dim=96` not transferring past 150M, activation-checkpointing's own real cost/benefit here) needs re-checking on whatever hardware is actually rented, not assumed to transfer. **`batch_size=1` locally, and an intermediate model size between 150M and 350M, were both considered as alternatives and explicitly not pursued** -- the rented-hardware path was judged the more direct route to the real, full-quality 350M/3B-token run this project actually wants, rather than compromising the preset's own already-frozen architecture/token-budget further to force a fit on hardware this task's own real evidence says is not suited to it at this size.
  - **Two hardcoded test literals updated for this reversion**: `tests/test_config.py`'s `test_all_frozen_presets_validate` (350m-modern now expects `head_dim=64`, not 96) and `tests/test_scale.py`'s 350m-modern parameter-count literal (332,919,744, reverted from the brief 369,621,696 under `head_dim=96`). Full fast suite and `ruff` re-checked after these changes.
  - **This also functionally answers this task's own "500M go/no-go" deliverable, though not the way originally framed**: 500M is strictly larger than 350M in every dimension that just failed locally, so a local 500M attempt is not a separate open question -- it inherits the same no-go, automatically, without needing its own dedicated probe. The real 500M question (like 350M's) is deferred to whatever is learned once rented hardware is actually in use.
- **Urgent, more consequential finding surfaced by the user's own follow-up question (2026-09-14): the same "measured at half the declared context" gap applies to the currently-adopted, required V1 release preset, not just the optional 350M scale-check target -- and it is real, precisely quantified, and now resolved.** The user asked directly whether "150M at seq_len=2048" and "350M at seq_len=1024" had actually been measured -- neither had. Real, direct tests of both, plus the two most promising fixes, run in sequence:
  - **The original, real, "healthy" `head_dim=96` measurement re-checked at its own source**: `artifacts/mf108-head-dim/96/run.json` (the actual real run [[MF-108]]'s adoption decision was based on) shows `peak_reserved_vram_bytes = 6.37 GB` against `total_vram_bytes = 8.59 GB` -- genuinely, comfortably under budget (74%), not paging. That original result was real and correctly measured; the gap is that it was only ever measured at `sequence_length=1024`, half of `max_seq_len=2048`'s own declared value.
  - **The same config (150M, `head_dim=96`, batch_size=2) at the real declared `seq_len=2048`: real, precisely quantified paging.** `peak_reserved_vram_bytes = 12.37 GB` against the same real `8.59 GB` physical total -- **44% over physical capacity**, not a guess from an ambiguous `nvidia-smi` reading this time, a direct field in the run's own real, persisted metrics. Throughput collapsed to **~239 tok/s** (real, stable across 5 consecutive progress checkpoints) -- roughly 11-13x slower than the same config's own real 1024-context throughput (2,600-3,107 tok/s across the two real measurements on record). **This means the currently-adopted, required V1 release preset (150M-Modern, `head_dim=96`) does not actually fit in 8GB at its own frozen, declared `max_seq_len=2048` -- a real problem for a required release artifact, not an optional stretch target.**
  - **`350M`/`head_dim=96` at the real declared `seq_len=1024` (the missing data point, tested with a scratch config restoring `head_dim_override=96` since the real `350m-modern.toml` had already been reverted to 64 above): also real, confirmed paging, even at the shorter context.** `peak_reserved_vram_bytes = 12.15 GB` / `8.59 GB` physical, ~150.8 tok/s. Confirms `head_dim=96` itself is not viable at 350M scale at any tested sequence length, independent of context -- consistent with, and further strengthening, this task's own already-recorded 350M reversion to `head_dim=64` above (which was decided from the `seq_len=2048` evidence alone; this closes the remaining "what about at 1024" gap for completeness).
  - **The fix for the 150M case, tested directly and confirmed to fully work: `--activation-checkpointing`.** Same 150M/`head_dim=96`/real `seq_len=2048` config, `--activation-checkpointing` added: **`peak_reserved_vram_bytes = 5.59 GB`** against the same `8.59 GB` physical total -- comfortably under budget (65%), genuinely no paging. Throughput recovered to **~1,966 tok/s** (still rising at the 10th update, not yet fully settled) -- a real ~33-38% cost relative to the healthy 1024-context throughput, not the ~11-13x collapse without it. **`head_dim=96` does not need to be reverted for 150M** -- unlike 350M (where the same lever only partially helped a model whose footprint is too large regardless), 150M's real footprint is small enough that activation checkpointing alone closes the real gap between its 1024-context and 2048-context memory needs.
  - **Real, new, necessary operational requirement, not previously known or documented**: any real 150M-Modern training run (the already-completed [[MF-064]]/[[MF-065]] release runs, and any future one) that trains at anything approaching the full declared `max_seq_len=2048` context **must** pass `--activation-checkpointing`, or risk the same real paging collapse just measured. This was never required or disclosed before, because every prior VRAM measurement behind [[MF-108]]'s own adoption decision (and this project's general practice) used `sequence_length=1024` packed shards. See [[MF-108]]'s own entry for the corresponding addendum recording this same finding at its source.
  - Real diagnostic artifacts (all four runs above, plus the earlier 350M/[[head_dim=64]]/`seq_len=2048` combination) were throwaway 10-update probes (`--no-checkpoint`, deleted after reading each real `run.json`) -- not full quality-bearing runs; the real numbers (peak reserved VRAM, tokens/second) are real and persisted in this entry, not estimated.
- **Resolved (2026-09-13), user decision: `configs/350m-modern.toml`'s GQA ratio (16 heads/4 KV heads, 4:1) stays as-is, no extra bounded arm.** An exact 3:1 was never achievable at 16 heads anyway (not evenly divisible by 3) without a bigger `n_heads` change than warranted here. The user accepted the directional evidence from [[MF-081]]'s 150M-built sweep (6:1 neutral, 12:1/MQA a small regression -- 4:1 sits much closer to the neutral point than the regressing one) as sufficient, explicitly declining to spend additional GPU time on a targeted 350M-scale confirmation arm before the real run. Not re-opened unless a real result later suggests otherwise. `global_rope_theta` similarly confirmed staying unset (same turn) -- the -0.21% CE result sits at the noise floor's edge and the actual motivating long-context question is unconfirmed, not strong enough evidence to adopt.
- **Acceptance:** the real single-GPU decision includes VRAM, throughput, time/cost, checkpointing needs, learning behavior, expected learning value, and stop criteria; failures remain first-class results and V1 artifacts are unchanged.
- **Status note:** trainer/profiler CUDA fields, measurement assembly, and the fail-closed decision tool are implemented. A decision cannot be emitted from CPU, failed, incomplete, or unmeasured evidence. The real 350M run and any 500M decision remain open.
- **Pre-work (2026-08-27): local_window tuning check, before scaling to 350M.** After [[MF-066]]'s 150M comparison showed Modern's validation PPL slightly worse than Edu's (50.1 vs 44.1), and after external review (`future-plan.md`/`more-context.md`) flagged that Muse Glimmer uses a much larger local-window-to-context ratio than our frozen `local_window=512`, the user asked to check whether our window was starving local layers before carrying a possibly-suboptimal config into the 350M run. First confirmed a real, useful hardware fact: eager (uncompiled) FlexAttention's compute/VRAM cost on this GPU is **independent of window size** (512 vs 1024: 4224 vs 4286 tok/s, identical 5.63GB peak — eager mode materializes the full score matrix regardless of the mask, so a larger window is free here). Then ran a real matched comparison (150m-modern, real data from `data/shards/mf064-150m-train`, 10.23M tokens, seed 42, batch=2, FP16, identical everything except `local_window`): **local_window=512 vs 1024 produced no meaningful difference** — validation cross-entropy 5.145 vs 5.147, perplexity 171.6 vs 171.9, bits/byte 1.7308 vs 1.7314. **Conclusion: local_window=512 is not starving local layers at this scale; the frozen config is not the explanation for Modern's slightly-worse MF-066 PPL, and there is no evidence to justify deviating from it before MF-070.** A real, tested null result, not an untested assumption either way.
- **Pre-work (2026-08-27 to 2026-09-01, Done): 1B-token 150m-modern training pass, before MF-070.** By explicit user decision, extended [[MF-065]]'s Modern checkpoint from ~150M to a real 1B-token budget (still short of the frozen 3B target — explicitly undertrained, matching [[MF-064]]/[[MF-065]]'s own labeling) before running MF-070's 350M profiling, so the eventual 350M/500M go/no-go decision is informed by a more-trained Modern rather than the earlier 150M-token snapshot. Real data: a new `data/shards/modern-1b-train` pool, 320,709,632 real FineWeb-Edu train tokens (pinned revision, documents 47,000+, past every range used by the tokenizer/gate/first 150M pool; 279,533/280,000 admitted, 283 exact + 184 near duplicates rejected), 1,540,096 validation tokens — giving ~3.1 epochs for the 1B-token budget rather than the ~22 epochs the smaller existing pool would have required. Data prep hit a real transient HF Hub read-timeout on one large parquet shard mid-run (unrelated to any repo rename — `HuggingFaceFW/fineweb-edu` was independently confirmed reachable and unchanged via direct API/resolve checks); fixed by clearing the partial output and retrying with `HF_HUB_DOWNLOAD_TIMEOUT=180`, which completed cleanly. Config: `configs/150m-modern.toml` (`local_window=512`, unchanged per the tuning check above), FP16, batch_size=2, seed=42, 488,280 updates targeting ~1B consumed tokens, launched via `train/pretrain.py --device cuda`.
  - **Real interruptions and a real bug found along the way**: the run was killed twice while running as a background task inside this session (once after ~21h/126,000 updates, once after a short partial resume) with no error in the training process itself — external kills, cause never fully diagnosed but suspected tied to this session's own process lifecycle across a multi-day conversation, not the machine sleeping (confirmed by the user: no lock/sleep occurred). The first kill exposed a real, previously-unknown bug: `save_training_checkpoint` wrote checkpoint files non-atomically, so the kill left `checkpoint-00147000` with only `model.safetensors` and no `config.json`/`trainer_state.json` — silently unresumable, losing ~21,000 updates (~3 hours) rolling back to `checkpoint-00126000`. Fixed for real as [[MF-079]] (atomic stage-then-rename checkpoint writes, new regression test). After the second kill, training was moved to the user's own foreground terminal, independent of this session — from which it ran the remaining ~362,000 updates to completion without further interruption, monitored via a self-paced `/loop` polling checkpoint files/GPU state hourly.
  - **Real result** (`artifacts/mf065-150m-modern-1b/final/trainer_state.json`): 488,280/488,280 updates completed, 999,020,880 tokens consumed (~1B), final train loss 9.7→**3.431**, learning rate correctly annealed to the configured minimum (3e-5).
  - **Validation** (`reports/mf065-150m-modern-1b-validation.json`, same-seed untrained model as "before" baseline, real held-out `data/shards/modern-1b-train/validation`, 1,538,592 predicted tokens): cross-entropy 10.17→**3.49**, perplexity 26,194→**32.8**, bits/byte 3.45→**1.18** — a real, substantial improvement over the earlier 150M-token Modern checkpoint's validation (CE 3.91, PPL 50.1, BPB 1.32; see [[MF-065]]), and validation loss dropped in step with training loss (no overfitting signal) despite the ~3.1x data-repetition (~3.1 epochs) this budget required.
  - **Inference evidence**: exported via `scripts/export.py --keep-source` to `artifacts/mf065-150m-modern-1b-release`; a real GPU FP16 greedy sample: *"The history of science shows that the world was created in the first place. The world was created in the first place..."* — grammatical but degenerately repetitive under greedy (temperature 0) decoding, a well-known small-model/greedy-decoding artifact rather than a regression (validation metrics improved substantially); not yet tested with non-zero temperature/sampling.
  - Still undertrained relative to the frozen 3B-token target (per [[MF-063]]); this pre-work's purpose — giving MF-070's eventual 350M/500M decision a more-trained Modern reference point than the 150M snapshot — is complete.
- **Pre-work (2026-09-02/03, Done): Muon/local-window/NoPE verification, per the approved plan at `before-mf-70-we-polymorphic-puddle`.** After the user pasted external architecture advice (MTP, local_window→2048, "hybrid sliding KV cache," Muon) ahead of the 350M run, a verification pass against the actual codebase found the external advice wrong or unverified in several places — the "missing" ring KV cache already exists and is measured (~3.4x savings, MF-050), MTP is correctly out of V1 scope, and both Muon (MF-057) and Global NoPE (MF-042) had real evidence gaps. Also found: the 2026-08-27 local-window pre-work above was flawed the same way `local_window=1024` always is at `sequence_length=1024` (a no-op, per `masking.py`'s window rule) — corrected below. Scope: 11 arms across 3 experiments (~7.4h GPU time), executed on real `data/shards/mf064-150m-train`, single seed 42, ~10.23M tokens/arm, run in the user's own foreground terminal after repeated background-task kills in this session (same fix as the 1B-token run), monitored via a self-paced `/loop`.
  - **Muon vs AdamW** (`reports/mf070-pre-muon-vs-adamw.md`, also recorded on [[MF-057]]): Muon's best (lr=3e-3) beats AdamW's best (lr=1e-3) on quality — validation PPL 121.9 vs 169.0 — but at ~2.3x slower throughput (~1,890 vs ~4,340 tok/s) and lower VRAM (5.08GB vs 5.63GB). Muon's LR ceiling wasn't found (still improving at the top of the tested range); a higher-LR follow-up and quality-per-wall-clock-time comparison remain open.
  - **Corrected local-window re-test** (`reports/mf070-local-window-corrected.md`): genuinely restrictive windows this time (128/256/512, all `< sequence_length=1024`). Validation PPL essentially flat (170.5/170.8/171.6) — confirms, with valid evidence, the earlier null result: **local window size doesn't measurably affect quality at this scale**, restricted or not. `local_window=512` stays the frozen default with no evidence to change it. `window=1024`/`2048` deliberately not tested (would be mathematically identical no-ops at this sequence length — see the report for why).
  - **Global NoPE quality** (`reports/mf070-global-nope-quality.md`): RoPE slightly beats NoPE in-distribution (PPL 171.6 vs 173.8). RoPE remains the correct default.
  - **Bonus: RoPE-vs-NoPE long-context extrapolation** (`reports/mf070-nope-long-context-extrapolation.md`, user-requested follow-up) — the quality test above can't address NoPE's actual research claim (length generalization), so both checkpoints were evaluated on fresh, real 2048-token sequences (a new `data/shards/mf070-longcontext-validation` pool, never seen at that length during training), with per-position cross-entropy bucketed early (0-1023) vs late (1024-2047, genuine extrapolation). Neither checkpoint degrades at 2x training length (both score *better* late than early); RoPE stays ahead in absolute terms at both segments; the two checkpoints' early→late improvement differs by only ~0.007 nats — within single-seed noise. **No evidence of a NoPE extrapolation advantage at this scale/range.**
  - **Net effect on MF-070's upcoming 350M run**: keep `local_window=512` and RoPE (both evidence-backed now, not just inherited defaults); optimizer choice (AdamW vs Muon) was resolved by the Muon LR-ceiling/speed follow-up below.
- **Pre-work (2026-09-03, Done): Muon LR-ceiling, speed-optimization, and wall-clock-matched-AdamW follow-up** (`reports/mf070-muon-followup.md`), closing every open question left by the Muon-vs-AdamW pass above. Six arms, real `data/shards/mf064-150m-train`, seed 42, ~10.23M tokens/arm (23.94M for the wall-clock-matched arm), run in the user's own foreground terminal, monitored via a self-paced `/loop`. User explicitly asked to also queue the "cutting-edge" Muon speed research discussed earlier; a source read of the installed `torch.optim._muon` found its Newton-Schulz iteration hardcoded to `bfloat16` with no public override — a real, verifiable lead — while the other cutting-edge ideas (Hierarchical/Tiled Newton-Schulz, Chebyshev acceleration, "Turbo-Muon") were deliberately not implemented from memory for an unattended multi-hour run (unacceptable risk of a silent, uncaught correctness bug; see finding 3 below for why that risk is real, not hypothetical).
  - **LR ceiling still not found**: lr=1e-2 (ns_steps=5, bf16) scored val PPL 110.75 — better than 3e-3's 121.9, continuing the monotonic improvement. Same throughput/VRAM as 3e-3 (LR doesn't change compute cost).
  - **`ns_steps=3` is a real, honest tradeoff, not free**: ~29% faster (2,429 vs 1,886 tok/s) but ~4% worse PPL (126.99 vs 121.9) at the same lr=3e-3.
  - **`torch.compile` on Muon's optimizer step is broken on this build — avoid it entirely.** Two arms tested it; neither gave any real speedup (one was even slightly slower), and both corrupted quality relative to their uncompiled controls — catastrophically at ns_steps=5 (PPL 389 vs 122, +219%) and moderately at ns_steps=3 (PPL 137 vs 127, +8%), an inconsistent, config-dependent severity not fully diagnosed. An earlier isolated 10-step synthetic smoke test showed a clean ~16% speedup with no visible issue — too short to expose whatever this is. Unrelated to [[MF-078]]'s separate, already-closed FlexAttention-compile-failure finding (independently reconfirmed this session, unchanged), but together both suggest `torch.compile` is broadly unreliable in this project's current PyTorch/CUDA/Windows setup.
  - **FP32 Newton-Schulz is a clean win — the standout finding.** Replacing `torch.optim.Muon`'s hardcoded internal `bfloat16` cast with FP32 (verified monkeypatch, byte-for-byte identical math otherwise, same algorithm this project's own tested `newton_schulz_reference` implements) gave **~21% higher throughput** (2,277.5 vs 1,886 tok/s) with **essentially identical quality** (CE 4.800 vs 4.803) at identical VRAM — confirms this RTX 2070 Super's lack of native BF16 tensor cores (per [[MF-049]]) was costing real, avoidable speed inside Muon's own optimizer internals, not just in model training precision.
  - **Quality-per-wall-clock-time reverses the earlier quality-per-token conclusion — AdamW wins.** AdamW lr=1e-3 run for 11,700 updates (23.94M tokens, 5,602s wall — matched to Muon 3e-3's 5,423s) scored **val CE 4.504 / PPL 90.38**, beating every Muon arm in this pass including the best one found (lr=1e-2: PPL 110.75). At matched *time* rather than matched *tokens* — the metric that actually matters for a fixed-compute-budget run like MF-070's 350M — **AdamW is the better choice on this hardware.**
  - **Resolved recommendation for MF-070's 350M run**: use **AdamW**, not Muon, given the wall-clock-matched result above. Confirmed by a direct follow-up test (2026-09-04, see [[MF-057]]'s status note): a "best case" hybrid Muon config combining every real speed win found (FP32 Newton-Schulz + `ns_steps=3`) at Muon's best known LR (1e-2), run wall-clock-matched to AdamW, still lost — real PPL 95.86 vs AdamW's real 90.38. An earlier extrapolation had suggested the hybrid might win; the real test overturned that estimate. If Muon is used regardless (e.g. for its lower-VRAM footprint, 5.08GB vs AdamW's 5.63GB), use the `fp32-ns-3e-3` configuration (FP32 Newton-Schulz, no compile) — never `--compile-optimizer-step`, and treat `ns_steps=3` as a real quality tradeoff, not a free speedup.
- **Pre-work (2026-09-04 to 2026-09-06, Done): bounded Multi-Token Prediction (MTP) quality test, run before MF-070 by explicit user decision.** After confirming AdamW/local-window/RoPE above, the user asked to also evaluate MTP (frozen out of V1 scope until this test) rather than starting MF-070 immediately. Governance: `AGENTS.md` was amended (2026-09-04, user-approved) to carve out MTP as a bounded, off-by-default, training-only experiment configured entirely through `TrainingConfig`, never touching `ModelConfig` or `MiniFrontier`'s `state_dict()` — verified empirically (byte-identical `config.json`, identical safetensors key set with/without MTP) to preserve full compatibility with every already-released checkpoint. Implemented `src/minifrontier/mtp.py` (`MTPHeads`: simplified independent linear heads per extra offset, not DeepSeek-V3's sequential chained-transformer-block design — considered and explicitly deferred, see the module docstring and `reports/mf070-mtp-quality.md`), wired into `training.py`'s `train_updates`, and `scripts/compare_mtp.py` (matched-token baseline-vs-MTP comparison, mirroring `compare_optimizers.py`'s methodology). A literature-based prediction (DeepSeek-V3 arXiv:2412.19437; Gloeckle et al. arXiv:2404.19737) was recorded in `reports/mf070-mtp-quality.md` *before* the real run.
  - **A real production bug was found and fixed before the real run completed**: the first attempt crashed with a cross-device `RuntimeError` (`MTPHeads` built on CPU in `compare_mtp.py`, never moved to CUDA, unlike the main model) — fixed by moving `mtp_heads.to(torch_device)` inside `train_updates` itself, alongside a new CUDA regression test. The crashed run's baseline arm had already completed and was kept as a valid partial result.
  - **Real result** (`reports/mf070-mtp-quality.md`, real data, seed 42, 10.23M tokens/arm, `n_extra_heads=1`/t+2 only matching DeepSeek-V3's own real choice, `mtp_loss_weight=0.3` fixed not decayed): MTP gave a **small, consistent improvement on held-out validation quality** — cross-entropy 5.137→5.130 (−0.14%), perplexity 170.3→169.0 (−0.74%), bits/byte 1.7283→1.7258 (−0.14%) — alongside a very slightly worse primary train loss (+0.06%, within noise) and a real **~5.3% wall-clock slowdown** (4189→3977 tok/s). The baseline arm reproduced the crashed run's numbers bit-for-bit. Matches the pre-registered prediction (small, uncertain effect, not a large unambiguous win) exactly.
  - **Decision: MTP stays off (default) for MF-070's 350M run.** The measured trade — a small quality gain at a real wall-clock cost — mirrors the Muon-vs-AdamW decision above: this project's actual bottleneck is wall-clock time, not parameter count or per-token quality, and a single-seed ~10M-token result is too thin to justify the added training-time cost in a real multi-hour run. MTP remains implemented, tested, and available for future revisiting (a second seed, or larger scale where the literature predicts a bigger effect) as independent follow-up work, not a blocker for MF-070.
- **Pre-work (2026-09-10): batch-size/accumulation flags for the real launch command, per [[MF-101]]'s decision.** [[MF-101]]'s real sweep confirmed `--batch-size 2` (already the default) and found a free, real throughput win from raising `--accumulation-steps` off its untested default of 1 — +3.8% at 8, +9.4% at 32, identical peak VRAM in both cases. Recommended flags for MF-070's eventual real launch command: `--batch-size 2 --accumulation-steps 32` on `configs/350m-modern.toml`. This is a flag recommendation only, not a complete launch command — the real 3B-token-scale training shard pool for MF-070 itself does not exist yet (no `data/shards/mf070-*` pool has been prepared; every shard pool built so far, e.g. `data/shards/mf064-150m-train`/`modern-1b-train`, was sized for smaller pre-work runs) and that data-prep step remains open, unblocked by this note. MF-101's own report also flags that this ratio is untested at 350M scale (larger activations could hit VRAM headroom differently than at 150M) — worth a quick real check (e.g. watching peak memory on the first few real updates) once the actual run starts, rather than assumed to transfer unchanged.
- **Real launch executed (2026-09-14), `scripts/run_mf070_150m_release.ps1`: the real 3B-token 150M-Modern release run (not 350M -- see the real local NO-GO decided above), data prep for all 5 [[MF-095]]-adopted mixture sources at the real declared `seq_len=2048`, then the real training launch (`head_dim=96`, `--activation-checkpointing`, MTP heads on, WSD, `batch_size=2`/`accumulation_steps=1` -- the exact real-measured-safe combination, not MF-101's untested-at-this-recipe 8/32).** Data prep run smallest-source-first (cosmopedia-v2 → finemath → fineweb-edu → dclm-edu → github-code) specifically so a real per-source wall-clock number would surface within minutes rather than after waiting through the largest source blind -- this paid off immediately, see the real numbers below.
  - **Real per-source prep rate and real tokens/doc, useful for sizing `--limit` on any future data-prep run at this scale (none of this was measured before; every `--limit` above except fineweb-edu's own in-session precedent was a reasoned estimate)**:
    | source | docs (`--limit`) | wall time | docs/sec | real tokens | real tokens/doc | this task's own prior estimate |
    |---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
    | cosmopedia-v2 | 110,000 | 13m51s | ~132.4 | 81,149,952 | **~738** | ~1,500 (too high) |
    | finemath | 620,000 | 2h34m07s | ~67.1 | 924,231,680 | **~1,491** | ~800 (too low) |
    | fineweb-edu | 800,000 | 2h46m32s | ~80.1 | 891,908,096 | **~1,115** | ~1,034 (this session's own MF-070 350M-scale-check precedent, held up well) |
    Real, useful lesson: reasoned tokens/doc estimates for content this project has never actually processed (cosmopedia-v2, finemath) were off by roughly 2x in *opposite* directions -- cosmopedia-v2's real synthetic-textbook content ran shorter per document than assumed, finemath's real web-math content ran almost twice as long. Only fineweb-edu, backed by an actual prior in-session measurement rather than a guess, came in close. **For any future `--limit` sizing at this project's own real scale, use this table's real tokens/doc figures directly instead of a fresh guess** -- dclm-edu and github-code's own real figures remain open (see below), and github-code in particular has no real precedent yet at all.
  - **Real, decisive finding: a per-source token-count miss against `--limit`'s target does not threaten the trained mixture proportions, only each source's real repetition (epoch) count over the run -- and this project already has direct, real evidence that its own tolerance for repetition is generous.** `--mixture`'s weight arguments (not pool size) are what `MixtureBatchProvider` actually honors during training; a smaller-than-planned pool is simply resampled (epoched) more, a larger one less. Real epoch counts implied by the table above, against each source's real share of the 3B-token target (`weight x 3e9`): cosmopedia-v2 (needs 150M, has 81.1M) → **~1.85 epochs**; finemath (needs 450M, has 924.2M) → **~0.49 epochs** (well under one full pass, the surplus data simply goes unused this run, not harmful, just more prep than strictly needed); fineweb-edu (needs 750M, has 891.9M) → **~0.84 epochs**. **Decision: none of this needs fixing or topping up.** This project's own real precedent, [[MF-065]]'s 1B-token pre-work run above, repeated its *entire* data pool **~3.1 epochs** with **no overfitting signal** (validation loss dropped in step with training loss throughout) -- cosmopedia-v2's ~1.85 epochs here is well under that already-validated-safe figure, on top of being only a 5%-weighted mixture slice. **The one real, still-open exception worth checking rather than assuming fine: github-code carries a bigger weight (20%, needs 600M tokens) than cosmopedia-v2's 5% case, so a real shortfall there (this project's least-precedented `--limit` estimate, ~500 tokens/doc assumed, no real basis) would matter more if it lands far under target — check its real `metadata.json` once it completes, don't assume the cosmopedia-v2 precedent transfers automatically.**
  - **dclm-edu completed (2026-09-14, 09:03:12 -> 14:17:51, ~5h15m for 1,150,000 docs, ~60.9 docs/sec): real yield 1,634,541,568 tokens vs. its 1,050,000,000 target -- 156% (real avg ~1,442 tokens/doc, another overshoot in the same direction as finemath, not fineweb-edu-like this time). No repetition needed for this source either; every source measured so far (cosmopedia-v2, finemath, fineweb-edu, dclm-edu) now has a real pool at or above its proportional target except cosmopedia-v2 (~1.85 epochs, already judged fine above).**
  - **github-code (queued last, started 2026-09-14 14:17:51): a real, dramatically different profile from every other source -- confirmed 10-30x slower doc-admission rate, root cause identified directly from the actual code, not guessed.** Live diagnostic run 2026-09-15 ~00:19 (real process/filesystem inspection, not a log line -- `prepare_data.py` itself prints no periodic progress): `iter_github_code`'s own docstring states the real mechanism -- it streams the *entire* underlying `codeparrot/github-code` dataset (~115M files/873GB) and filters client-side against the 161-repo allowlist, since the dataset's own `languages=`/`licenses=` kwargs don't filter under streaming (already known, MF-110); most fetched rows get discarded. **Confirmed network/stream-bound, not CPU-bound**: the real live process (PID 40512) had consumed only 15,952.7 CPU-seconds against 36,070 seconds of real wall-clock since its start -- 44% utilization, meaning more than half the elapsed time is spent idle/waiting on I/O, the real signature of stream-bound work, not local filtering cost. Parquet export was confirmed **not** a contributing factor -- its output directory was completely empty at check time (`ParquetDocumentWriter` only flushes at `finalize()`, no interim writes). Real shard-based progress estimate (87 real train shards written, one shard = 2,097,152 tokens packed in a measured 359 real seconds -> ~5,842 tokens/sec): since github-code's own real tokens/doc average isn't known until it completes, bracketed against the other four real sources' observed range (738-1,491 tok/doc) gives **~9-18% of the real 1,380,000-doc target reached after ~10 real hours**, implying **~2-5 more real days** to finish if the rate holds steady (highly uncertain either direction -- the underlying stream's own repo clustering could make the real rate better or worse further in, not measurable in advance). **Decision (2026-09-15, user-confirmed): let it keep running as-is**, not killed/restarted with a smaller `--limit` or capped by a time budget -- the ~10 hours of real progress already invested stays, and [[MF-065]]'s own already-established repetition-tolerance precedent (~3.1 epochs, no overfitting) means an eventual real shortfall here (if the final pool undershoots its 600M-token, 20%-weighted target) would still likely be fine, matching the same reasoning already applied to cosmopedia-v2 above -- to be confirmed for real once this source's own `metadata.json` actually exists.
  - **github-code stopped and being redone (2026-09-15): `codeparrot/github-code` confirmed for real (not assumed) to be a static, stale one-time snapshot -- a wrong source for this project's real intent, not just a slow one.** User question, prompted directly by the slowness diagnosis above: "that's not the behaviour i thought we want... to pull outdated source from dataset, we should pull it from github with fetch or api... to get latest source directly." Verified via a real fetch of the dataset's own HF card (`https://huggingface.co/datasets/codeparrot/github-code`): its v1.1 BigQuery query ran **March 16, 2022** -- a one-time snapshot with no stated refresh mechanism, ~4.5 years stale relative to this project's real 2026-09-15 date. This is the same real root cause as the slowness finding above, not a separate problem: streaming the *entire* frozen 115M-file dataset to find 161 specific repos is both slow (scans almost entirely irrelevant, rejected rows) and wrong (even a successful scan would return multi-year-old code, not each repo's real current state). **Decision: kill the running process (PID 40512, confirmed stopped) and delete its partial output (`data/shards/mf070-150m-3b-github-code`, `data/parquet/mf070-150m-3b-github-code` -- both confirmed gitignored/untracked before removal), then build a real direct-fetch replacement** (`git clone --depth 1` per allowlisted repo, no GitHub API/credentials needed for the clone itself, avoiding both the staleness and the "scan an entire unrelated 873GB dataset" slowness at once) rather than resume the stale-dataset approach. **A real, separate gap this redesign surfaces, not yet resolved**: the existing per-file license gate relies on `codeparrot/github-code`'s own dataset-provided `license` field (normalized via `_GITHUB_CODE_PERMISSIVE_LICENSES`), with only 13 of 161 allowlisted repos having a project-side manual override (`_MANUALLY_VERIFIED_REPO_LICENSES`, for cases where that field was wrong/missing) -- a direct clone has no equivalent per-repo license source built in yet; the real fix (per-repo GitHub API `license.spdx_id` lookup, falling back to the existing manual-override table, rejecting -- not guessing -- anything unresolved) is part of the real redo, not deferred. Real implementation and re-run in progress; the other four sources' real completions (cosmopedia-v2, finemath, fineweb-edu, dclm-edu) are unaffected and stay skipped on re-run via their own already-done `metadata.json` markers.
  - **Real direct-fetch implementation done (2026-09-15), `src/minifrontier/data.py`: `iter_github_code_from_repos` + supporting helpers, tests-with-implementation per `AGENTS.md`.** User correction along the way, twice, both real and both applied: (1) the REST API-based license lookup in the first draft would have hit GitHub's unauthenticated 60/req-hour cap (148 non-manually-verified repos, ~2.5h just for pacing) -- replaced with reading the real cloned repo's own `LICENSE`-family file directly (zero extra network calls, no rate limit at all; cloning itself is a different subsystem from `api.github.com` and has no comparable cap for normal sequential traffic). (2) user pointed out `configs/code-repo-allowlist.txt` was already curated with a real per-repo license check (MF-121's own compilation pass) -- re-verifying via strict text-pattern matching and *rejecting* on any non-canonical wording would re-litigate that curation and silently shrink the corpus for repos already known-good. **Resolved design**: `_MANUALLY_VERIFIED_REPO_LICENSES` first (13 known repos, unchanged), then a best-effort real read of the cloned LICENSE file classified via `_detect_license_from_text` (canonical-text-signature matching for all 7 `PERMISSIVE_CODE_LICENSES` values); a clear signal of **degradation to a copyleft license** (GPL/AGPL/LGPL/MPL signatures) is loudly logged (`WARNING: ...`) and excludes that repo -- a real possible relicense since curation, worth a manual re-check; genuinely undetectable text (non-canonical wording, the same false-negative pattern the 13 manual entries already document) is trusted as permissive per the allowlist's own prior curation and quietly admitted -- **a real correctness point caught and fixed mid-implementation**: an earlier draft defaulted an undetectable case to a guessed specific SPDX label ("Apache-2.0"), which would have fabricated wrong provenance for some files; reverted before landing -- an undetectable-but-clean repo is admitted by trusting the allowlist, never given an invented specific license string.
    `_clone_github_repo`: `git clone --depth 1` (shallow, no GitHub API, no credentials) into a `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory()`, deleted right after that repo's files are extracted -- correct and simple for a one-time run; a persistent per-repo cache for cheaper *future* re-runs is real, deliberately deferred work, see [[MF-134]]. Real per-file admission mirrors `iter_github_code`'s own conventions: `_strip_leading_license_comment` reused as-is, plus a new 1MB file-size cap and a 1000-char max-line-length cap (matching `codeparrot/github-code`'s own disclosed filtering convention, kept for parity). Wired into `scripts/prepare_data.py`: `--source github-code` dispatches to `iter_github_code_from_repos` whenever `--github-repo-allowlist` is given (the real, only-used-in-practice case), keeping the old `iter_github_code` path only for the (currently unused) no-allowlist case.

Full fast suite: **526 passed**, `ruff check`/`format --check` clean.

Real precedent, not just theory: [[MF-065]]'s 1B-token run *did* run this exact schedule shape through a full decay to completion and landed at the good numbers in the reference table above -- the closest real anchor available for what full annealing can produce here, even though token budget and data mixture differ from this run.
  - **Real disaster-recovery procedure, verified directly against the actual running code and live checkpoint state (2026-09-18), for the real, honest question "what if the PC restarts accidentally mid-run (Windows Update, power outage)?"** Three things checked, not assumed:
    1. **Checkpoint writes are atomic** ([[MF-079]]/[[MF-130]], re-confirmed by reading `checkpoint.py`'s own current docstring directly): every file stages in a hidden temp directory first; only an atomic rename publishes it at the real path. An interruption mid-write leaves either the complete previous checkpoint or the complete new one -- never a corrupted, half-written one to accidentally resume from.
    2. **Real checkpoints exist on exactly the configured schedule** -- confirmed by listing the live directory at update 118,200: `checkpoint-00117000`, `-00117500`, `-00118000` (latest, ~1.9GB each), matching `--checkpoint-interval 500`/`--keep-last-n-checkpoints 3` exactly. **Worst-case lost work on an unplanned interruption is bounded to ~500 updates (~17-18 minutes at this run's real ~1,948 tok/s), not the whole run.**
    3. **The launch script itself auto-resumes -- no manual command construction needed.** Read `run_mf070_150m_release.ps1`'s real resume logic directly: it checks for `final/model.safetensors` first (skip entirely if the run already completed), then lists `checkpoint-*` directories, sorts descending, and automatically appends `--resume <latest>` to the training args if any exist. **The real recovery action after any unplanned restart is simply re-running the exact same command** (`.\scripts\run_mf070_150m_release.ps1`) -- it re-detects the 5 already-done data-prep steps (skips them instantly) and resumes training from the latest checkpoint automatically, with the same optimizer state, WSD schedule position, and data-mixture cursor (no re-processing of already-consumed batches).
    - **Real, disclosed caveat**: [[MF-146]]'s SIGTERM/Ctrl+C emergency-checkpoint safety net (implemented this same session) is **not active on this specific running process** -- it's a source-code-only change, and since the live process was deliberately never touched (editing files doesn't affect an already-running Python process), it's still executing the pre-MF-146 code. A "graceful" Windows-initiated shutdown here would not trigger an early emergency save; recovery still falls back to the plain 500-update interval bound above either way -- already a good, real bound, not a gap needing urgent action. A genuine power outage was always going to hit that same bound regardless, with or without MF-146, since nothing can catch an unpowered machine.

Dataset repo URL not yet recorded here; add it if/when convenient.
    **Real Hub confirmation (2026-09-16)**: the dataset repo (`https://huggingface.co/datasets/igalk474/MiniFrontier-150M-Modern-3B-token-mixture`) is live and being used -- 58 real downloads recorded, 6.72GB total, closely matching the expected sum across all 5 sources' real Parquet pairs (~0.8GB github-code + ~2.9GB dclm-edu + ~1.6GB fineweb-edu + ~1.1GB finemath + ~0.16GB cosmopedia-v2), confirming the upload landed complete, not partial. The Hub's own automated `parquet-converter` bot posted its standard comment (`refs/convert/parquet` branch) -- confirmed harmless and expected, not a re-encode: per the bot's own message, "when the dataset is already in Parquet format, the data are not converted and the files in refs/convert/parquet are links to the original files." No zstd re-compression, no duplicate storage, no action needed.

## M11 — Ecosystem adapters (post-V1; not part of the neural core)

These tasks turn the frozen MiniFrontier artifacts into externally loadable formats. Uploading the
existing release directory to a model hub is useful file hosting, but does not by itself satisfy any
compatibility task below.

### MF-071 — Export a Transformers-compatible Hugging Face repository

- **Priority / state:** P2 / In progress
- **Depends on:** MF-067
- **Deliverable:** separate Hub-ready Edu and Modern repositories with a MiniFrontier `PretrainedConfig`, base model, causal-LM wrapper, tokenizer integration, safe weights, generation config, model card, and explicit `architectures`/`auto_map` metadata; the raw-PyTorch core remains framework-independent.
- **Acceptance:** in a clean pinned environment, `AutoConfig`, `AutoModel`, `AutoModelForCausalLM`, and `AutoTokenizer` load the local export and a pinned Hub revision with the documented remote-code trust setting; native-versus-Transformers logits and greedy tokens match for Edu and Modern fixtures, including GQA, QK-Norm, hybrid local/global attention, and the global-NoPE variant; tied weights, special/FIM/chat tokens, chat template/default system prompt, context limits, dtype, hashes, license, and revision are preserved. A repository upload without these tests is labeled hosted, not Transformers-compatible.
- **Status note:** the standalone configuration/model, safe export, tokenizer/chat metadata, complete hashes, local Auto-class loading, cached decode, and native FP32 logit/argmax parity are implemented and tested for tiny Edu/Modern/global-NoPE fixtures. Canonical 150M exports and pinned-Hub clean-environment tests remain gated on MF-067.

### MF-072 — Validate MiniFrontier with vLLM on Windows-hosted NVIDIA CUDA

- **Priority / state:** P2 / In progress
- **Depends on:** MF-050, MF-071
- **Deliverable:** a pinned vLLM integration that first targets the Transformers modeling backend and uses an out-of-tree model plugin only if the backend cannot express the frozen architecture, plus a Windows 11 + WSL2 run guide.
- **Acceptance:** the adapter follows vLLM's custom-model contract (`auto_map.AutoModel`, forwarded model/attention kwargs, `ALL_ATTENTION_FUNCTIONS`, and `_supports_attention_backend`); `vllm serve <hub-id> --model-impl transformers --trust-remote-code` or the documented plugin equivalent loads both canonical presets under WSL2 CUDA; prompt logprobs and greedy continuations match the native reference within dtype-specific tolerances across prefill/decode, GQA, hybrid layers, QK-Norm, and global NoPE; an OpenAI-compatible API smoke, BF16/cache behavior, version/revision, VRAM, TTFT, and decode throughput are recorded. Pinned plain-chat smokes cover Vercel AI SDK (`generateText`, `streamText`, messages, and an application-managed prompt loop), OpenCode, Cline, Roo Code, Kilo Code, and Aider with the real context limit and text-only/tool-disabled capability metadata. Transport success is reported separately from code-edit quality; V1 tool/function calling and native Win32 vLLM support are not claimed.
- **Status note:** the Transformers-backend structural contract, interleaved sliding/full layer metadata, WSL2 launch guide, native parity-fixture generator, fail-closed evidence schema, and completions/chat API harness are implemented. Actual WSL2 CUDA load/parity/performance and coding-client transports remain unmeasured.

### MF-073 — Add high-precision GGUF and llama.cpp architecture support

- **Priority / state:** P2 / In progress
- **Depends on:** MF-071
- **Deliverable:** a pinned upstream patch or maintained adapter implementing MiniFrontier conversion metadata, tensor mapping, tokenizer/chat/FIM metadata, model loading, and the llama.cpp compute graph for Edu and Modern.
- **Acceptance:** BF16 or F16 safetensors convert to GGUF without pretending the model is `llama` unless exact graph equivalence has been proven; the loader and graph preserve RoPE, GQA, QK-Norm, per-layer local/global attention, global NoPE, tied embeddings, special-token IDs, and context/window metadata; `llama-cli` and `llama-server` pass native-reference logit/greedy parity fixtures and a Windows 11 NVIDIA-CUDA smoke before quantization is attempted; upstream/adaptor revision and artifact hashes are pinned.
- **Status note:** pinned-checkout verification and fail-closed F16/BF16 conversion/report orchestration are implemented against the reviewed current upstream layout (`conversion/<model>.py`, `src/models/<model>.cpp`). They refuse conversion unless an actual MiniFrontier converter, GGUF constants/tensor mapping, architecture registration/declaration, and C++ graph exist. The reviewed baseline revision is recorded in `adapters/llama_cpp/upstream.json`; that upstream implementation and native Windows parity evidence remain open, and no Llama compatibility is claimed.
- **Sequencing confirmed by the user (2026-09-09): the real upstream llama.cpp graph work resumes after [[MF-070]], not before.** This task's own conversion/report scaffolding (fail-closed checks, pinned-checkout verification) is already built and stays as-is; the actual C++ graph implementation and native-parity evidence are the part explicitly deferred until [[MF-070]]'s 350M run is done, matching the same priority ordering already established for [[MF-098]]'s distillation work. Not a new decision — restated here because the user explicitly confirmed it alongside rejecting the architecture-downgrade suggestion below, so both are recorded together rather than only the rejection.
- **External feedback (2026-09-09) proposed downgrading the architecture for easier GGUF conversion — not adopted; contradicts this task's own already-recorded principle.** The suggestions: replace the 3-local/1-global hybrid schedule with a Gemma-2-style plain alternating pattern (or drop it for pure causal attention) because "arbitrary repeating 3:1 schedules may require modifying llama.cpp source code"; skip [[MF-081]]'s value residual entirely since "ggml has no layer-to-layer value-residual bypass in standard LLaMA/Qwen runners"; drop global NoPE in favor of split RoPE theta because matching llama.cpp's NoPE flag handling is "complex"; and use QK-Norm instead of [[MF-082]]'s gated attention since gating is "unsupported in standard LLaMA GGUF models." **This task's own acceptance criterion already directly contradicts the first two changes** — it requires the upstream graph to "preserve RoPE, GQA, QK-Norm, per-layer local/global attention, global NoPE" exactly, and `docs/GGUF_LLAMA_CPP.md`'s own opening line states the actual governing principle: "MiniFrontier must never be labeled `llama` merely to make conversion run." Watering down the real architecture to fit vanilla llama.cpp's existing Llama-shaped graph is precisely what this task exists to avoid; the harder, real path (upstreaming an actual `minifrontier` architecture) is the one already chosen and in progress. **Real, useful part of this feedback, recorded as a genuine scope gap this task's acceptance criterion needs to grow to cover, not an argument to shrink it:** [[MF-081]]'s `value_residual`/[[MF-082]]'s `gated_attention` did not exist when this task's acceptance criterion was written, so neither is currently named in the list of semantics the upstream graph must preserve. If either is ever adopted into a real Modern preset after its own bounded test, this task's acceptance criterion must be updated to add it to that list — dropped for GGUF convenience only if a real decision says so, not by default. The split-RoPE-theta-instead-of-NoPE suggestion is moot either way: this project already has both mechanisms (MF-081 adopted split theta directly; NoPE remains a separate, already-tested experiment, see `reports/mf070-global-nope-quality.md`) and neither replaces the other.
- **Real, potentially task-redefining finding (2026-09-17), from a real read of llama.cpp's own current source (not the reviewed baseline snapshot alone) -- this task's own docstring premise may be outdated.** `src/minifrontier/gguf.py`'s own docstring states llama.cpp "needs to know the architecture it is loading, and MiniFrontier's hybrid local/global schedule is not one it implements," hence the fail-closed refusal. Checked directly against the real, current llama.cpp source: **a general, metadata-driven mechanism for exactly this pattern already exists**, used by several real shipped architectures (`granite-swa.cpp`, `gemma4.cpp`, `cohere2.cpp`, `dots3note.cpp`, `exaone4.cpp`). `granite-swa.cpp`'s `load_arch_hparams` reads a per-layer sliding-window boolean array (`LLM_KV_ATTENTION_SLIDING_WINDOW_PATTERN` -> `hparams.is_swa_impl`) plus a window-size key (`LLM_KV_ATTENTION_SLIDING_WINDOW`) straight from GGUF metadata, and builds its compute graph via a shared, already-implemented `build_attn_inp_kv_iswa()` input builder -- not bespoke per-model graph code for the local/global split itself. **This suggests MiniFrontier's own 3-local:1-global schedule (a 20-entry `is_swa` array matching the real per-layer pattern, `n_swa=512`) could plausibly reuse this existing, general mechanism rather than requiring the wholly novel C++ graph contribution this task's own acceptance criterion currently assumes** -- GQA/RoPE/SwiGLU are already common building blocks across these same real files. **Not yet verified in enough depth to change this task's own acceptance criterion or status** -- a real, concrete look at whether `is_swa_impl`'s actual real semantics match MiniFrontier's schedule exactly (not just "some sliding-window support exists") is needed before revising the "wholly novel graph" assumption; recorded as a real lead worth checking before the deferred graph-implementation phase (still gated on [[MF-070]] per the sequencing note above) actually starts, potentially narrowing its real scope.
- **Full correctness audit completed (2026-09-17), at the user's own direct request, closing the "not yet verified in enough depth" gap above -- five separate checks against the real, current llama.cpp source, all confirmed correct or better-supported than assumed. Genuinely good news: nothing in `gguf.py`'s own real assumptions needs correcting.**
  1. **File paths -- CORRECT.** All seven paths `verify_llama_cpp_support()` checks for exist exactly as expected in the real, current repo: `gguf-py/gguf/constants.py`, `gguf-py/gguf/tensor_mapping.py`, `src/llama-arch.cpp`, `src/llama-model.cpp`, `src/models/models.h`. `src/models/<arch>.cpp` (confirmed via real examples: `cohere2.cpp`, `granite-swa.cpp`, `gemma3.cpp`) is the real, current per-architecture compute-graph convention -- `src/models/minifrontier.cpp` is the right expected shape, not stale.
  2. **Architecture registration -- CORRECT in spirit, one real nuance worth keeping.** The real mechanism is a `conversion/<file>.py` module with one or more classes decorated `@ModelBase.register("<HFArchClassName>")` (confirmed 229 such decorators across real `conversion/*.py` files) -- **not** strictly one file per architecture. Real precedent: `conversion/granite.py` registers `GraniteForCausalLM`/`GraniteSWAForCausalLM`/`GraniteMoeForCausalLM` all in one file; `conversion/command_r.py` holds both `CohereForCausalLM` and `Cohere2ForCausalLM`. So a single `conversion/minifrontier.py` registering **both Edu and Modern as two separate classes** is exactly the real, idiomatic shape -- `gguf.py`'s single-file assumption is correct, and doesn't need splitting by preset when the real converter is eventually written.
  3. **Sliding-window semantics -- CONFIRMED accurate, and more nuanced than the original finding stated.** Two real, coexisting mechanisms exist in `llama-hparams.cpp`: (a) `set_swa_pattern(n_pattern, dense_first)` -- a periodic formula (`is_swa_impl[il] = il % n_pattern != 0`) most SWA architectures (Gemma2/3, Cohere2, etc.) use from a single integer period; (b) `granite-swa.cpp`'s own `LLM_KV_ATTENTION_SLIDING_WINDOW_PATTERN` -- a genuine arbitrary explicit per-layer array read straight from one GGUF metadata key. **MiniFrontier's fixed 3-local:1-global schedule is expressible either way**: `set_swa_pattern(4)` reproduces it exactly as a periodic case, or the explicit-array path (Granite's own real precedent) works too. The original "task-redefining" finding holds up under real scrutiny, not overstated.
  4. **RoPE convention parity -- a real, correct match exists, but must be explicitly selected, not assumed automatic.** MiniFrontier's own `rope.py` uses `rotate_half` (pairs feature `i` with `i + head_dim/2`) -- confirmed this is exactly ggml's `GGML_ROPE_TYPE_NEOX` mode (`ggml.h`'s own comment: NEOX `n_dims=8 -> [ccccssss]`, matching rotate-half precisely), distinct from `GGML_ROPE_TYPE_NORMAL` (interleaved, `[cscscscs]`). A real, already-supported, correct mode -- the eventual `minifrontier.cpp` graph must set `rope_type = GGML_ROPE_TYPE_NEOX` explicitly. This is exactly the class of silent convention-mismatch bug this audit was meant to catch -- confirmed avoidable with the right explicit choice, not a hidden gap.
  5. **Metadata key coverage -- CORRECT, better than assumed.** Real, confirmed GGUF keys: `{arch}.attention.head_count_kv` (GQA), and **`{arch}.attention.key_length`/`value_length` -- an explicit head_dim key, independent of `n_embd/n_head`** -- directly supports MiniFrontier's own `head_dim_override` ([[MF-108]]) with zero mapping gap, better than assumed going in. `{arch}.rope.freq_base` **plus a separate `{arch}.rope.freq_base_swa`** is an exact, already-existing real precedent for MiniFrontier's own local/global RoPE-theta split ([[MF-081]]). No metadata mapping gap found beyond the already-known missing compute graph itself.
  - **Net verdict**: every real building block this eventual implementation would need -- file layout, registration pattern, arbitrary per-layer SWA support, the correct RoPE mode, and explicit head_dim/GQA/split-theta metadata keys -- is real, current, and already sufficient. The only real remaining gap is exactly what this task's own acceptance criterion already names: the actual `src/models/minifrontier.cpp` graph and `conversion/minifrontier.py` converter don't exist yet. Nothing here changes this task's sequencing (still gated on [[MF-070]]) -- it substantially de-risks the eventual implementation once that gate clears, confirming the real work is narrower and better-supported than originally assumed.

### MF-074 — Quantize, evaluate, and publish the 4-bit GGUF artifacts

- **Priority / state:** P2 / In progress
- **Depends on:** MF-038, MF-066, MF-073
- **Deliverable:** reproducible high-precision-GGUF-to-4-bit conversion, initially `Q4_K_M`, with separate Edu and Modern GGUF model repositories and usage cards.
- **Acceptance:** quantization starts from BF16/F16 GGUF rather than requantizing; the decision to use an importance matrix/calibration corpus is explicit, and any calibration data has a pinned revision, tokenizer/template, sampling recipe, license, hash manifest, and evaluation-contamination check; an uncalibrated comparison is retained when needed to isolate its effect. Windows 11 CUDA `llama-cli` and `llama-server` load and generate; size, peak RAM/VRAM, prompt/decode throughput, perplexity or validation loss delta, fixed-prompt greedy changes, LM/code regressions, tokenizer/template behavior, quantizer command/version, source checkpoint revision, and SHA-256 hashes are recorded. A file being 4-bit and loadable is not sufficient without the quality-regression report.
- **Status note:** a Q4_K_M-only candidate runner, GGUF validation, calibration-provenance gate, exact command/revision/hash report, and forced `publish_ready=false` state are implemented. Actual high-precision sources, quantized artifacts, Windows CUDA CLI/server smokes, and quality-regression reports remain open.
- **Weight-only INT8/INT4 quantization research (2026-09-12), cross-referenced from [[MF-115]]'s own investigation, belongs here instead.** Real, web-verified finding (not this task's own measurement): batch-1 decode is memory-bandwidth-bound, so quantizing weights alone (keeping activations higher-precision) directly cuts bytes moved per token -- general guidance cites up to ~2.7x for INT4 vs BF16 in the right conditions. Turing (this project's reference RTX 2070 Super) has real INT8 Tensor Core support, unlike its already-documented lack of native BF16, so this is plausibly a well-matched lever for this exact card. **Real caveat, matching this project's own measured-not-assumed precision discipline**: the bandwidth saving only materializes if the actual kernel used efficiently streams quantized weights -- naive int8 storage without a properly optimized dequantize-and-matmul path would not deliver the claimed speedup, and this task's own `Q4_K_M`-via-`llama.cpp` path already uses real, purpose-built quantization kernels rather than a hand-rolled one, so this note is mostly a confirmation that the existing plan (quantize via GGUF/llama.cpp, not a custom PyTorch quantization path) is the right one, not a call to add a separate quantization mechanism to this project's own training/eval-time code.

## Addendum — hardware-aware precision (discovered during MF-050/063 home-RTX evidence)

### MF-075 — Add real FP16 with GradScaler for non-native-BF16 CUDA hardware

- **Priority / state:** P1 / Done
- **Depends on:** MF-046
- **Deliverable:** a `"float16"` precision option alongside `auto|float32|bfloat16`, with automatic loss scaling for training, a hardware-aware `"auto"` policy that prefers genuine Tensor Core acceleration, and exact-resume-safe scaler state.
- **Acceptance:** `resolve_precision`'s `"auto"` mode prefers native BF16 (`torch.cuda.is_bf16_supported(including_emulation=False)`) over FP16 over FP32, rather than accepting PyTorch's default emulation-inclusive BF16 check; explicit `float16`/`bfloat16`/`float32` requests are still honored as asked; `float16` on CPU falls back to FP32 with a clear reason; `GradScaler` wraps the training backward/step path only when precision resolves to `float16` (a no-op for BF16/FP32); a skipped (inf/nan) optimizer step still advances the schedule/token counters, documented rather than silently surprising; the scaler's state round-trips through checkpoint save/resume exactly; every CLI that exposes `auto|float32|bfloat16` (`scripts/chat.py`, `scripts/eval.py`, `scripts/eval_sft.py`, `scripts/profile_model.py`, `scripts/sample.py`, `train/pretrain.py`, `train/sft.py`) adds `float16` consistently; CUDA FP16 full/cached logit parity uses a tolerance declared before measurement, mirroring the existing BF16 CUDA tests.
- **Status note:** discovered 2026-08-25 while reviewing `future-plan.md`: this project's RTX 2070 Super (Turing, CC 7.5) has no native BF16 Tensor Cores, so every prior `"auto"`/`"bfloat16"` GPU run (including all of `reports/mf050-*`, `reports/mf049-*`, `reports/mf063-*`) measured emulated, not accelerated, BF16. Implemented: `Precision` extended with `"float16"`; `resolve_precision`'s hardware-aware `"auto"` ordering; `GradScaler` wired into `train_updates` (unconditional calls that are transparent no-ops when disabled, verified against both plain AdamW and the Muon `CombinedOptimizer`); scaler state persisted via a new `TrainingState.grad_scaler_state` field, which round-trips through the *existing* generic checkpoint save/resume path with no changes needed to `checkpoint.py` or `pretrain.py`/`sft.py`; all 7 CLIs updated. Six new tests (2 CPU-mocked, 4 real-CUDA) all pass on this hardware. Re-measured on the real RTX 2070 Super (`reports/mf049-rtx2070s-checkpointing-benchmark.md`, `reports/mf050-rtx2070s-profile-matrix.md`, both updated 2026-08-25): **training throughput is ~2.7x faster under real FP16 than emulated BF16** (150M-edu batch_size=1: 7424 vs 2747 tok/s, using less VRAM too), and batch_size=4 — which never completed under BF16 eager (VRAM-thrashing) — completes cleanly at 10,968 tok/s under FP16 eager. Inference-only single-stream numbers are more mixed (FP32 still fastest for prefill/decode at this small scale) and are reported as measured, not rationalized further. FP16 is now the recommended training precision for this hardware going forward. This finding is now recorded in `AGENTS.md`'s Engineering rules as project-wide guidance.

### MF-076 — Stop training artifacts from accumulating unbounded disk usage

- **Priority / state:** P2 / Done
- **Depends on:** MF-031, MF-032
- **Deliverable:** a `--no-checkpoint` benchmark mode for `train/pretrain.py`/`train/sft.py` that never writes checkpoint files, and a verified-then-delete step in `scripts/export.py` so a training checkpoint's optimizer/RNG state does not outlive the release it was exported into.
- **Acceptance:** `--no-checkpoint` writes only `run.json`, no `checkpoint-*`/`final` directories, and does not change default behavior when omitted; `scripts/export.py` calls the new `minifrontier.release.verify_release` (manifest integrity, no pickle state, a real load-test forward pass with finite logits) before deleting anything, and only deletes the source checkpoint after that verification passes; an interrupted or corrupted export must never cost the only remaining copy of the trained weights; `--keep-source` opts out of deletion for users who want to keep resuming training from that exact checkpoint.
- **Status note:** discovered 2026-08-26 after a session of MF-046/049/050/063/075 benchmarking left `/artifacts` at ~22GB, almost entirely disposable intermediate checkpoints whose numbers were already captured in `reports/*.md`. Implemented and tested: `train/pretrain.py`/`train/sft.py --no-checkpoint` (new `tests/test_pretrain_cli.py`, the project's first functional — not just `--help` — test of a `train/*.py` entry point), `minifrontier.release.verify_release` (new unit test in `tests/test_release.py`), and `scripts/export.py`'s default-on auto-delete with `--keep-source` (verified end to end with a real CUDA checkpoint export, not just unit tests). `/artifacts` manually pruned from ~22GB to 816MB in the same session (kept the real MF-063 gate's `final/` checkpoint and its exported release).
- **Follow-up (2026-09-01): periodic-checkpoint retention.** The `--no-checkpoint`/export-delete pair above didn't cover the remaining gap: a long *running* training pass (the real [[MF-065]] 1B-token run) still accumulated every periodic `checkpoint-<N>` directory with no cap, reaching 23 checkpoints / ~38GB for one run. Added `minifrontier.checkpoint.prune_old_checkpoints(output_dir, *, keep_last_n)` — deletes all but the most recent `keep_last_n` periodic checkpoints by parsed update count, matches only the `checkpoint-<digits>` naming so `final/`/`run.json`/anything else is never touched, and is a safe no-op when nothing needs deleting. Wired into both `train/pretrain.py` and `train/sft.py` via a new `--keep-last-n-checkpoints N` flag (omitted by default, so existing behavior is unchanged unless a caller opts in — periodic pruning happens automatically after each checkpoint save when set). New test `tests/test_checkpoint.py::test_prune_old_checkpoints_keeps_only_most_recent_and_never_touches_final` (also covers the idempotent-no-op case and an unrelated same-prefix directory being left alone). Full fast suite passes (195 passed), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean. Used immediately for real: pruned the completed MF-065 1B-token run's 23 checkpoints down to the 2 most recent + `final/`, reclaiming **~33GB** (38GB → 4.7GB for that run's directory).

### MF-077 — Harden cache compatibility, compile reporting, and controlled labs

- **Priority / state:** P0 / Done
- **Depends on:** MF-043, MF-048
- **Deliverable:** reject model/cache configuration mismatches, report lazy compilation only after a real compiled execution, and keep architecture/QK-Norm teaching comparisons controlled.
- **Acceptance:** a same-shaped Modern cache with a different local window fails before mutation; a backend failure raised during the first compiled execution is recorded and falls back to eager unless strict failure is requested; successful compilation is not reported before the first execution; architecture comparison labs use matched four-layer tiny fixtures; the QK-Norm lab starts common parameters identically and compares gradients over common parameters only; focused tests and the full fast suite pass.
- **Status note:** implemented and verified 2026-08-26. `KVCache.validate` now also compares the full `ModelConfig` (not just batch size/device) before any cache mutation, catching a same-shaped cache built from a different `local_window` (new `tests/test_cache.py::test_cache_rejects_same_shaped_model_with_different_local_window`). `minifrontier.compilation.maybe_compile` no longer reports `compiled=True` at wrap time: it returns a `_LazyCompileFallback` wrapper whose `CompileReport` is only marked compiled after the wrapped module's first real execution succeeds, and a backend failure on that first call is recorded and falls back to eager (or re-raises under `fail_on_error=True`), covered by two new `tests/test_training.py` cases with an injected failing backend. `labs/03_qk_norm.py` was reseeding once before building both models sequentially, so the qk_norm on/off models' shared weights were never actually identical; it now reseeds before each construction, asserts common parameters are bit-identical up front, and reports gradient norm over common parameters only, excluding QK-Norm's own scale parameters. `labs/02_mha_vs_gqa.py` already used matched `n_layers=4` fixtures on both sides, so no change was needed there. Verified: `pytest tests/test_cache.py tests/test_training.py` (30 passed), `pytest -m "not slow"` full fast suite (192 passed), `ruff check` on all changed files (clean), and `labs/03_qk_norm.py` run directly.

### MF-078 — Compile FlexAttention directly, not just the outer model

- **Priority / state:** P1 / Done
- **Depends on:** MF-041, MF-048, MF-050
- **Deliverable:** a real attempt at fixing Modern's eager-FlexAttention training bottleneck (MF-050 found eager `attention_impl=auto` 6.4x slower than manual on the same hybrid preset) by compiling `flex_attention` directly, since wrapping the whole model in `torch.compile` does not fuse it — FlexAttention checks whether it is itself being compiled, and a plain call from inside a compiled model still hits a graph break, silently keeping the slow eager path regardless of the outer model's compile status.
- **Acceptance:** a new, narrower compile switch (`set_flex_attention_compilation`, independent of the model-level `--compile` flag so eager tests are unaffected by default) compiles `flex_attention` itself; a compile failure — at wrap time or at first call — degrades safely to the eager kernel and is never retried on subsequent calls; the compiled and eager paths agree within a documented tolerance; `train/pretrain.py --compile` and `scripts/profile_model.py --compile-prefill` both wire it in.
- **Status note:** implemented and measured for real 2026-08-26, at the user's request after they flagged MF-050's eager-FlexAttention finding directly. `src/minifrontier/attention.py` adds `set_flex_attention_compilation`/`flex_attention_compilation_enabled` plus a `_flex_attention_callable` wrapper used at the "flex" call site instead of raw `flex_attention`. Two new tests in `tests/test_modern.py`: `test_compiled_flex_attention_matches_eager_flex` (real compile attempt, `@pytest.mark.slow`) and `test_flex_attention_compilation_failure_falls_back_and_stays_disabled` (injects a broken already-compiled callable directly rather than breaking `torch.compile` globally, since FlexAttention's own eager path also depends on `torch.compile` internally for its mask_mod handling — breaking it globally would take down the fallback too and not exercise the intended failure mode). **A real bug was caught by the second test during development**: the initial implementation only wrapped the *call* to the compiled function in try/except, not the `torch.compile(...)` wrapping step itself, so a failure at wrap time propagated uncaught — fixed by moving the wrap inside the try block.
