Full fast suite still passes (193 passed) after the fix. **Real-hardware result, on both CPU and the RTX 2070 Super (CUDA)**: compiling `flex_attention` directly does **not** work on this PyTorch 2.13.0+cu130 build — both backends fail identically with `InductorError: LoweringException: SubgraphLoweringException: Buffers cannot be created while lowering a pointwise subgraph`, a real Inductor limitation lowering the `mask_mod` subgraph, not a bug in this project's code. The fallback correctly catches it and produces identical output to eager (loss `7.716275` matches the uncompiled baseline exactly), but throughput is *worse* than uncompiled (2307-2351 vs. 4224 tok/s baseline for 150m-modern, batch=2, FP16) due to the wasted compile attempt. **Conclusion: Modern's ~4,224 tok/s (150m-modern, batch=2, FP16, eager, real data) remains the honest real training-throughput number on this hardware** — this was tested and ruled out, not skipped; the infrastructure is real and safely no-ops today, but will automatically start working if a future PyTorch/Inductor release fixes this lowering limitation, since the fallback only triggers on an actual failure. Recorded in `reports/mf050-rtx2070s-profile-matrix.md` as an addendum.
- **Reconfirmed (2026-09-03), ruling out a missing-triton explanation.** While investigating whether `torch.compile` could speed up Muon's optimizer step ([[MF-057]] follow-up), this environment turned out to have no `triton` package installed at all — raising the question of whether that, not a genuine Inductor bug, explained this task's FlexAttention-compile failure. After installing `triton-windows`, a direct retest (`train/pretrain.py --config configs/150m-modern.toml --compile`, same real data) reproduced the **exact same failure and throughput** (`SubgraphLoweringException`, ~2,397 tok/s, unchanged from the original ~2,307-2,351 tok/s range) — confirming this was always a genuine Inductor lowering bug, not a triton-availability artifact. This task's conclusion stands unrevised. (Separately, that same triton install did let `torch.compile` succeed on `torch.optim.Muon.step()` — a different code path with no custom subgraph — though it turned out to silently corrupt training quality there; see [[MF-057]]'s follow-up note.)

### MF-079 — Make checkpoint saves atomic

- **Priority / state:** P0 / Done
- **Depends on:** MF-031
- **Deliverable:** `save_training_checkpoint` must never leave a partially-written, unresumable checkpoint on disk if the process is killed mid-save.
- **Acceptance:** an interrupted save leaves either nothing at the target path (fresh checkpoint) or the previous complete checkpoint untouched (overwrite) — never a directory with some files present and others missing; a stale staging directory from a prior interrupted attempt does not block a later successful save.
- **Status note:** discovered 2026-08-28/29 the hard way, mid-session, during the real 1B-token 150m-modern training run ([[MF-070]]'s pre-work): the background training process was killed by something outside this session (real interruption, cause unknown — not a graceful stop) after ~21 real hours and 147,000/488,280 updates. The most recent checkpoint (`checkpoint-00147000`) turned out to contain only `model.safetensors` — `config.json`/`trainer_state.json`/`training_state.pt` were never written, because `save_training_checkpoint` wrote files sequentially straight into the target directory with no atomicity, unlike this project's other publication paths (shards, code manifests) which already use a stage-then-`os.replace` pattern. Lost ~21,000 updates (~43M tokens, ~3 hours of real compute) falling back to the last good checkpoint (`checkpoint-00126000`). Fixed for real: `save_training_checkpoint` (`src/minifrontier/checkpoint.py`) now writes every file into a hidden `.{name}.tmp` staging directory and only calls `staging.replace(target)` once everything is safely on disk; a stale staging directory from a previous interrupted attempt is cleared at the start of the next save. New regression test `tests/test_checkpoint.py::test_interrupted_checkpoint_save_never_corrupts_target` simulates a kill mid-write (a monkeypatched `torch.save` that raises) for both the fresh-checkpoint and overwrite-existing-checkpoint cases, and confirms a subsequent successful save still works despite the leftover staging directory. Full fast suite passes (194 passed, up from 193), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean. The corrupted `checkpoint-00147000` was deleted; training resumed from `checkpoint-00126000` under the now-atomic save path.

### MF-080 — Fix training-loop numerical-safety and MTP gradient gaps found by external code review

- **Priority / state:** P0 / Done
- **Depends on:** MF-041, MF-075
- **Deliverable:** close five real, verified gaps in the training loop's gradient handling and two smaller correctness/consistency gaps, found by the user's own line-by-line read of `src/minifrontier/` (not accepted at face value — each claim was independently re-verified against the actual source before any fix, per this project's own evidence discipline).
- **Acceptance:** a non-finite gradient under BF16/FP32 (no `GradScaler` to catch it) is detected and the step skipped, not silently applied; MTP head parameters (which live outside `MiniFrontier`, see [[MF-070]]'s MTP pre-work) are included in gradient clipping, not just the model's own; RoPE's rotation multiply runs in FP32 (or higher, never downcasting an already-higher-precision caller) regardless of the model's running precision; the FlexAttention block-mask cache is bounded rather than growing forever; `MTPHeads`' init std is a parameter, not a hardcoded constant that silently stops matching `ModelConfig.resolved_init_std` once a config sets an explicit `init_std`.
- **Status note:** all five claims verified true by direct source inspection before fixing (`training.py`, `rope.py`, `attention.py`, `mtp.py`) — this project's established practice of not trusting external review at face value, same as the recurrent-depth/MTP-architecture verification passes earlier in [[MF-070]]'s pre-work. Fixed: (1) `TrainingState` gained `nonfinite_updates: int = 0`; `train_updates` now checks `torch.isfinite(gradient_norm)` and skips `scaler.step(optimizer)` (zeroing grads, counting the skip) whenever the scaler itself is disabled (BF16/FP32) and the norm isn't finite — FP16's own `GradScaler`-mediated skip is unchanged. (2) `trainable_parameters` (model + `mtp_heads`, when present) is now built once and passed to `clip_grad_norm_`, instead of `model.parameters()` alone. (3) `rope.apply_rotary` computes the rotation in FP32 (or the caller's dtype if already FP32/FP64 — never downcasting), casting only the final result back to the input dtype; `model.py`'s call site now requests FP32 cos/sin tables from `RoPE.forward` unconditionally so there is no double-rounding through an intermediate BF16 table. (4) `attention._BLOCK_MASK_CACHE` is now an `OrderedDict` capped at 128 entries with LRU eviction (`move_to_end` on hit, `popitem(last=False)` on overflow). (5) `MTPHeads.__init__` gained an `init_std: float | None = None` parameter (defaulting to the prior `d_model**-0.5` when omitted); `scripts/compare_mtp.py` now passes `config.resolved_init_std` explicitly. Seven new tests (`test_training.py` x2, `test_rope.py` x2, `test_mtp.py` x2, `test_modern.py` x1), each targeting the exact failure mode via deterministic monkeypatch injection (matching this project's established `torch.compile`-failure-injection testing pattern from [[MF-078]]) rather than trying to trigger real numerical instability. Full fast suite passes (215 passed, up from 208), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean.

### MF-081 — Bounded ablation: z-loss, LayerNorm scaling, value residuals, split local/global RoPE theta

- **Priority / state:** P3 / In progress — real bounded GPU ablations for LN-scaling (3 arms), GQA-ratio (2 arms), and global_rope_theta (1 arm) are Done; **LayerNorm scaling adopted as a real default on every Modern preset (2026-09-09)**; GQA ratio stays at 3:1, no change; `global_rope_theta` stays unset (mechanism adopted, no value chosen); value residual's ablation stays explicitly deferred per the acceptance criterion — see 2026-09-09 progress note and `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_DECISIONS.md`
- **Depends on:** MF-070 (Muon/local-window/NoPE pre-work methodology), MF-088 (seed-variance measurement, must exist before either remaining item's ablation is conclusive)
- **Deliverable:** narrowed after a 2026-09-06 second review round (see status note): only LayerNorm scaling ("The Curse of Depth in LLMs", arXiv:2502.05795, `1/√l` per-layer downweighting) and normalized value residuals (mixing `V^(1)` into every layer's `V^(l)` via a learned, zero-initialized gate) remain here as bounded-ablation items. Split local/global RoPE theta is adopted directly, without a bounded ablation (see below); z-loss moved to [[MF-084]] (near-free to add inside its chunked loss loop); per-head gating stays in [[MF-082]].
- **Acceptance:** LayerNorm scaling and value residuals each land behind their own `ModelConfig` flag, off by default. LayerNorm scaling's bounded test must explicitly compare (a) baseline, (b) LN-scaling alone, (c) LN-scaling alongside the *existing* `residual_std = config.resolved_init_std / sqrt(2*n_layers)` init damping in `model.py` — both mechanisms damp residual-stream growth with depth from different ends (one at init, one every forward pass) and must not be silently combined without checking whether they compound or fight each other. Value residuals are implemented but their bounded test stays **explicitly deferred** (not run underpowered and recorded as "no measurable difference") until [[MF-088]]'s seed-variance number says a real effect at this size (individual IMU-1 deltas were −0.36% to −0.63%) is actually measurable on this hardware at an affordable token budget. Split local/global RoPE theta needs only a correctness/regression test (each layer type rotates by its own theta correctly) — no matched-token ablation, per the reasoning below.
- **Scope addition (2026-09-08), cheap and config-only:** a more aggressive GQA ratio (fewer KV heads relative to query heads) is worth a slot in this same bounded-ablation pass — Muse Glimmer (cited earlier this session) reportedly uses a more aggressive ratio than this project's current `n_heads=12, n_kv_heads=4` (3:1) on the 150m/350m-modern presets. No new code is needed (GQA is already fully implemented and tested, MF-039) — just an `n_kv_heads` config sweep (e.g. 2 or 1) measured the same way as every other item here. Not independently verified that a more aggressive ratio helps at this project's scale; worth one real bounded data point alongside the other items in this task's slot.
- **Status note:** proposed by external code review, citing IMU-1 (arXiv:2602.02522) ablations on their own 70M proxy model/data/hardware (`d_model=768`, 12 heads, 4 KV heads — architecturally close to `configs/150m-modern.toml`, but not this project's own measured evidence). Their reported deltas (QK-Norm −0.63%, value residual −0.38%, LN scaling −0.36%, per-head gating −0.05%, all combined −1.64%, exceeding the individual sum) are a real, citable prior, not yet independently confirmed here. **Revised 2026-09-06** after the reviewer's own follow-up correctly pointed out that "each intervention needs a bounded matched-token test" doesn't scale to effects this small: the ~0.04% "noise floor" cited from the corrected local-window test is the delta between two *different configs*, not a same-config/different-seed measurement — this project has never actually run that experiment, so the true noise floor is unknown and likely larger. Applying the reviewer's own MTP rule ("zero wall-clock cost, well-attested, can't plausibly hurt → adopt without ablation, because the ablation costs more than the risk") splits the four original items: z-loss and split RoPE theta qualify (no added parameters, negligible FLOPs) and move to direct adoption; LayerNorm scaling and value residuals do not (real architectural interaction risk / real added compute) and stay gated on [[MF-088]]. Not started.
- **Progress (2026-09-08): all four items' CODE is done, real and tested; the real GPU bounded ablations (LN scaling's 3 arms, the GQA-ratio sweep) have not been run yet.**
  - **Split local/global RoPE theta — adopted directly, fully done.** `ModelConfig.global_rope_theta: float | None = None` (only legal outside `attention_pattern="hybrid"` when `None`); `resolved_global_rope_theta` property. `MiniFrontier` now builds `local_rope`/`global_rope` (the same module object, zero extra cost, whenever `global_rope_theta` is unset — the overwhelming default case) and routes each layer to its own table by `is_local`. Mirrored into the HF export path (`configuration_minifrontier.py`, `modeling_minifrontier.py`) since `hf_export.py`'s `transformers_config` unpacks *every* `ModelConfig` field onto `MiniFrontierConfig` unconditionally — leaving the adapter unaware of a real theta split would have silently produced wrong-but-uncaught exported logits the moment any real trained checkpoint used it. 7 new tests (`test_config.py`: defaults/divergence/validation x4; `test_modern.py`: a forward-hook-based test proving each layer type actually receives its own table, plus the zero-cost shared-object case; `test_huggingface_adapter.py`: a real parity case with a 100x-divergent theta). No frozen preset sets a non-default value — the mechanism exists and is tested; no specific theta value is proposed or adopted into any preset, since none is specified anywhere in this task or its citation.
  - **LayerNorm scaling — implemented behind `layer_norm_scaling: bool = False`, off by default, correctness-tested; the 3-arm bounded ablation has NOT been run.** Applies a fixed (non-learned) `1/sqrt(layer_index + 1)` multiply to both the attention-input and FFN-input norm outputs in `TransformerBlock.forward`. Discovered while implementing: the task's own acceptance criterion (compare (a) baseline, (b) LN-scaling alone, (c) LN-scaling + the existing init-time damping) is not actually buildable as originally scoped, because `model.py`'s `residual_std = resolved_init_std / sqrt(2*n_layers)` init damping was **unconditional** — there was no way to construct arm (b) at all. Added `residual_std_damping: bool = True` (default `True`, preserving every existing checkpoint/preset/test exactly) so arm (b) can switch it off. Mirrored into the HF adapter the same way as the theta split (`layer_norm_scaling` needs real forward-pass parity since it affects already-trained-weight inference; `residual_std_damping` is init-only and needs no adapter-side computation, just field storage for the mirror test). 4 new correctness tests plus 1 real HF parity case.
  - **Value residual — implemented behind `value_residual: bool = False`, off by default, correctness- and cache-tested; the bounded ablation stays explicitly deferred per this task's own acceptance criterion.** Every layer but the first gets one learned, zero-initialized scalar gate (`attention.py`'s `value_residual_gate`) mixing layer 1's value vectors into its own, mixed in *before* KV-cache append so cached and uncached forward passes are automatically identical with no special-casing (verified directly, not assumed — see below). `MiniFrontier.forward` computes layer 0's value vectors once per call (redundant with block 0's own internal computation, at zero cost when the flag is off) and threads it to every later block. 5 new tests: no gates when disabled; layer 0 never gets a gate; **zero-init is verified to be a true no-op** (identical logits to the flag-disabled model, same seed); a nonzero gate is verified to actually change output; and — per `AGENTS.md`'s standing "cached and uncached logits must agree" rule — a real KV-cache prefill+incremental-decode run with nonzero gates matches a full uncached forward within the same `atol=2e-5` tolerance already used elsewhere in this codebase. Mirrored into the HF adapter with a real parity test using a *nonzero* gate (a zero gate would pass parity trivially even with a wrong mixing formula, since 0 times anything is 0 — the test deliberately perturbs the gate before comparing).
  - **Whether to actually run value residual's bounded ablation is still an open call, not resolved by this progress update.** [[MF-088]]'s own note says the target effect size (IMU-1's −0.38%) is "6-14x noise floor — plausibly measurable, though still close enough that this task's own explicit-deferral criterion remains the right caution." That is genuinely ambiguous, not a clear go/no-go — left for an explicit decision before spending the GPU time, rather than resolved unilaterally here.
  - **Real GPU commands, ready to run in the user's own terminal** (matching this session's own established reliability pattern: real multi-hour GPU training via this session's own background tool has been silently killed twice this session; the user's own foreground terminal has been reliable every time). All five arms reuse `data/shards/mf064-150m-train` (real FineWeb-Edu data, 16k tokenizer, the current post-revert default), seed 42, batch=2, 5,000 updates (~10.24M tokens/arm), matching the scale of every other bounded comparison this session (Muon, local-window, NoPE):
    ```
    # LayerNorm scaling, 3 arms (a=baseline unmodified, b=alone, c=both)
    ./.venv/Scripts/python.exe train/pretrain.py --config configs/150m-modern.toml --train-shards data/shards/mf064-150m-train/train --output artifacts/mf081-ln-scaling/baseline --updates 5000 --batch-size 2 --seed 42 --device cuda
    ./.venv/Scripts/python.exe train/pretrain.py --config configs/scratch-mf081-ln-scaling-alone.toml --train-shards data/shards/mf064-150m-train/train --output artifacts/mf081-ln-scaling/alone --updates 5000 --batch-size 2 --seed 42 --device cuda
    ./.venv/Scripts/python.exe train/pretrain.py --config configs/scratch-mf081-ln-scaling-both.toml --train-shards data/shards/mf064-150m-train/train --output artifacts/mf081-ln-scaling/both --updates 5000 --batch-size 2 --seed 42 --device cuda

    # GQA-ratio sweep, 2 arms (baseline is the same unmodified 150m-modern.toml run above)
    ./.venv/Scripts/python.exe train/pretrain.py --config configs/scratch-mf081-gqa-2kv.toml --train-shards data/shards/mf064-150m-train/train --output artifacts/mf081-gqa-sweep/2kv --updates 5000 --batch-size 2 --seed 42 --device cuda
    ./.venv/Scripts/python.exe train/pretrain.py --config configs/scratch-mf081-gqa-1kv.toml --train-shards data/shards/mf064-150m-train/train --output artifacts/mf081-gqa-sweep/1kv --updates 5000 --batch-size 2 --seed 42 --device cuda

    # MF-082: gated attention (baseline is the same unmodified 150m-modern.toml run above)
    ./.venv/Scripts/python.exe train/pretrain.py --config configs/scratch-mf082-gated-attention.toml --train-shards data/shards/mf064-150m-train/train --output artifacts/mf082-gated-attention/gated --updates 5000 --batch-size 2 --seed 42 --device cuda

    # MF-081: global_rope_theta=500,000 (Llama 3's real value), local layers unchanged
    ./.venv/Scripts/python.exe train/pretrain.py --config configs/scratch-mf081-global-rope-theta-500k.toml --train-shards data/shards/mf064-150m-train/train --output artifacts/mf081-global-rope-theta/500k --updates 5000 --batch-size 2 --seed 42 --device cuda
    ```
    After each arm completes, load its checkpoint and run `evaluate_token_batches`/`batches_from_packed_shards` (the standing path from [[MF-086]]) against `data/shards/mf064-150m-train/validation` for real held-out CE/PPL/BPB, the same recipe used for every other bounded comparison this session. Record results in a new `reports/mf081-ln-scaling.md` and `reports/mf081-gqa-sweep.md`.
    The gated-attention arm additionally needs [[MF-082]]'s own real needle-haystack re-evaluation (baseline vs. gated checkpoint) once both finish training — **driver decided and built (2026-09-08):** `scripts/eval_needle_haystack.py` gained a `--checkpoint`/`--tokenizer` alternative to `--release` (mutually exclusive), loading a raw `train/pretrain.py` checkpoint directory directly (`ModelConfig` from its `config.json`, weights via `load_training_checkpoint(..., trusted_local_state=True)` since this is always your own local training output, never a downloaded artifact) rather than requiring a full `export_release` for a throwaway bounded-comparison arm. Verified for real (not just `--help`): a real tiny checkpoint saved via `save_training_checkpoint`, loaded back through the new path, and run through both `run_needle_haystack_eval` directly and the full CLI as a subprocess — both produced a real trial result. Once both checkpoints exist:
    ```
    ./.venv/Scripts/python.exe scripts/eval_needle_haystack.py --checkpoint artifacts/mf081-ln-scaling/baseline/final --context-lengths 512 1024 2032 --output reports/mf082-needle-haystack-baseline.json
    ./.venv/Scripts/python.exe scripts/eval_needle_haystack.py --checkpoint artifacts/mf082-gated-attention/gated/final --context-lengths 512 1024 2032 --output reports/mf082-needle-haystack-gated.json
    ```
    (Reuses the same baseline checkpoint as [[MF-081]]'s LN-scaling comparison — one training run serves both.)
  - Full fast suite: 304 passed (up from 286 before this task), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean across `src/`, `tests/`, `scripts/`, `train/`.
  - **Scope decision (2026-09-08, user-directed): every architecture-level item this task adds is Modern-only.** Edu must stay exactly the classic architecture (`AGENTS.md`'s own Frozen V1 list), understandable to a beginner regardless of what Modern's bounded ablations end up adopting. `ModelConfig.__post_init__` now rejects `layer_norm_scaling=True`/`value_residual=True` on `preset="edu"` (2 new tests); GQA-ratio changes and a non-default `global_rope_theta` were already structurally impossible on Edu via its existing MHA-only/full-attention-only guards, so no new code was needed for those two. Mirrored into the HF adapter's own Edu guard. Recorded in `AGENTS.md`'s Frozen V1 section, alongside a note that [[MF-083]]'s two training-recipe items split further: WSD is an operational safeguard (resumability against an interrupted multi-day run), not a quality technique, so it applies to **both** Edu's and Modern's real release runs (2026-09-08, user-confirmed); cautious weight decay is a real quality technique gated on its own bounded test, so it stays Modern-only like the architecture items above. Neither is config-validated (`TrainingConfig` has no preset awareness), so this split is a followed convention, not a code guard. Full fast suite: 306 passed.
  - **A concrete candidate value for `global_rope_theta`, from external feedback (2026-09-09), worth testing rather than leaving the mechanism permanently unset.** Llama 3's real, public config uses `rope_theta=500,000` (not this project's `10,000` default) specifically to support longer-context extrapolation without further fine-tuning; some later models go to `1,000,000`. Since [[MF-081]]'s split-theta mechanism already exists and is adopted (no ablation needed for the mechanism itself, per the earlier decision above), the *value* is still an open, untested question for this project's own hybrid schedule — worth one real bounded arm (`global_rope_theta=500_000` on `configs/150m-modern.toml`, local layers unchanged at `10_000`) alongside the rest of this task's queued arms, checked the same way (real held-out CE/PPL/BPB, and ideally cross-referenced against [[MF-086]]'s needle-haystack eval to see whether it measurably helps retention at the boundary [[MF-082]] found degradation past). Not independently verified beyond the well-known Llama 3 config fact itself; no config currently sets this.
  - **A free export-time optimization for LayerNorm scaling, found via external feedback (2026-09-09), verified correct — recorded for whenever `layer_norm_scaling` is actually adopted, not implemented now since the feature itself is still unadopted.** `layer_norm_scaling`'s runtime multiply (`attention_input * (layer_index+1)**-0.5`, applied after `RMSNorm`) is a plain scalar multiplying every element of the norm's output — and `RMSNorm`'s own last step already multiplies by a learned per-channel `weight` vector. Scalar multiplication commutes with that: `(x/rms(x)) * weight * scale == (x/rms(x)) * (weight * scale)` exactly, not an approximation. So at **export time only** (after training, when `weight` is frozen), folding `effective_weight = weight * scale` into the RMSNorm's own weight tensor reproduces the exact trained behavior with **zero runtime multiply** — the exported graph (native inference, the HF adapter, or a future GGUF export under [[MF-073]]) just sees an ordinary `RMSNorm`, no new op needed at all. This must **not** be done during training itself: baking a fixed scalar into a *trainable* weight changes what gradient the optimizer sees for it (a real, if likely small, effect via AdamW's per-parameter normalization), so the runtime-multiply implementation already in `model.py` stays exactly as it is for training; this fold only ever applies to a checkpoint's frozen weights after training ends. No task number of its own — small enough to fold into whichever export utility exists when `layer_norm_scaling` is actually adopted into a real preset.
  - **Real GPU results (2026-09-09): all queued arms trained and evaluated — LN-scaling (3 arms), GQA-ratio (2 arms), global_rope_theta (1 arm), plus [[MF-082]]'s gated-attention arm.** Training ran in the user's own foreground terminal per this task's own established mitigation; evaluation used a new `scripts/eval_checkpoint.py` (see the tooling-gap note below) against the shared `data/shards/mf064-150m-train/validation` set (435,798 predicted tokens / 1,868,949 UTF-8 bytes), same seed/recipe as training.
    - **LN-scaling** (`reports/mf081-ln-scaling.md`): LN scaling *replacing* the existing `residual_std_damping` is a real regression (+2.84% CE, +15.7% PPL) — the two mechanisms are not interchangeable. LN scaling *added alongside* the existing damping (matching the frozen default) gives a small, plausibly-real improvement (-0.35% CE/BPB, -1.81% PPL), 2-8x the estimated noise floor (~0.046-0.2%, [[MF-088]]) but single-seed. `layer_norm_scaling` stays off pending an adoption decision.
    - **GQA-ratio sweep** (`reports/mf081-gqa-sweep.md`): 6:1 (2kv) is indistinguishable from the frozen 3:1 default (-0.015% CE, inside the noise floor). 12:1/MQA (1kv) shows a small, plausibly-real regression (+0.24% CE, +1.26% PPL). No evidence supports a more aggressive ratio than 3:1 at this scale; `n_kv_heads=4` unchanged.
    - **global_rope_theta=500,000** (`reports/mf081-global-rope-theta.md`): small in-distribution improvement (-0.21% CE), right at the noise floor's edge. A follow-up needle-haystack re-eval (`reports/mf081-needle-haystack-global-rope-theta.json`) came back inconclusive for the same reason as [[MF-082]]'s: 0.0 retrieval at every length including inside the local window, the same undertrained-budget signature as the baseline and gated-attention arms, not evidence the larger theta fails to help extrapolation. `global_rope_theta` stays unset.
    - **[[MF-082]]'s gated-attention arm** (`reports/mf082-gated-attention-quality.md`): the clearest real signal of any arm this pass — -0.67% CE / -3.37% PPL, well above the noise floor. The companion needle-haystack re-eval (this task's queued command below) came back **inconclusive, not negative**: both baseline and gated arms scored 0.0 retrieval at every context length including inside the local window, because a 5,000-update (~10.24M-token) bounded checkpoint is ~100x short of [[MF-086]]'s reference (the real 1B-token MF-065 release, which does retrieve within-window). See [[MF-082]]'s own status note for the follow-up this implies.
    - **A real, previously-unknown `checkpoint.py` bug was found and fixed while evaluating these checkpoints, not worked around**: `load_training_checkpoint` compared a checkpoint's raw `config.json` dict directly against a freshly-built model's `ModelConfig.to_dict()`, so any checkpoint saved before a later additive `ModelConfig` field existed (here, [[MF-107]]'s `rope_fraction`, added between the LN-scaling arms finishing and the GQA-sweep arms starting) could never be reloaded again — confirmed for real: the LN-scaling `alone`/`both` checkpoints failed to load with `"checkpoint model configuration does not match the target model"` until fixed. Fixed by round-tripping the saved dict through `ModelConfig(**saved_config)` before comparison, so both sides go through the same default-filling normalization; a genuinely incompatible config (verified by the existing `test_training_checkpoint_rejects_same_shape_semantic_config_change` test, still passing) is still rejected exactly as before. New regression test `test_training_checkpoint_loads_after_a_new_modelconfig_field_is_added`. Full fast suite: 337 passed (up from 306), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean.
    - **A standing tooling gap was also closed**: `scripts/eval.py` only accepted a full `export_release` directory, never a raw `train/pretrain.py` checkpoint — the exact gap this task's own progress note flagged ("load its checkpoint and run evaluate_token_batches/batches_from_packed_shards"). New `scripts/eval_checkpoint.py` (`evaluate_checkpoint(checkpoint, validation_shards, tokenizer_dir, ...)`) fills it: loads a raw checkpoint directly and reports CE/PPL/BPB via the same [[MF-086]] `batches_from_packed_shards`/`evaluate_token_batches` recipe every other bounded comparison this session reuses, without retraining or exporting anything. 2 new tests in `tests/test_eval_checkpoint.py` (real finite-metric round trip; loud failure on a corrupted/mismatched checkpoint). Reusable for [[MF-107]]/[[MF-108]]'s own queued arms.
  - **Adopted (2026-09-09), user-directed: `layer_norm_scaling=true` is now the default on every frozen Modern preset** (`configs/50m-modern.toml`/`150m-modern.toml`/`350m-modern.toml`/`500m-modern.toml`; `residual_std_damping` stays at its existing default `true` — LN scaling was adopted *alongside* it, never as a replacement, per the real regression the `alone` arm measured above). Full reasoning, exact deltas, and real recomputed parameter counts (`layer_norm_scaling` itself adds none) are in `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_DECISIONS.md`'s 2026-09-09 entry; also recorded in `AGENTS.md`'s Frozen V1 section. **GQA ratio was considered and explicitly left unchanged at each preset's own existing default** — the 6:1 arm's result was evidence-neutral (no quality upside found to justify switching) and the 12:1 arm's small regression argues against going further; kept pending either a stronger reason or a real test built from the 350M preset itself. **Correction (2026-09-13), a real inaccuracy caught by the user**: this note and `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_DECISIONS.md`'s mirror of it both previously said "3:1" as if that ratio were uniform across presets. It is not: `n_heads`/`n_kv_heads` is 12/4 (3:1) on 150M and 18/6 (3:1) on 500M, but 8/2 (4:1) on 50M and **16/4 (4:1) on 350M** — the exact preset MF-070's real scale check will actually use. The 2-arm sweep this decision rests on was built entirely from the 150M preset's own 3:1 baseline; 350M's pre-existing 4:1 was never the literal tested configuration (16 is not evenly divisible by 3, so an exact 3:1 is not achievable at 16 heads without also changing `n_heads`, a bigger change than this ablation's own scope). Directionally, 4:1 sits much closer to the tested-and-neutral 6:1 point than to the regressing 12:1/MQA one, so there is no real evidence against 350M's existing ratio — but it was never directly measured either. Not changed as a result of this correction alone; see [[MF-070]]'s own entry for whether a targeted arm built from the 350M preset itself is worth running before that config locks in for real. Full fast suite: 337 passed, `ruff check`/`format --check` clean; `tests/test_scale.py`'s hardcoded 350m-modern parameter count updated (332,460,544 -> 332,919,744, the real `gated_attention` delta — see [[MF-082]]'s own adoption note).
  **Dissenting caution recorded (2026-09-10), not acted on, from external review.** A reviewer flagged that this promotion was made from "~10M tokens, one seed, 150M architecture" and argued it should be re-validated before carrying into any future larger-scale (350M+) config rather than assumed to transfer automatically — correctly restating a caveat this project's own record already carried (the `both` arm's own note above already says "plausibly real, not confirmed by a second seed"). Not a new finding, but a legitimate, worth-keeping reminder: before [[MF-070]]'s real 350M run locks in its actual config, a second-seed confirmation (or the 350M run itself serving as one) is the honest bar for treating this as settled rather than provisional.

### MF-082 — Attention-sink handling for windowed local layers under the ring KV cache

- **Priority / state:** P2 / In progress — real degradation confirmed ([[MF-086]]), fix chosen and implemented+tested; the retrain + validation-quality comparison is Done (real, positive result) and **adopted as the Modern default (2026-09-09)**; the needle-haystack re-eval ran but came back inconclusive (undertrained budget) — a conclusive re-test needs a ~1B-token retrain, explicitly deferred until after [[MF-070]], see 2026-09-09 progress note
- **Depends on:** MF-050 (hybrid ring cache), MF-086 (long-context retrieval eval, to actually detect the failure this addresses)
- **Deliverable:** a real evaluation of whether this project's Local/Local/Local/Global hybrid attention degrades on long generations because the ring cache evicts position 0 (the classic StreamingLLM attention-sink failure mode, arXiv:2309.17453), and, if it does, a chosen fix — per-head gated attention (Qiu et al., arXiv:2505.06708, NeurIPS 2025, shipped in Qwen3-Next: a per-head sigmoid gate on the SDPA output before `out_proj`), gpt-oss-style learned per-head sink logits in the softmax denominator, or permanently pinning the first *k* ring-cache slots.
- **Acceptance:** the long-context retrieval eval from [[MF-086]] exists and actually runs against the current hybrid/ring-cache checkpoints first — there is currently no eval in this project that would distinguish a working windowed model from a broken one at long generation lengths. Only after that eval shows a real degradation does a fix get chosen and bounded-tested (matched tokens, real validation) before being frozen into the Modern preset. Modern-only by construction (per [[MF-081]]'s 2026-09-08 scope decision, and structurally: Edu has no local layers or ring cache to need a sink fix for in the first place).
- **Scope note (2026-09-08):** this task currently frames per-head gated attention only as a candidate *sink-specific fix*, gated on finding a real degradation first. A comparable model (Muse Glimmer, cited earlier this session's own architecture-verification pass) applies gating to *every* layer — local and global alike — as a general quality/efficiency technique, not specifically to fix sink eviction. [[MF-081]]'s own cited IMU-1 ablation numbers give this some independent support, though weak: per-head gating measured the *smallest* of that paper's four ablated effects (−0.05%, vs. QK-Norm's −0.63%/value-residual's −0.38%/LN-scaling's −0.36%). If [[MF-086]]'s eval finds no real sink degradation (this task's current gate), that does not by itself rule out testing gated attention as a general technique separately — worth revisiting as its own small bounded-ablation candidate for [[MF-081]]'s slot if [[MF-088]]'s noise floor says a ~0.05%-scale effect is even measurable (per [[MF-088]]'s own note, [[MF-081]]'s smallest target effects are already close to that floor).
- **Status note:** flagged by external code review as a real, previously-undiscussed gap tied to an already-known, already-accepted caveat (`reports/mf050-rtx2070s-profile-matrix.md`'s ring-cache eviction behavior) — correctly identifies that this project has never actually tested for the specific failure mode that caveat implies. Not started; genuinely blocked on [[MF-086]]'s eval existing first, since choosing a fix before being able to measure whether it works would repeat the same mistake as the original flawed 512-vs-1024 local-window evidence earlier in [[MF-070]]'s pre-work.
- **Progress (2026-09-08): degradation confirmed for real, fix chosen and fully implemented+tested; the actual bounded ablation is still pending real GPU time.** [[MF-086]]'s needle-haystack eval already ran against a real checkpoint and found real degradation (retrieval rate `{512: 1.0, 1024: 0.4, 2032: 0.0}` by context length beyond `local_window=512`) — this task's own acceptance gate is satisfied. **Chosen fix: per-head gated attention** (the first candidate listed, and the one this task's own 2026-09-08 scope note already argued for as a general technique, not just a sink-specific patch — matching Muse Glimmer's real design). The other two candidates (gpt-oss-style learned sink logits, pinning ring-cache slots) were not implemented; per-head gating was chosen as the more general, better-precedented mechanism and this task does not require trying all three.
  - **Implementation:** `ModelConfig.gated_attention: bool = False` (Modern-only, same Edu guard pattern as [[MF-081]]'s items). `attention.py`'s `CausalSelfAttention` gains `gate_proj: nn.Linear(d_model, n_heads, bias=True)`, applied as `attended = attended * sigmoid(gate_proj(inputs))` (broadcast over `head_dim`) right before the heads are merged and handed to `out_proj` — reading the same normed residual-stream input that produced Q/K/V, not the attention output itself, so a head's decision to suppress its own contribution does not depend on what it just attended to. Weight zero-initialized, bias set to 4.0 (`sigmoid(4.0)~=0.982`, near-open) — a **two-pass** init exactly like `out_proj`/`down_proj`'s existing `residual_std` damping, since the generic `self.apply(self._initialize)` pass would otherwise overwrite the zero weight with random noise; `MiniFrontier.__init__` re-zeroes `gate_proj.weight` for every block after that pass, matching the established precedent rather than inventing a new one. Not an exact no-op at init the way [[MF-081]]'s `value_residual_gate`'s zero-init is (a sigmoid gate structurally cannot reach exactly 1.0) — a small, deliberate, near-identity start, verified directly (gate output is exactly `sigmoid(4.0)`, input-independent, until the weight moves away from zero).
  - **Mirrored into the HF export adapter** (`configuration_minifrontier.py`, `modeling_minifrontier.py`) with a real parity test using a genuinely nonzero, head-varying `gate_proj.weight` — a zero-weight (default-init) gate would pass parity trivially (spatially uniform, so a wrong axis-alignment bug would be invisible against a constant multiplier), so the test deliberately perturbs it first, the same discipline already used for [[MF-081]]'s `value_residual` parity case.
  - **Tests:** 4 new in `test_model.py` (disabled by default has no `gate_proj`; init is exactly `sigmoid(4.0)` and input-independent across multiple random probes; a nonzero weight changes forward output; cached-vs-uncached logits match within `atol=2e-5`, per `AGENTS.md`'s standing cache-parity rule), 1 in `test_config.py` (Edu rejection), 1 real HF-adapter parity case. Full fast suite: 312 passed (up from 306), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean.
  - **Not yet done: the real bounded ablation this task's own acceptance criterion requires.** Gating needs real trained weights to matter — it cannot be bolted onto an already-trained checkpoint and meaningfully evaluated, since an untrained `gate_proj` only ever contributes its near-1.0 init value. This means confirming the fix actually helps requires: (1) a real bounded training run with `gated_attention=True` (matched tokens/seed against a `gated_attention=False` baseline, same discipline as every other bounded comparison this session), then (2) re-running [[MF-086]]'s `scripts/eval_needle_haystack.py` against both resulting checkpoints to see whether the retrieval-rate collapse at `context_length > local_window` is actually reduced. Config for the gated arm: a scratch copy of `configs/150m-modern.toml` with `gated_attention = true` added, everything else unchanged, same `data/shards/mf064-150m-train`/seed 42/batch=2 recipe as [[MF-081]]'s pending arms — queued for the same real-GPU-terminal session as those, not yet run.
  - **Real GPU result (2026-09-09), `reports/mf082-gated-attention-quality.md`: real positive quality signal, needle-haystack re-eval inconclusive, not negative.** Ran the queued arm for real (`configs/scratch-mf082-gated-attention.toml`, same `data/shards/mf064-150m-train`/seed 42/batch=2/5,000-updates recipe as [[MF-081]]'s arms) and evaluated both this and the shared baseline via the new `scripts/eval_checkpoint.py` (see [[MF-081]]'s 2026-09-09 progress note for the tooling gap this closed). **Validation quality**: gated attention beats baseline by -0.666% CE / -3.374% PPL / -0.666% BPB — the clearest, most-above-noise-floor result of any [[MF-081]]/[[MF-082]] arm this pass ([[MF-088]]'s estimated floor is ~0.046-0.2%). **Needle-haystack re-eval** (`reports/mf082-needle-haystack-baseline.json`, `reports/mf082-needle-haystack-gated.json`, same `--context-lengths 512 1024 2032` command already queued above): both arms scored **0.0 retrieval at every length, including 512 (inside the local window)** — this is not the "gating didn't fix the collapse" result it might look like at a glance. [[MF-086]]'s own reference degradation (`{512: 1.0, 1024: 0.4, 2032: 0.0}`) was measured against the real **1B-token** MF-065 release checkpoint; these two arms are bounded 5,000-update/~10.24M-token comparisons, ~100x short of that budget, and at this budget *neither* arm has learned in-context needle retrieval at all yet, even well inside the window where it should trivially work if the capability existed. The comparison as run cannot distinguish "gating doesn't fix sink eviction" from "retrieval hasn't emerged yet at this token budget" — a real, honest inconclusive result, not evidence against the fix. **This task's actual acceptance criterion (does gating reduce the retrieval-rate collapse) remains open**, now blocked specifically on a larger-budget `gated_attention=true` training run (closer to MF-065's 1B tokens) before the needle-haystack comparison can be re-run meaningfully — not on any remaining code or tooling gap.
  - **Adopted (2026-09-09), user-directed: `gated_attention=true` is now the default on every frozen Modern preset**, on the strength of the real validation-quality result alone — not because the sink-eviction fix is confirmed. The user's own framing: enable it for Modern now regardless of which exact gating formula eventually turns out best, since the quality case already stands on its own (matching Muse Glimmer's real precedent of gating every layer as a general technique, independent of any sink-specific motivation). **The conclusive sink-fix re-test (a real ~1B-token `gated_attention=true` retrain, ~3-4 days at this project's measured throughput, then re-running the needle-haystack comparison against MF-065-scale budgets) is explicitly deferred until after [[MF-070]]'s real 350M run** — that run is the priority claim on multi-day GPU time right now, not a second ~1B-token bounded-comparison retrain. **The sigmoid/Qwen3-Next formula currently implemented is not claimed to be the best possible mechanism.** DeepSeek-V4's real, more surgical alternative (a learned per-head scalar added inside the softmax denominator itself, letting attention mass sum to less than one — see the `MF-082 addendum` below) remains recorded and available, and is mechanistically closer to what a real StreamingLLM attention sink does than a post-hoc output gate is. Not switched to now: there is no real evidence the current formula underperforms specifically at the sink-eviction task (the one eval that would show that is exactly the inconclusive, deferred re-test above) — swapping mechanisms on a citation rather than a real bounded result would repeat the mistake this project's own evidence discipline exists to prevent (same reasoning already applied to Muon-vs-AdamW, local window, and tokenizer size). If the deferred re-test ends up showing the sigmoid formula does not fix the collapse, DeepSeek-V4's formula is the recorded next candidate to implement and bounded-test, not a default swap. Full reasoning, exact parameter-count deltas, and the four updated preset files are recorded in `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_DECISIONS.md`'s 2026-09-09 entry and `AGENTS.md`'s Frozen V1 section.

### MF-083 — WSD learning-rate schedule (adopted operationally) and cautious weight decay (bounded test)

- **Priority / state:** P1 / Done — WSD implemented and wired into the real CLI; cautious weight decay (real C-AdamW) implemented, tested, and the real bounded GPU comparison against plain AdamW ran for real (2026-09-11) -- **rejected, plain AdamW stays the default**, see final status note
- **Depends on:** MF-070 (AdamW-vs-Muon resolution)
- **Deliverable:** a `WarmupStableDecaySchedule` alongside the existing `WarmupCosineSchedule` (config-selectable, not a replacement), adopted for the real 3B-token release run **on operational grounds, not a quality ablation**; and a separate cautious-weight-decay AdamW variant (skip decay on a parameter when `sign(update) != sign(weight)`), which still gets a bounded matched-token test before becoming a new default. **Scope (2026-09-08, user-confirmed):** WSD applies to *both* Edu's and Modern's real release runs, since it is a resumability safeguard rather than a technique competing with Edu's classic-architecture identity; cautious weight decay, if it passes its bounded test, is Modern-only like [[MF-081]]'s architecture items.
- **Acceptance:** WSD's stable-phase length and decay-fraction/shape are explicit config fields, not hardcoded. WSD's justification is operational, not a comparison: a real 3B-token run is 488,280 updates (~8 days of continuous compute at this hardware's measured throughput), this project has already been killed mid-run once for real (the MF-079 incident), and cosine hard-couples its curve shape to `max_updates` — extending a finished cosine run means a different, incomparable schedule, while WSD lets a stable-phase checkpoint be branched and continued at any point. The quality comparison against cosine falls out for free from the real run itself (matching the published result that a 20% decay fraction matches cosine, not beats it) rather than needing its own dedicated GPU-time experiment. Cautious weight decay, by contrast, has no such operational argument and keeps its own real matched-token comparison (reusing `compare_optimizers.py`'s methodology) before adoption.
- **Status note:** proposed by external code review alongside a "promote Muon to default" recommendation, citing IMU-1's NorMuon-over-AdamW result (−2.88%, iteration/token-matched). **That recommendation is not adopted** — this project already ran the real, decisive test this exact question needed (`reports/mf070-muon-followup.md`): at *wall-clock-matched* budgets (this project's actual constraint, not iteration count), AdamW real val PPL 90.38 beats every tested Muon configuration including a best-case hybrid (FP32 Newton-Schulz + `ns_steps=3` + Muon's best LR), real PPL 95.86. IMU-1's comparison is not wall-clock-matched and does not override this project's own already-completed, more-relevant evidence; the reviewer's own follow-up withdrew this recommendation. **Revised 2026-09-06:** WSD's disposition changed from "needs a bounded ablation" to "adopt on operational grounds" after the reviewer pointed out that treating it as a pure quality ablation undersells its actual value — the real 3B-token run's own resumability is worth more than the small loss delta a dedicated comparison would measure, given this project's real, demonstrated interruption risk. Raised to P1 since it directly affects how the eventual 3B-token run should be structured. μP (learning-rate/init transfer across this project's four size presets) was also raised — genuinely useful for the 50M/150M/350M/500M ladder, but a bigger, separate investment; noted here as a considered, deferred idea, not scheduled.
- **WSD implemented (2026-09-08).** `WarmupStableDecaySchedule` added to `training.py` alongside the existing `WarmupCosineSchedule`: warmup identical to cosine's own ramp, then flat at the peak rate through the stable phase, then the same cosine-shaped fall `WarmupCosineSchedule` uses for its own tail, compressed into the last `wsd_decay_fraction` of `max_updates` (measured against the *whole* run, not the post-warmup remainder, so a large warmup cannot silently shrink the decay window). `TrainingConfig` gained `schedule: Literal["cosine", "wsd"] = "cosine"` and `wsd_decay_fraction: float = 0.2` (validated, citing Hägele et al., arXiv:2405.18392 for the 20% default — verified real via `WebSearch`, confirms a ~20% cooldown fraction achieves performance comparable to cosine in transformer LM pretraining). Both schedule classes are unified behind a new `LearningRateSchedule` `Protocol` (mirroring the existing `BatchProvider` pattern in the same file) and a `build_schedule(config, completed_updates=0)` factory — the one place a `TrainingConfig` turns into the schedule it actually selects; `train_updates`'s own internal default now calls `build_schedule` instead of hardcoding cosine, so `config.schedule="wsd"` is respected even by a caller that passes no explicit `schedule=` object.
  **Wired into the real CLI, not just the library** — the same class of gap [[MF-084]]'s follow-up found for `loss_chunk_size`/`z_loss_weight`/`mtp_extra_heads` (a `TrainingConfig` field with no path from `train/pretrain.py`'s actual argument parser, so a real run could never reach it). `train/pretrain.py` gained `--schedule {cosine,wsd}` and `--wsd-decay-fraction`, and its own schedule construction now calls `build_schedule(train_config)` instead of hardcoding `WarmupCosineSchedule`. `train/sft.py` deliberately left untouched — SFT runs are "minutes rather than days" (its own module docstring) with no multi-day interruption risk WSD exists to protect against, so adding this CLI surface there would be unused complexity, not a missing feature.
  **Tests:** 7 new in `test_training.py` (schedule shape at the warmup/stable/decay boundaries, large-warmup-does-not-shrink-decay-window, state_dict round-trip, invalid-`completed_updates`/`schedule`/`wsd_decay_fraction` rejection, `build_schedule` selects the right class, and a real `train_updates` run confirming `config.schedule="wsd"` is honored even with no explicit `schedule=` argument) plus 1 new real CLI test in `test_pretrain_cli.py` (two otherwise-identical tiny real training runs, one per schedule, same seed/data/everything else, confirming the resulting losses genuinely differ -- proof the two runs actually followed different learning-rate paths end-to-end through the real entry point, not just that the flag parses). Full fast suite: 325 passed (up from 318), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean.
  **Not yet decided: which decay fraction (or schedule at all) the real MF-070 350M/release run actually uses** -- the mechanism exists and is tested; `wsd_decay_fraction=0.2` is a literature-sourced starting point, not yet validated against this project's own hardware/data, and adopting WSD for the real run is still a decision to make when that run is actually being configured, per this task's own acceptance criterion.
  **Cautious weight decay not started** -- still needs its own real matched-token bounded comparison (reusing `compare_optimizers.py`'s methodology) before consideration, per this task's own acceptance criterion; Modern-only if adopted.
  **Citation check before implementation (2026-09-10): the mechanism as literally written above does not match any known published technique.** Verified directly against the real "Cautious Optimizers: Improving Training with One Line of Code" paper (Liang, Chen, Liu, Liu, arXiv:2411.16085, ICLR 2026; code at `github.com/kyleliang919/C-Optim`) -- the real C-AdamW masks the *entire* momentum-based update by sign-agreement between the update and the **gradient** (`u_t ⊙ g_t > 0`), not just the decay term compared against the **weight**, as this task's own text above says. The real algorithm (their Algorithm 2, fetched and confirmed): `φ_t = 𝕀(u_t ⊙ g_t > 0)`; effective learning rate `ε̄_t = ε_t · dim/(‖φ_t‖₀ + ξ)` (ξ=1 default) rescales to preserve Adam's Hamiltonian/convergence guarantee (proven in the paper, not just empirical); masked update `w_t ← w_{t-1} − ε̄_t · φ_t ⊙ u_t`; weight decay applied **afterward, unmasked, at the same scaled rate**: `w_t ← w_t − ε̄_t · γ · w_t`. Real reported result: up to 1.47x speedup on Llama pretraining. **Asked the user directly given the real mismatch; decided (2026-09-10): implement the real published C-AdamW mechanism above, not the narrower weight-decay-only variant this task's text originally described** -- it is the citable, evidence-backed technique, and the narrower variant has no known citation or published result behind it. This supersedes the "skip decay on a parameter when sign(update) != sign(weight)" line above; the task's own title ("cautious weight decay") stays as the informal name matching the paper's own C-AdamW naming, not a description of the exact mechanism.
  **Implemented and tested (2026-09-10).** New `src/minifrontier/cautious_adamw.py`: `CautiousAdamW`, a from-scratch readable `torch.optim.Optimizer` subclass (manual per-parameter loop, matching `muon.py`'s Newton-Schulz-reference precedent for correctness-first over fused/foreach) implementing the paper's Algorithm 2 exactly -- per-element mask `𝕀(update ⊙ gradient > 0)`, effective learning rate rescaled by `numel/(aligned_count + xi)`, weight decay applied afterward at that same scaled rate, unmasked. `TrainingConfig` gained `optimizer: Literal["adamw","cautious_adamw"] = "adamw"` and `cautious_xi: float = 1.0` (validated). `training.py` gained `build_cautious_adamw` (reuses the existing `_parameter_groups` decay/no-decay split, identical to `build_adamw`'s) and `build_optimizer` (dispatches on `config.optimizer`, mirroring `build_schedule`'s own dispatch pattern); `train_updates`'s internal default optimizer construction now calls `build_optimizer` instead of hardcoding `build_adamw`, so `config.optimizer="cautious_adamw"` is honored even by a caller that passes no explicit `optimizer=`. Wired into the real CLI from the start, not left as a library-only gap like several earlier fields this session (`train/pretrain.py` gained `--optimizer {adamw,cautious_adamw}` and `--cautious-xi`, and its own optimizer construction now calls `build_optimizer`). 15 new tests: `tests/test_cautious_adamw.py` (5 -- hyperparameter validation, a from-scratch independent re-derivation of Algorithm 2 cross-checked step-for-step including weight decay, a constructed case proving an element whose accumulated momentum disagrees with a freshly sign-flipped gradient is genuinely masked out that step, no-grad-means-no-op), `tests/test_training.py` (4 -- config validation, `build_optimizer` dispatch, `build_cautious_adamw`'s grouping matches `build_adamw`'s, a real `train_updates` run), `tests/test_pretrain_cli.py` (1 -- two real CLI training runs, identical seed/data/lr, `--optimizer adamw` vs `--optimizer cautious_adamw`, confirming the resulting loss genuinely differs -- proof the flag reaches real training, not just that it parses). Full fast suite: **400 passed** (up from 390), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean.
  **Real bounded GPU comparison against plain AdamW: queued for the user's own terminal, not run in this session** -- matching this session's own established reliability pattern (multi-hour real GPU work in this session's own background tool has been killed unexplained multiple times this session; the user's own foreground terminal has been reliable every time). Two arms, same recipe as every other bounded comparison this session (real `data/shards/mf064-150m-train`, seed 42, `150m-modern.toml`, batch=2, matched updates):
    ```
    ./.venv/Scripts/python.exe train/pretrain.py --config configs/150m-modern.toml --train-shards data/shards/mf064-150m-train/train --output artifacts/mf083-cautious-adamw/baseline --updates 5000 --batch-size 2 --seed 42 --device cuda --keep-last-n-checkpoints 2
    ./.venv/Scripts/python.exe train/pretrain.py --config configs/150m-modern.toml --train-shards data/shards/mf064-150m-train/train --output artifacts/mf083-cautious-adamw/cautious --updates 5000 --batch-size 2 --seed 42 --device cuda --optimizer cautious_adamw --keep-last-n-checkpoints 2
    ```
    Then evaluate both via `scripts/eval_checkpoint.py` against `data/shards/mf064-150m-train/validation` (the same recipe [[MF-081]]'s arms used) for real held-out CE/PPL/BPB, and record in a new `reports/mf083-cautious-adamw.md`. Per this task's own acceptance criterion and `AGENTS.md`'s Frozen V1 split, cautious AdamW becomes Modern's new default **only if** this real comparison shows a real, noise-floor-exceeding improvement ([[MF-088]]'s measured noise floor is the reference, ~0.046% relative CE/BPB); otherwise plain AdamW stays the default and this stays an available, off-by-default, tested experiment.
  **Real result (2026-09-11), `reports/mf083-cautious-adamw.md`, full detail there.** Both arms ran for real overnight (150m-modern, RTX 2070 Super, FP16, seed 42, 5000 updates/10.23M tokens each). **Cautious AdamW is a real, decisive loss on both quality and speed**: cross-entropy 5.185800 vs baseline's 5.100674 (+1.669% relative, 36.3x [[MF-088]]'s noise floor -- not noise), perplexity 178.72 vs 164.13 (+8.89%), and ~7.8% slower throughput (3,541.0 vs 3,842.3 tok/s) at essentially identical VRAM. No plausible upside on either axis. **Decision: Cautious AdamW is not adopted.** Plain AdamW remains the default for [[MF-070]] and every future run; the implementation stays in the codebase, tested and available, matching this project's own precedent for negative results (Muon/[[MF-057]]) staying implemented rather than deleted. This task is now fully Done -- both its real sub-parts (WSD adoption, cautious-AdamW bounded test) have real, recorded conclusions.
  **Real, important follow-up (2026-09-14), external review challenge -> independently verified -> partially retracted, a genuine two-round exchange worth recording precisely.** A reviewer claimed this project's `CautiousAdamW` diverges from "the widely used/reference implementation" on two counts: (1) mask computed from `update * gradient` instead of `exp_avg * gradient`; (2) weight decay scaled by the same cautious-mask-derived `effective_lr` instead of applied separately at the plain `lr`. Verified directly rather than trusted, in both directions:
  - **Claim (1), checked mathematically: no real difference exists.** `update = moment_hat / (variance_hat.sqrt() + eps)`, and both `bias_correction1` and `variance_hat.sqrt() + eps` are strictly positive by construction -- so `update` and `exp_avg` always share the same sign, element-wise, always. `(update * gradient > 0)` and `(exp_avg * gradient > 0)` are provably identical masks. Not a bug.
  - **Claim (2), checked against two primary sources that turned out to genuinely disagree with each other.** Fetched `kyleliang919/C-Optim`'s real `c_adamw.py` directly: it applies weight decay at the plain, unscaled `lr`, decoupled and applied *before* the masked update -- confirming the reviewer's description of the reference *code*. But the paper's own Algorithm 2 (fetched independently twice, both explicitly checking the exact step-size symbol used) uses the *same* renormalized `ε̄_t` for **both** the masked-update line and the weight-decay line -- i.e. this project's implementation is faithful to the paper as published; it is the paper's *own reference code* that diverges from its own algorithm box on this specific point, not this project's implementation diverging from a single, unambiguous source. The reviewer's own follow-up, after seeing this, explicitly retracted "your implementation is wrong" and reclassified the earlier MF-083 result as **"C-AdamW-paper"** -- a real, valid test of the published algorithm, not an invalid or buggy one.
  - **One genuinely new, real, scientifically relevant detail surfaced in the same exchange**: the paper's own 60M/100M/350M LLM experiments used `weight_decay=0`, and only its 1B experiment used `weight_decay=0.1` -- at `wd=0`, the paper and reference-code formulations are mathematically identical (the disputed term vanishes), so the discrepancy could not have been observable in the paper's own published <=350M results. This project's own MF-083 test used `weight_decay=0.1` at 150M specifically, which is exactly the condition under which the two formulations *do* differ -- making a real, additional "C-AdamW-reference" arm (decoupled weight decay at plain `lr`, everything else identical) a genuinely informative follow-up, not a pedantic one. **Not yet run** -- would need a `cautious_decay_mode: Literal["paper", "reference"] = "paper"` switch added to `CautiousAdamW`/`TrainingConfig` and one additional matched arm (same 150M config, seed, ~10.23M tokens) against the same real baseline. Recorded as a real, scoped, not-yet-executed follow-up; this task's own already-Done status and adopted decision (plain AdamW) stand unchanged regardless of that follow-up's eventual result, since AdamW already beat `C-AdamW-paper` decisively on both quality and speed -- the open question is only whether `C-AdamW-reference` would have fared differently, a real question worth answering someday but not gating anything currently in flight.

### MF-084 — Chunked cross-entropy (plus near-free z-loss) to reduce logits memory pressure

- **Priority / state:** P0 / Done
- **Depends on:** MF-041
- **Deliverable:** compute `lm_head` projection and cross-entropy loss over sequence-length chunks and sum, instead of materializing the full `[B, S, vocab_size]` logits (and their FP32-upcast gradient) in one piece — a real, measurable memory reduction at this project's vocabulary size on an 8GB card. Since the chunk loop already computes each chunk's log-sum-exp explicitly, fold in z-loss (PaLM-style `λ·log²(Σexp z)` logit-magnitude penalty) as a near-free addition — no second pass over the logits — rather than treating it as a separate ablation item.
- **Acceptance:** chunked and unchunked loss agree exactly (or within documented FP32 tolerance) on the same batch; peak VRAM at a fixed batch size/context measurably drops (or, equivalently, a larger batch size/context becomes reachable at the same VRAM ceiling), measured for real on this hardware, not estimated; z-loss is off by default (`TrainingConfig` weight, defaulting to 0.0), and its own contribution to the loss stays out of the logged/reported primary loss, matching the MTP auxiliary-loss precedent in `training.py`.
- **Status note:** proposed by external code review, correctly identifying that `next_token_loss_stats`'s full `[B, S, V]` logits plus their FP32-autocast-upcast gradient is a disproportionate memory cost for a small model. The review's other suggestion, "Cut Cross-Entropy" (arXiv:2411.09009), is **not** adopted as stated — it requires custom Triton kernels, which `AGENTS.md`'s frozen V1 scope explicitly excludes ("Do not add... custom CUDA/Triton kernels... in V1"). **Reprioritized to P0, ahead of [[MF-087]], 2026-09-06**: doubling the tokenizer's vocabulary directly doubles this exact memory cost, on hardware already shown to hit VRAM-thrashing at modest headroom margins (`batch_size=4` caused 9.25GB thrashing at the *current* 16k vocab — MF-065's own status note).
  - **Implementation, per a second review round that sharpened the design**: a naive per-chunk `torch.utils.checkpoint` approach was rejected — it recomputes the `lm_head` projection during backward, costing a verified ~5.6% of forward+backward compute (~11 hours over a real 8-day 3B-token run) for no reason. Instead, `src/minifrontier/loss.py` adds `_ChunkedCrossEntropy`, a custom `torch.autograd.Function` whose `forward` computes each chunk's loss *and* its closed-form gradient (`dL/dlogits = softmax(logits) - one_hot(target)`) together, discarding that chunk's `[C, vocab_size]` logits immediately and accumulating only `hidden`/`weight`-shaped gradients; `backward` then does no recomputation at all, just returns what `forward` already built — so the rest of the model's backward pass still runs exactly once, not once per chunk (a plain per-chunk `.backward()` loop without a custom Function would re-walk the entire transformer's graph on every chunk, which was seriously considered and rejected during implementation for being far more expensive than either alternative). `chunked_next_token_loss_stats(hidden_states, weight, tokens, ...)` wraps it with the same shift/mask/offset contract as `next_token_loss_stats`, plus `z_loss_weight`. `MiniFrontier.forward` gained `skip_logits: bool` to skip the `lm_head` call entirely when the caller is about to score `hidden_states` itself in chunks. `TrainingConfig` gained `loss_chunk_size: int | None = None` (opt-in; `None` preserves the exact original full-materialization path, matching the manual-attention-kept-alongside-SDPA/Flex precedent of never deleting a simpler reference path) and `z_loss_weight: float = 0.0` (requires `loss_chunk_size` to be set).
  - **Correctness verification**: `torch.autograd.gradcheck` (finite-difference vs. analytical gradient — the definitive check for a hand-derived backward, not just agreement with another implementation that could share the same mistake) passes for both the main loss and z-loss terms; chunked and unchunked loss_sum/count match within 1e-9 across offset∈{1,2}, uneven/exact/single chunk sizes, `loss_mask`, and scattered `ignore_index` targets; a full `train_updates` run from identical starting weights produces bit-for-bit identical final weights whether `loss_chunk_size` is set or not; a tied-embeddings model's shared `token_embedding.weight`/`lm_head.weight` correctly accumulates the chunked path's gradient. 12 new tests across `test_loss.py`/`test_model.py`/`test_training.py`; full fast suite 225 passed (up from 215), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean.
  - **Real measured result** (`labs/09_chunked_vs_fused_cross_entropy.py`, RTX 2070 Super, `batch_tokens=2048, d_model=768, vocab_size=32768, chunk_size=512`): peak allocated memory dropped from 1,189.4MB to 646.2MB, a real **1.8x reduction** (smaller than the ~4x element-count arithmetic suggests, due to allocator overhead/fragmentation and the reference path's own extra autograd-graph buffers — reported as measured, not reconciled to the estimate). This is the same lab the reviewer suggested as a teaching artifact showing the memory arithmetic (naive vs. chunked, no Triton needed); it doubles as this task's own real-hardware evidence.
  - z-loss folded in per the reviewer's own follow-up ("zero wall-clock cost, well-attested, can't plausibly hurt → adopt without ablation" — see [[MF-081]]'s revised scope).
  - **Follow-up (2026-09-07): real gap found — none of this was reachable from real training.** A second code-review round correctly found that `train/pretrain.py` and `train/sft.py` built `TrainingConfig` from CLI args without `loss_chunk_size`/`z_loss_weight` among them (confirmed: grepping the whole repo outside `src/`/`tests/` found zero references to either field before this fix), so the real 3B-token run at 32k vocab would have silently taken the unfused path regardless — precisely the OOM risk this task exists to prevent. `mtp_extra_heads`/`mtp_loss_weight` had the identical gap. Fixed: `--loss-chunk-size`/`--z-loss-weight` added to both `train/pretrain.py` and `train/sft.py`; `--mtp-extra-heads`/`--mtp-loss-weight` added to `train/pretrain.py` (constructs `MTPHeads`, adds its params to the optimizer, passes `mtp_heads=` to `train_updates`) with a new guard rejecting `--resume` combined with `--mtp-extra-heads > 0` (MTP head weights are not part of the saved checkpoint, so resuming would silently reinitialize them — documented, not silently broken). Four new functional tests in `tests/test_pretrain_cli.py` exercise real training runs through the new flags, not just `--help`.
  - **Follow-up (2026-09-07): three real memory findings in `_ChunkedCrossEntropy`, all fixed.** (1) `grad_hidden_z`/`grad_weight_z` were allocated unconditionally regardless of `z_loss_weight` — at 32k vocab, `grad_weight_z` alone is ~100MB held from forward through backward for nothing when z-loss is off (the default); now allocated only when `z_loss_weight` is truthy. (2) The per-chunk loop held three `[chunk_size, vocab_size]`-shaped tensors simultaneously (`logits_chunk`, `probabilities`, and a `.clone()` of `probabilities`) — roughly 3x the necessary peak; now reduced to ~1x by computing z-loss's gradient from the untouched softmax first, then mutating `probabilities` in place for the main gradient instead of cloning it, and freeing `logits_chunk` as soon as `probabilities` exists. (3) The default `chunk_size` (1024) was larger than necessary; lowered to 256 (documented tradeoff: negligible extra kernel-launch count against a real ~4x smaller peak). All three verified via the existing gradcheck/equivalence tests, which still pass unchanged.
  - **Follow-up (2026-09-07): a real precision gap, and a genuinely missing test.** The module's own comment claimed "FP32 (or better) throughout," but under a real `torch.autocast(dtype=float16)` context (the precision every actual training run uses), `matmul` casts its inputs to FP16 regardless of an explicit `.to(torch.float32)` beforehand — verified empirically. Only the reductions (`logsumexp`, `softmax`, `gather`) are actually FP32-guaranteed by autocast's own promotion rules; the matmuls follow the ambient precision, identically to how the old unfused path's plain `lm_head` matmul already behaved under the same context — not a regression, but the comment was wrong, and none of the five original tests exercised this combination (they all ran without autocast). Fixed: comment corrected to describe what's actually guaranteed; new CUDA-gated `test_cuda_chunked_matches_unchunked_loss_under_real_autocast` in `tests/test_loss.py` verifies chunked-vs-unchunked equivalence under a real `autocast(dtype=torch.float16)` context, the scenario that actually matters for the real run. Full suite passes (237 passed, up from 232), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean.

### MF-085 — Document-boundary intra-document attention masking for packed shards

- **Priority / state:** P2 / Not started — schema half folds into [[MF-087]]'s repack
- **Depends on:** MF-034 (shard packing), MF-053 (SFT packing), MF-087 (the repack this schema addition rides along with)
- **Deliverable:** a `doc_ids` array alongside `PackedSequence`'s existing fields (and the equivalent in `pack_sft_examples`), recording which source document each packed position came from, plus an optional FlexAttention `mask_mod` that also requires `doc_id[q] == doc_id[kv]` (in addition to the existing causal/window rules) so a token cannot attend across a packed document boundary.
- **Acceptance:** adding `doc_ids` to the shard schema does not change any existing training run's behavior when the new masking is left off (the default); a real bounded comparison (matched tokens, real validation) measures whether intra-document masking actually helps at this project's scale before it becomes a default; SFT packing's cross-conversation leakage (Zhao et al., arXiv:2402.13991, ACL 2024) is addressed by the same mechanism.
- **Status note:** proposed by external code review. **Revised 2026-09-06** per the reviewer's own follow-up: since [[MF-087]]'s tokenizer change already forces a full repack of every shard (old token IDs become meaningless under the new vocabulary), writing `doc_ids` into the shard schema during that *same* repack pass is nearly free — doing it later would mean a second full repack. The schema addition therefore moves into [[MF-087]]'s scope directly; this task now covers only the masking logic itself (left off by default) and its own bounded real test, to be run whenever it's picked up. Not started.
- **A third real, independent frontier-lab confirmation (2026-09-11), from DeepSeek-V4.1-Flash's real tech report (`ds4/DeepSeek_V41_Tech_Report.txt`, user-provided, read directly):** "Similar to DeepSeek-V4, we employ sample-level attention masking during pre-training" (their §4.2.2) -- joining Llama 3's own real documented practice ([[MF-097]]'s own 2026-09-10 research pass) as further, current, independent confirmation that intra-document masking is standard frontier practice, not a niche idea. Does not change this task's still-not-started status or its own acceptance criterion (a real bounded test at this project's scale before adoption) -- recorded as additional supporting citation only.

### MF-086 — Evaluation harness expansion: BPB, long-context retrieval, and broader lm-eval coverage

- **Priority / state:** P1 / Done — all four parts complete, see status notes
- **Depends on:** MF-066 (existing 5-tier gate)
- **Deliverable:** (1) a standing bits-per-byte evaluation path (not just the ad hoc scratch recipe reused informally in several `reports/mf070-*` comparisons this session) usable as a first-class metric anywhere token-level comparison is unsafe — critically, comparing checkpoints trained under *different* tokenizers, which `ComparisonKey` currently refuses outright; (2) a synthetic long-context needle/retrieval eval at multiple context fractions (512/1024/2048+), the eval [[MF-082]] is blocked on; (3) BLiMP and additional lm-eval tasks (Lambada, WinoGrande, OpenBookQA, CommonsenseQA, BoolQ) added to `DEFAULT_TASKS` or its gate-tier equivalent, since `arc_easy`/`hellaswag`/`piqa` alone score near chance at this project's current scale and would not detect most of the improvements proposed in [[MF-081]]; (4) post-hoc checkpoint EMA averaging in `checkpoint.py` as a cheap, optional quality lever.
- **Acceptance:** BPB is computable and reported for any two checkpoints regardless of tokenizer; the long-context eval runs against an existing real checkpoint (e.g. the MF-065 1B-token Modern release) and produces a real, recorded result, not just infrastructure; new lm-eval tasks run cleanly in this project's existing harness (matching the Windows-compatibility work already done for HumanEval/MBPP in [[MF-066]]); EMA is opt-in and round-trips through checkpoint save/resume.
- **Status note:** proposed by external code review, correctly identifying that this project's current default eval set is too thin (near-chance at 150M scale, run-to-run noise likely exceeding small effect sizes like [[MF-081]]'s ~1.6% loss delta) to actually validate any of the other proposed changes. This task is a prerequisite for evaluating [[MF-081]] and [[MF-082]] meaningfully, not just a nice-to-have — raised to P1 for that reason. **Revised 2026-09-06**: parts (1) and (2) are the priority — bits-per-byte is already computed ad hoc, informally, in several `reports/mf070-*` comparisons and `mf065-*-validation.json` this session (the recipe exists, just not promoted to a standing, reusable, tokenizer-agnostic evaluation path), so part (1) is smaller than originally scoped; part (2) is what [[MF-082]] is genuinely blocked on. Parts (3) (BLiMP/additional lm-eval tasks) and (4) (checkpoint EMA) remain real but lower urgency. Not started.
- **Progress (2026-09-08): parts (1) and (2) done for real, both with real recorded results.** Part (1): `batches_from_packed_shards(dataset, tokenizer, *, batch_size, device)` added to `src/minifrontier/evaluation/validation.py` as the one standing, reusable path for the tokenizer-agnostic BPB recipe — decodes each packed sequence back to UTF-8 (skipping padding) purely to count real bytes, exactly the recipe `compare_tokenizers.py`/`compare_mtp.py` had each separately reimplemented. Both scripts' own duplicated `_validation_batches`/`_stack_validation_batch` functions were deleted and their call sites switched to the standing function (pure refactor, no behavior change — full fast suite still 286 passed, `ruff check`/`format --check` clean on all touched files, both scripts' `--help` smoke-tested as real subprocesses after the edit).
  Part (2): `src/minifrontier/evaluation/retrieval.py` (new) implements a real Kamradt-style needle-in-haystack eval — `build_needle_prompt` embeds an unguessable 6-digit code at a controlled token-fraction position inside filler text, `run_needle_trial`/`run_needle_haystack_eval` generate greedily and check the exact code is reproduced. 7 new tests in `tests/test_retrieval.py`. `scripts/eval_needle_haystack.py` (new) is the CLI driver. **Real result** (`reports/mf086-needle-haystack.json`, run against the real `artifacts/mf065-150m-modern-1b-release` checkpoint, `local_window=512`): retrieval rate by context length **{512: 1.0, 1024: 0.4, 2032: 0.0}** — confirms [[MF-082]]'s premise directly: real, measurable long-context retrieval collapse once context exceeds the hybrid cache's local window, not a hypothetical risk. This unblocks [[MF-082]], which was gated exactly on this eval existing and showing a real degradation.
  Parts (3) (BLiMP/Lambada/WinoGrande/OpenBookQA/CommonsenseQA/BoolQ) and (4) (checkpoint EMA) remain deferred per the 2026-09-06 revision — real but lower urgency than unblocking [[MF-081]]/[[MF-082]], not scheduled before [[MF-070]]. Task kept open (not marked Done) until a decision is made on whether to implement (3)/(4) before or after [[MF-070]]'s 350M run.
- **Done (2026-09-10): parts (3) and (4) implemented and tested for real, closing this task.** Part (3): `src/minifrontier/evaluation/language.py` gained `EXTENDED_TASKS = ("blimp", "lambada_openai", "winogrande", "openbookqa", "commonsense_qa", "boolq")` and `harness_settings(..., include_extended: bool = False)`, kept opt-in like the existing `include_gsm8k`/`OPTIONAL_TASKS` pattern rather than folded into `DEFAULT_TASKS` -- `blimp` alone expands to 67 real subtasks (confirmed directly against the installed `lm_eval`'s own `TaskManager`), so appending unconditionally would multiply the cost of every quick default-task smoke/comparison run, not just real evaluation passes. `scripts/eval.py` gained `--include-extended`. **Every one of the 6 new task names was actually run** (not just confirmed to exist) through this project's real `MiniFrontierEvalLM` adapter against a real tiny model/tokenizer, via `lm_eval.simple_evaluate` -- `winogrande`/`openbookqa`/`boolq`/`lambada_openai`/`commonsense_qa` individually (limit=2, real HF dataset downloads succeeded, all reported `OK`), and `blimp` as the full real group (limit=1, real result: `68` -- the 67 real BLiMP paradigm subtasks plus the aggregate summary row), matching the same "does it actually work on Windows" verification [[MF-066]] already did for HumanEval/MBPP. New tests: `test_harness_settings_include_extended_adds_the_mf086_tasks`, `test_harness_settings_include_gsm8k_and_extended_compose` (`tests/test_evaluation.py`).
