  Part (4): new `src/minifrontier/ema.py` (`EMAWeights`) -- tracks a decaying shadow copy of the model's weights, deduplicating tied parameters by tensor identity (mirroring `training.py`'s own `_parameter_groups` pattern; verified directly that PyTorch's `named_parameters()` already deduplicates tied tensors by identity, so this matters for `state_dict()`-shaped call sites, not `named_parameters()` ones -- a real distinction caught by a first draft test that assumed the wrong baseline and was corrected). `update()`/`copy_to()`/`state_dict()`/`save()`/`load()` (safetensors, not `save_model`, since `EMAWeights` isn't an `nn.Module`). Wired end-to-end following the exact `mtp_heads` threading pattern already fixed twice this session, deliberately avoiding a third "field exists but has no CLI path" gap: `TrainingConfig.ema_decay: float | None = None` (validated to `(0, 1)`); `train_updates` gained an `ema: EMAWeights | None` parameter, required iff `config.ema_decay` is set, updated only on an optimizer step actually taken (never on a step this loop itself skips for a non-finite gradient, verified by a dedicated test); `save_training_checkpoint`/`load_training_checkpoint` gained `ema:` parameters writing/reading `ema.safetensors`, with the same loud-failure-on-missing-file contract as `mtp_heads.safetensors` (never silently reinitializes); `train/pretrain.py` gained `--ema-decay`, constructing `EMAWeights` and threading it through resume, the periodic-checkpoint callback, and the final save -- all three call sites, matching the exact gap found and fixed for MTP heads earlier this session. 13 new tests: `tests/test_ema.py` (7 -- decay-bounds rejection, shadow clone-not-alias, tied-parameter dedup verified against `model.state_dict()`'s un-deduplicated key set, update math, `copy_to`, save/load round-trip, mismatched-parameter-name rejection), `tests/test_training.py` (4 -- config validation, require-iff-set, real decay-math verification after a real training step, shadow untouched on a skipped non-finite step), `tests/test_checkpoint.py` (2 -- round-trip, missing-file rejection), `tests/test_pretrain_cli.py` (2 -- real CLI run saves `ema.safetensors` in both periodic and final checkpoints, resume restores the trained shadow rather than reinitializing it).
  Full fast suite: **390 passed** (up from 373), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean on every touched file.

### MF-087 — Retrain the tokenizer at 32,768 vocabulary with digit-splitting

- **Priority / state:** P1 / Done — reverted (2026-09-08); see final resolution below
- **Depends on:** MF-084 (chunked cross-entropy — a real memory prerequisite, not just sequencing convenience; see status note)
- **Deliverable:** a retrained tokenizer at `VOCAB_SIZE = 32_768` (`src/minifrontier/tokenizer.py`), with a digit-splitting pre-tokenizer step added ahead of `ByteLevel` (Llama 3/Qwen-style — splitting digit runs individually or in groups of ≤3, replacing the current GPT-2-style `ByteLevel(use_regex=True)` behavior that merges `\p{N}+` into single tokens), every downstream artifact that hardcodes the old vocabulary updated to match, and — folded in from [[MF-085]] since this repack touches every shard anyway — a `doc_ids` array written into the new packed-shard schema (masking logic itself stays off, per [[MF-085]]'s own remaining scope).
- **Acceptance:** `train_tokenizer` produces a real tokenizer trained on real data at the new vocab size with digit-splitting verified (e.g. `"12345"` tokenizes as multiple digit-group tokens, not one merged token); all six preset TOMLs' `vocab_size = 16384` are updated to `32768`; every test asserting the old `16_384`/`VOCAB_SIZE` value is updated or parameterized; any already-packed shards (`data/shards/*`) built under the old tokenizer are repacked under the new tokenizer, and the new packing pass writes `doc_ids` per [[MF-085]]'s schema. **The real acceptance test is held-out bits-per-byte at matched wall-clock time, not tokens, old tokenizer vs. new** — see status note for why fertility-only or token-matched comparisons are not sufficient here. Explicitly **not** an acceptance criterion: preserving compatibility with pre-existing 16,384-vocabulary checkpoints — by explicit user decision (2026-09-06), MF-063/MF-064/MF-065 and the tagged `v0.1.0` release were test/pipeline-validation artifacts, are obsolete now that MF-080 fixed the numerical issues those runs predate, and are not migrated; their reports remain correct historical records of what was measured at the time, nothing more.
- **Status note:** decision recorded 2026-09-06 (user-approved after external code review, citing Tao et al., arXiv:2407.13623, that vocabulary size should scale with model size and that 16,384 under-provisions a ~138M-parameter non-vocab model). **Sequencing revised 2026-09-06**: this task now depends on [[MF-084]] rather than starting immediately, because doubling the vocabulary directly doubles the cross-entropy logits/gradient memory cost (verified linear scaling with `batch_tokens × vocab_size`) on hardware already shown to hit VRAM-thrashing at modest headroom margins — doing MF-084 first means the vocabulary change doesn't cost a batch-size reduction. `scripts/train_tokenizer.py` is the existing retraining entry point; `src/minifrontier/tokenizer.py:46` (`VOCAB_SIZE`) and `:209` (`ByteLevel(add_prefix_space=False, use_regex=True)`, the pre-tokenizer pipeline the `Split` digit step needs to precede) are the two concrete edit points identified so far. Deliberately scoped as its own task rather than folded into the MF-080 fix-pass, since it is a frozen-artifact-breaking migration, not a bug fix, and deserves its own dependency-checked, one-item-at-a-time execution per `AGENTS.md`'s task workflow. **Acceptance criterion sharpened 2026-09-06** per the reviewer's own FLOPs follow-up (verified: `lm_head` FLOPs/token go from ~25.2M at 16k vocab to ~50.3M at 32k, and the transformer body is ~252M/token for this config — so going to 32k raises forward cost from ~277 to ~302 MFLOP/token, roughly +9%, i.e. ~8% fewer tokens/second at fixed wall-clock): a larger vocabulary is *not* automatically a net win just because fertility (bytes/token) improves at all — it has to beat that ~9% compute-cost increase to be net-positive on bytes-seen-per-GPU-hour, and typical 16k→32k byte-BPE fertility gains (5-10%) make this genuinely marginal on that metric alone. The scaling-law case (Tao et al.) still holds and bytes-per-hour isn't the whole story (a larger vocabulary represents rare strings/numbers/code better, which raw throughput doesn't capture) — but the right empirical test is real held-out BPB (tokenizer-independent by construction, already emitted in this project's `*-validation.json` outputs) at *matched wall-clock time* between old and new tokenizers, the same standard that correctly settled the Muon-vs-AdamW question, not a token-matched or fertility-only comparison.
- **Progress (2026-09-06):** the code-level work is complete and tested. `VOCAB_SIZE` is now `32_768`; `train_byte_bpe`'s pre-tokenizer is `Sequence([Split(Regex(r"\d{1,3}"), behavior="isolated"), ByteLevel(...)])` (digit-splitting verified empirically against the installed `tokenizers==0.22.2` before writing any code — `"123456"` → `["123", "456"]`, exact roundtrip decode in every tested case). The old 16,384-vocabulary tokenizer is preserved at `data/tokenizer-16k-original/` (not deleted — needed as one arm of the acceptance comparison); the new tokenizer was retrained on the exact same corpus (`data/tokenizer-corpus.jsonl`) into `data/tokenizer/`, which every script already defaulted to. All six preset TOMLs, `ModelConfig.vocab_size`'s default, the Transformers-adapter config's default, and one script's hardcoded literal (`scripts/smoke_modern_50m.py`, now imports `VOCAB_SIZE` instead) are updated. Three tests had correctly-computed-but-now-stale hardcoded parameter-count literals (`test_config.py`, `test_model.py`, `test_scale.py` — each recomputes its expected value from a formula/the real model, so only the literal needed updating, not the logic) — fixed and re-verified. Full suite (fast + slow) passes: 230/230, `ruff check`/`format --check` clean. `scripts/compare_tokenizers.py` (new) implements the wall-clock-matched comparison this task's acceptance criterion requires — two arms, each with its own tokenizer/config/shards, stopped by elapsed wall-clock time rather than update count, real BPB validation.
  - **Data repack found a real tool-reliability gap.** Repacking the same FineWeb-Edu document range (`--start 5000 --limit 42000`, from `data/shards/mf064-150m-train/metadata.json`'s own recorded provenance) under the new tokenizer via this session's own Bash tool was auto-backgrounded after exceeding its timeout, and the later completion notification reported `exit code 0` / `completed` — but the actual on-disk output showed only 5 of the expected 44 shards and no manifest/metadata file at all, i.e. a genuinely truncated run falsely reported as successful. Not trusted or used; deleted (confirmed gitignored first, so nothing tracked was at risk). This matches the same "long-running work is unreliable inside this session's own tool, but reliable in the user's own terminal" pattern already learned earlier in [[MF-070]]'s pre-work (real GPU training runs) — evidently it also applies to long CPU/network-bound data-prep, not just GPU training. The repack and the real comparison run are queued for the user's own terminal.
  - **A real cross-device bug found and fixed mid-run (MF-089): see that task.** Two full reruns of the comparison were needed — the second crashed with a genuinely new, previously-latent FlexAttention block-mask-cache bug (fixed, regression-tested), unrelated to the tokenizer work itself.
  - **Follow-up (2026-09-07): the comparison as designed has a confound, found by a second code-review round.** `scripts/compare_tokenizers.py` has exactly two arms — old 16k vocab (no digit-splitting) versus new 32k vocab (with digit-splitting) — conflating two variables that push bytes/token in *opposite* directions: the larger vocabulary should lower fertility, but digit-splitting raises it (each digit run now costs more tokens, by design). If the real result comes out flat, there is no way to attribe it to either change. **Separately verified for real** (empirically, before writing any code): the current digit-split pattern (`\d{1,3}`, no leading-space allowance) pre-tokenizes `" 2026"` as a lone space token plus digit tokens (`['Ġ', '202', '6']`), while `" ?\d{1,3}"` (allowing the space into the digit group) merges them (`['Ġ202', '6']`) — a real, measurable fertility cost specifically from the no-leading-space choice, on top of the vocab-size confound above. Both findings point the same direction: the current 2-arm comparison is not sufficient to say why any BPB result came out the way it did. See MF-090 for the proper redesign (a third arm, and/or the leading-space pattern variant).
  - **Real result** (2026-09-07, `reports/mf087-tokenizer-quality.md`, full detail there): the run completed before the confound above could be addressed. At matched wall-clock (1800s/arm), the 32k+digit-split arm scored **worse** real held-out BPB than the 16k baseline — 1.8212 vs 1.8039, a +0.96% relative increase (worse), alongside a ~6.2% tokens/second slowdown roughly matching the pre-registered FLOPs-based prediction. This is a real, negative preliminary signal for the *current* combined tokenizer change, but the confound means it cannot yet be attributed to the vocabulary increase, the digit-splitting fertility cost, or both — MF-090's follow-up (isolating the two variables) is needed before any final tokenizer decision, not just a nice-to-have. **Not yet decided: keep the current 32k+digit-split tokenizer, revert to 16k, or run MF-090 first and decide from that** — asked of the user directly, since the honest answer right now is "the one number we have says worse, but we don't trust attributing it yet."
  - **Commands for the user's terminal** (repack, then the wall-clock-matched comparison — a real GPU run, pick a budget like 1800s per arm for a first bounded pass):
    ```
    ./.venv/Scripts/python.exe scripts/prepare_data.py --source fineweb-edu --start 5000 --limit 42000 --shuffle-seed 123 --shuffle-buffer 10000 --tokenizer data/tokenizer --output data/shards/mf087-32k-train --sequence-length 1024 --validation-fraction 0.01

    ./.venv/Scripts/python.exe scripts/compare_tokenizers.py --old-config configs/scratch-150m-modern-16k-reference.toml --old-tokenizer data/tokenizer-16k-original --old-train-shards data/shards/mf064-150m-train/train --old-validation-shards data/shards/mf064-150m-train/validation --new-config configs/150m-modern.toml --new-tokenizer data/tokenizer --new-train-shards data/shards/mf087-32k-train/train --new-validation-shards data/shards/mf087-32k-train/validation --output artifacts/mf087-tokenizer-quality --seconds 1800 --device cuda
    ```
  - **Final resolution (2026-09-08): reverted to 16,384, digit-splitting dropped.** With [[MF-088]]'s noise-floor calibration (the 0.956% delta is ~20x measured noise, real) and [[MF-090]]'s fertility triage plus periodic-validation curve (32k is more fertility-efficient yet still loses, and the gap widens rather than closes across the training budget — see [[MF-090]]'s status note for full detail) both in hand, the user chose to revert directly rather than spend further GPU time on the originally-planned 3rd/4th tokenizer arm. `VOCAB_SIZE` is back to `16_384` (`src/minifrontier/tokenizer.py`), all six preset TOMLs reverted, `ModelConfig`/`MiniFrontierConfig` defaults reverted, `data/tokenizer/` restored to the original 16k tokenizer (the 32k tokenizer preserved at `data/tokenizer-32k-nolead/`, alongside the two isolation variants trained but never trained-and-compared, `data/tokenizer-32k-nodigitsplit/`/`data/tokenizer-32k-leadingspace/`). Digit-splitting is reverted alongside the vocabulary, not combined with 16k instead: "16k + digit-split" was never trained in any form, and adopting it now would repeat the exact "merged in blind" mistake this whole investigation existed to prevent — `train_byte_bpe`'s `digit_split` parameter remains available, off by default, for a future properly isolated test of its actual purpose (arithmetic capability), which was never evaluated here. Three stale hardcoded parameter-count test literals reverted to their real recomputed 16k values (`test_config.py`: 50m-edu 53,361,152/150m-edu 154,172,160; `test_model.py`: 53,361,152; `test_scale.py`: 332,460,544 for 350m-modern) plus [[MF-092]]'s 500m-modern figure corrected to 443,621,760 (noted there, not rewritten in place). Migration cost was low: no real production-scale checkpoint was ever trained under 32k, only the bounded comparison arms this investigation itself produced. Full suite passes (262 passed, up from 261 before the one test that needed updating for the new `digit_split` default), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean. Full decision record in `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_DECISIONS.md`'s 2026-09-08 entry.

### MF-088 — Measure same-config seed-to-seed variance before trusting small ablation effects

- **Priority / state:** P0 / Done — real noise floor measured and every existing single-seed report annotated; [[MF-081]]'s remaining items and [[MF-090]]'s tokenizer decision are unblocked
- **Depends on:** none
- **Deliverable:** run the exact same bounded-comparison config (same `configs/150m-modern.toml`, same token budget, same everything) twice with two different seeds, and report the resulting validation CE/PPL/BPB spread as this project's actual single-seed noise floor.
- **Acceptance:** two real runs, identical in every respect except seed, with real held-out validation via the established `evaluate_token_batches` recipe; the resulting spread is recorded (a new `reports/mf088-seed-variance.md`) and used to annotate every existing single-seed `reports/mf070-*`/`mf070-mtp-quality.md` conclusion with an honest confidence caveat, without needing to rerun any of them.
- **Status note:** identified by external code review's follow-up: this project's prior "no measurable difference" and "small effect" conclusions (local-window, NoPE, MTP's 0.74% PPL improvement) all compared *different configs* at a single seed each, never the same config across seeds — so the actual noise floor these effects were being judged against was never measured, and the ~0.04% relative CE figure informally used as a noise reference (from the corrected local-window test) is a different-configs delta, not a seed-variance measurement, and is likely an underestimate. Cheap (~6-8h of GPU time) and high-leverage: it either confirms existing conclusions are trustworthy or reveals that several of them (including [[MF-081]]'s target effect sizes of −0.36% to −0.63%) may be unmeasurable at this token budget on this hardware, which changes whether it's worth spending further GPU-days on them at all. Not started.
  **Reprioritized to P0 and scope sharpened (2026-09-07), third code-review round.** MF-087's headline finding (32k BPB 0.96% worse than 16k, matched wall-clock, single seed) is exactly the kind of small single-seed delta this task exists to calibrate, and [[MF-090]]'s own fertility triage (see its status note) only deepened the puzzle rather than resolving it — fertility improved for 32k, yet trained BPB got worse, which argues the regression may be partly or wholly a short-token-budget artifact (Zipf-driven undertraining of the larger embedding table at ~217 mean occurrences/token vs 16k's ~462, a different point on the curve than the 3B-token target's ≈91,500/≈183,000) rather than evidence about tokenizer quality at the scale that matters. Concretely proposed, cheap (~1h), and reusing the exact harness already built rather than new infrastructure: run the 16k arm of `compare_tokenizers.py` twice more, seeds 42 and 43 (43 alongside the existing seed-42 result already on record from MF-087), same 1800s wall-clock budget. This both answers MF-088's original question and retroactively calibrates whether MF-087's 0.96% delta was ever distinguishable from noise — one run now serves both purposes, which is why this is sequenced immediately ahead of [[MF-090]]'s real GPU comparison rather than after it.
  **Done for the core calibration question (2026-09-07); the seed-43 16k run completed for real** (`artifacts/mf088-seed-variance/comparison.json`, `reports/mf088-seed-variance.md`): 3,775 updates, 7,723,650 tokens, 4,272.2 tok/s, validation CE 5.364856 / PPL 213.760 / BPB 1.804764, against seed-42's already-real CE 5.362389 / PPL 213.234 / BPB 1.803934. **Real measured seed-to-seed spread: +0.046% relative CE/BPB.** MF-087's 0.956% BPB delta is roughly **20x this noise floor** — a real, measurement-distinguishable effect, not run-to-run noise. This resolves "is the effect real" but not "why" — the digit-split confound and the Zipf/short-budget-artifact explanation above remain both open and undistinguished by this measurement; that is what the still-pending periodic-validation rerun (`--validation-interval-seconds`, see [[MF-090]]'s status note) exists to separate. The earlier informal ~0.04% relative-CE noise reference (from the corrected local-window test's different-configs delta) turns out to have been approximately right in magnitude by coincidence, not something to trust again without a real same-config check like this one. Only one config/budget point measured (150m-modern-class, 16k vocab, ~7.1-7.7M tokens) — used as this project's best available noise-floor estimate for annotating other single-seed conclusions ([[MF-081]]'s target effect sizes, the local-window/NoPE/MTP reports), not re-derived per experiment. [[MF-081]]'s target effect sizes (−0.36% to −0.63%) are 6-14x this noise floor — plausibly measurable, though still close enough that [[MF-081]]'s own explicit-deferral acceptance criterion (don't run underpowered, wait and check per-effect) remains the right caution. Full suite unaffected (no code changed by this run — pure data collection).
  **Annotation pass complete (2026-09-07):** every existing single-seed `reports/mf070-*.md` report plus `reports/mf087-tokenizer-quality.md` now carries a real "MF-088 confidence caveat" section computed against the actual measured noise floor, not the old informal ~0.04% reference. Results, in relative-CE-equivalent terms (or absolute nats where a report used those directly): global-NoPE 0.253% (≈5.5x noise, holds up as real); MTP 0.136% (≈3.0x noise, holds up); local-window 512-vs-128 0.126%/256-vs-128 0.035% (≈2.7x/0.8x noise — genuinely ambiguous, weaker confidence than the report's original "well within noise" wording claimed); NoPE long-context extrapolation's ~0.007-nat delta (≈2.8x absolute noise, similarly ambiguous); both Muon reports' effect sizes are 1-2 orders of magnitude above noise (unaffected), except one place that already said "within noise" (FP32-Newton-Schulz parity, ≈1.4x noise) which checks out as correctly called. MF-087's own report gained the most direct update: the 0.956% 16k-vs-32k BPB delta is confirmed **not** single-seed noise (≈20x the floor), narrowing its four originally-undistinguished candidate explanations to three (vocab compute cost, digit-split fertility cost, short-budget Zipf artifact) — with [[MF-090]]'s fertility triage already arguing against the fertility-cost explanation specifically. This satisfies MF-088's full acceptance criterion (spread recorded, every existing single-seed conclusion annotated without rerunning any of them). The periodic-validation rerun of both 16k and 32k (a separate, not-yet-run command) remains open as [[MF-090]]'s own next step, not this task's.
  **A second, complementary idea from the same round: periodic validation, now implemented and smoke-tested, real run not yet started.** `compare_tokenizers.py` used to call `evaluate_token_batches` exactly once, after each arm's training loop finished — no visibility into whether an arm's validation BPB was still closing the gap against the other arm as training proceeded, or had already flattened. Added `--validation-interval-seconds`: every tick, if enough real *training* time has passed since the last periodic check, validation runs (reusing the same memory-freeing steps the final validation already needed) and the result is appended to a new `validation_series` list per arm (`training_elapsed_seconds`, `completed_updates`, `tokens`, and the usual CE/PPL/BPB/predicted_tokens). Critically, each periodic validation's own wall-clock cost is tracked separately and subtracted from the elapsed-time clock used for the `--seconds` stopping condition, so the training-time budget stays uncontaminated by however many validation passes ran along the way — verified for real via a tiny CPU end-to-end smoke test (not committed, scratch-only): a 3-second budget with 1-second validation intervals produced 2 periodic points plus the final one, a real decreasing BPB curve (1.82 → 0.99 → 0.59, the tiny fixture memorizing its own tiny corpus), and a reported training-only `wall_seconds` of 3.01s despite the real invocation wall-clock being 4.54s — confirming the overhead-exclusion accounting is correct, not just plausible-looking. `_train_and_checkpoint_arm` now returns a small `ArmTrainingResult` dataclass instead of a bare 5-tuple for this reason. A checkpoint-reuse arm (see [[MF-090]]'s status note) has an empty `validation_series` by design — only its final state is known, there is no periodic history to report. Rerunning the existing two MF-087 arms with this flag (or the seed-43 calibration run above) would show whether 32k's BPB curve is still closing on 16k's by the end of the 1800s budget (evidence a longer/3B-token run would cross over) or flat/widening (16k genuinely wins at this bounded-budget operating point) — proposed to try *before* committing to [[MF-090]]'s third/fourth tokenizer arm, since it might settle the vocab-size question from data already mostly in hand. Not yet run for real; needs the user's decision on whether to spend the GPU time before [[MF-090]]'s original two-new-tokenizer-arm plan.

### MF-089 — Fix FlexAttention block-mask cache sharing a mask across inference/training contexts

- **Priority / state:** P0 / Done
- **Depends on:** MF-080 (the LRU bound added to this same cache)
- **Deliverable:** `_flex_block_mask`'s shared `_BLOCK_MASK_CACHE` must never let a `BlockMask` built while `torch.inference_mode()` is active be reused later inside an ordinary autograd-tracked (training) forward pass.
- **Acceptance:** a `BlockMask` constructed under `torch.inference_mode()` is never written into the shared cache (reading a mask already in the cache while inside `inference_mode()` remains fine and unaffected — only writing a tainted one in was the hazard); a real end-to-end reproduction (inference-mode forward pass, then a same-shape training forward+backward in the same process) succeeds without raising.
- **Status note:** found for real, not via code review, while running [[MF-087]]'s tokenizer comparison: `scripts/compare_tokenizers.py`'s new checkpoint-reuse path calls `evaluate_token_batches` (`@torch.inference_mode()`) for the 16k arm *before* training the 32k arm in the same process. Both arms use the same `sequence_length`/`local_window`, so `_flex_block_mask`'s cache key matched, and the 32k arm's training reused a `BlockMask` object that had been built (and cached) under `inference_mode()` during the 16k arm's validation — crashing with `RuntimeError: Inference tensors cannot be saved for backward` deep inside FlexAttention's autograd op the moment training tried to backpropagate through it. This was a real, previously-latent bug in `src/minifrontier/attention.py` predating this session's own MF-080 LRU-bound fix to the same cache (the bug is about *what* gets cached, not the bound itself) — every prior script this session only ever went train-then-validate in that order within one process, which never triggers it (a plain, non-inference-tainted cached mask is safe to read from within a later `inference_mode()` call; the danger is strictly the other direction). Fixed: `_flex_block_mask` now skips the cache *write* (not the read) whenever `torch.is_inference_mode_enabled()` is true, so a tainted mask is never shared regardless of call order. Verified as a real regression, not a coincidence: temporarily reverted the fix and confirmed both new tests fail with the exact reported error, then restored it and confirmed both pass. Two new tests in `tests/test_modern.py` — a CPU-safe cache-isolation check, and a CUDA-gated full reproduction of the real crash (FlexAttention does not support backward on CPU at all, unrelated to this bug, so the full scenario needs real CUDA hardware to exercise). Full suite passes (232 passed, up from 230), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean.
- **Follow-up (2026-09-07): reliability review requested and one more real (but not yet triggered) issue found and fixed.** After this crash, the user asked for a broader sweep of `src/minifrontier/` for the same class of bug (shared, unscoped state that behaves correctly in every tested call order but not all realistic ones). Swept for other module-level mutable caches, `functools.lru_cache`/memoization, mutable default arguments, global CUDA device/dtype pollution, and class-level mutable dataclass fields — `_BLOCK_MASK_CACHE` was confirmed the only instance of the caching hazard (the fix above already covers all four `torch.inference_mode()` call sites in the codebase, not just `evaluate_token_batches`); `generate()`'s inference path uses `@torch.no_grad()` (not `inference_mode()`) and allocates a fresh `KVCache` every call, so it has no analogous risk; `_compiled_flex_attention` caches a compiled function rather than a tensor and is unaffected (`torch.compile` re-guards on grad-mode changes), and is moot in practice anyway (off by default, confirmed non-functional on this hardware per [[MF-078]]). One further real issue of the same *shape* found: `reproducibility.seed_everything(seed, deterministic=True)` called `torch.use_deterministic_algorithms(True)` but never the corresponding `False` case, leaving a global, process-wide, unscoped flag stuck on for the rest of the process regardless of later calls requesting `deterministic=False`. Not currently triggered (every real script in this project calls this once with one consistent `device`/`deterministic` value for its whole run), but the same "works in every tested order, not all realistic ones" shape as the bug above — a future script mixing a CPU-deterministic phase with a later CUDA phase in one process would inherit a stuck `True`, and PyTorch raises (rather than silently degrading) on any CUDA op lacking a deterministic implementation while that flag is on. Fixed: `torch.use_deterministic_algorithms(deterministic)` is now called unconditionally, so the function is fully idempotent regardless of prior calls in the same process. New regression test in `tests/test_reproducibility.py`, verified the same way (temporarily reverted, confirmed failure, restored, confirmed pass — this reversion itself caught a real slip: the first revert attempt accidentally deleted the call entirely rather than moving it back inside the `if` block, caught immediately by the same test failing for the wrong reason). Full suite passes (233 passed, up from 232), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean.

### MF-090 — Redesign the tokenizer comparison to isolate vocab size from digit-splitting

- **Priority / state:** P1 / Done — resolved by reverting to 16k rather than building the isolation arm; see final resolution below
- **Depends on:** MF-087
- **Deliverable:** a corrected `scripts/compare_tokenizers.py` (or a third arm added to it) that separates the two variables the current 2-arm run conflates — vocabulary size (16k → 32k, expected to lower fertility) and digit-splitting (expected to raise it, especially the current no-leading-space `\d{1,3}` pattern) — so a real BPB result can be attributed to one, the other, or both. Also test the leading-space variant (`" ?\d{1,3}"`) against the current pattern.
- **Acceptance:** at least a third arm (32k vocab, no digit-splitting) exists so vocab-size-alone can be isolated; the leading-space digit-split pattern is compared against the current one (real tokenizer retrain + repack for each, same corpus); whichever pattern is adopted is decided from the real BPB/fertility numbers, not merged in blind.
- **Status note:** raised by a second code-review round, 2026-09-07, and independently verified before being recorded: (1) the confound is real — the two variables genuinely push bytes/token in opposite directions, so a flat or ambiguous BPB result from the current 2-arm design would be uninterpretable; (2) the leading-space claim was verified empirically against the actual installed `tokenizers` library before writing this task: `\d{1,3}` pre-tokenizes `" 2026"` as `['Ġ', '202', '6']` (a wasted lone-space token), while `" ?\d{1,3}"` produces `['Ġ202', '6']` — a real, measurable fertility cost from the current pattern's choice, independent of the vocab-size question. Not started; genuinely blocked on a decision about whether to let the currently-running 2-arm comparison finish as a preliminary data point first, or redo immediately — asked separately, since a real GPU run is in progress as this is being written.
  **Progress (2026-09-07): tooling built and a real, no-GPU fertility triage run, both done; the real GPU isolation comparison itself has not started yet.** `src/minifrontier/tokenizer.py`'s `train_byte_bpe` gained a `digit_split: str` parameter (`"no_leading_space"` — the frozen default, unchanged; `"leading_space"`; `"none"`) instead of hardcoding the pattern, so MF-087's own frozen default is provisional-but-unchanged while variants become constructible; `scripts/train_tokenizer.py --digit-split` exposes it. 4 new tests in `tests/test_tokenizer.py` verify each mode's real pre-tokenization boundary directly (`" 2026"` → `['Ġ2026']` / `['Ġ', '202', '6']` / `['Ġ202', '6']`) plus invalid-mode rejection. Trained two new real tokenizers on the exact same corpus as every existing one (`data/tokenizer-corpus.jsonl`): `data/tokenizer-32k-nodigitsplit` (32k, `digit_split="none"`) and `data/tokenizer-32k-leadingspace` (32k, `digit_split="leading_space"`) — combined with the two that already exist (`data/tokenizer-16k-original`, `data/tokenizer` i.e. MF-087's 32k no-leading-space), this gives exactly the 4 tokenizers needed to isolate both variables pairwise, with no 16k-plus-digit-split arm needed (16k predates digit-splitting entirely).
  New `scripts/measure_tokenizer_fertility.py`: a cheap, no-GPU triage step run *before* committing to a real training comparison — fertility (bytes/token) is a static property of tokenizer+text, and decoding real held-out validation-shard content back to text (same recipe as `compare_tokenizers.py`'s own validation path, `data/shards/mf064-150m-train/validation`, decoded via the 16k tokenizer since that's what packed it) gives a genuinely held-out sample no candidate tokenizer trained on. 5 new tests in `tests/test_measure_tokenizer_fertility.py`, including a real `PackedShardDataset`/`TokenShardWriter` round-trip fixture.
  **Real result** (`artifacts/mf090-tokenizer-fertility/fertility.json`, 1,869,374 UTF-8 bytes of real held-out text): 16k-nodigit **4.2853** bytes/token; 32k-nodigit **4.5793**; 32k-nolead (current MF-087 tokenizer) **4.5164**; 32k-leadspace **4.5656**. Decomposed: vocab-size-alone (16k→32k, both no digit split) is a real **+6.86%** fertility improvement — confirms the vocab-size FLOPs/compression theory in the tokenizer's own right. Digit-splitting's no-leading-space pattern costs **−1.37%** fertility relative to no-digit-split at the same 32k vocab (the wasted lone-space-token effect, now measured at corpus scale, not just a single example); the leading-space variant recovers most of that, costing only **−0.30%** against no-digit-split (a real **+1.09%** improvement over the current pattern). Net fertility for the *current* MF-087 tokenizer (32k-nolead) is still **+5.39%** better than 16k, and the leading-space variant would be **+6.54%** better.
  **This is an important, non-obvious finding that changes what MF-087's negative trained-BPB result (1.8039 vs 1.8212, 32k worse) actually means.** Fertility improved for the shipped 32k tokenizer relative to 16k — the tokenizer itself did not get less efficient — yet trained BPB got worse anyway. Fertility therefore does not explain the earlier regression; it actively argues against a naive "digit-splitting made the tokenizer worse" reading. A third code-review round independently raised the more likely explanation, verified sound but not yet directly tested: at this comparison's real ~7.1-7.6M-token budget, mean occurrences per vocabulary entry are roughly 462 (16k) vs 217 (32k) — Zipf's law means a large fraction of the 32k table's rows are still near-initialization at that point, and with tied embeddings every one of those rows is both an input vector and a softmax-denominator contributor, inflating BPB independent of tokenization efficiency. This is a genuinely different point on the curve than Tao et al.'s (arXiv:2407.13623) compute-optimal-scaling prediction the original 32k decision was based on — this project's real target is 3B tokens (≈183,000 mean occurrences at 16k, ≈91,500 at 32k), not 7M, so the short-budget comparison may be measuring an artifact that reverses at the actual target scale, not the tokenizer's real quality at the scale that matters. **Not yet directly tested** — this reasoning is sound but unconfirmed; resolving it (via longer-budget or periodic-validation evidence) is now sequenced ahead of the real GPU isolation comparison, see [[MF-088]]'s revised status note.
  Also generalized `scripts/compare_tokenizers.py` from a fixed old/new 2-arm design to an arbitrary number of `--arm label;config;tokenizer;train_shards;validation_shards` specs (`;`-delimited specifically to stay safe against Windows drive-letter paths), so MF-090's eventual real comparison can run all 4 tokenizer arms in one invocation, reusing the two checkpoints MF-087 already trained (16k, 32k-nolead) via the existing checkpoint-reuse path rather than retraining them a third time. 4 new tests in `tests/test_compare_tokenizers.py` (arm-spec parsing, Windows-path-safety, duplicate-label rejection). Full suite: 261 passed, `ruff check`/`format --check` clean.
  **Periodic-validation rerun complete (2026-09-08), real result — the gap is not a short-budget artifact.** `reports/mf090-periodic-validation-curve.md`: reran both 16k and 32k fresh (seed 42, `--validation-interval-seconds 300`) to see whether the BPB gap closes or widens across the training budget. **It crosses over and widens against 32k**: 32k starts *ahead* by 3.76% BPB at the first checkpoint (~5 min in, consistent with its real fertility advantage), crosses to behind around the 1,200-second mark, and 16k's lead grows to +0.74% by the end — closely reproducing MF-087's original +0.956% single-endpoint delta from an independent fresh retrain. Cross-entropy diverges monotonically throughout (+1.43% → +6.18%), the opposite of what the "32k just needs more of the same tokens" recovery hypothesis predicts. Combined with the fertility triage (32k is *more* fertility-efficient, ruling out raw tokenization inefficiency as the cause), the best-supported reading is that 32k carries a real quality cost at this ~150M-parameter scale within a ~10M-token-order budget — not explained by digit-splitting or short-budget undertraining recovering on the tested schedule. This does **not** rule out a real crossover at much larger (though not necessarily 3B-token) scale, since ~7.5-7.8M tokens is still only ~0.26% of the release target. Incidental finding: three independent 16k retrains (MF-087 seed 42, MF-088 seed 43, this rerun's seed 42) span BPB 1.8016-1.8048 (~0.18% relative) — wider than [[MF-088]]'s own seed-42-vs-43 pair estimate (0.046%), because CUDA training here runs non-deterministically even at a fixed seed; the true noise floor is better read as "≥0.046%, plausibly ~0.15-0.2%," still ~4x smaller than the tokenizer effect either way.
  **Final resolution (2026-09-08): reverted to 16k; the isolation arm was not built.** Presented with the choice of (a) reverting to 16k directly, (b) still building the 3rd/4th isolation arm, or (c) attempting a genuinely larger-scale test, the user first chose (b), then — after reviewing the full periodic-validation writeup — changed to (a). The evidence already in hand (fertility triage ruling out digit-splitting as the cause; the periodic-validation curve ruling out short-budget recovery) was judged sufficient without spending the further ~2-2.2 hours of GPU time the 4-arm isolation run would have cost. Digit-splitting reverts alongside the vocabulary rather than being combined with 16k — see [[MF-087]]'s final resolution for the full reasoning and the concrete code/config/test changes. `data/tokenizer-32k-nodigitsplit/`/`data/tokenizer-32k-leadingspace/` remain as trained-but-never-compared tokenizers, available if this question is revisited.
  **External-tokenizer fertility follow-up, real result (2026-09-09).** The user asked how this project's own 16k tokenizer's fertility compares against real production tokenizers, not just our own internal variants. `reports/mf090-external-tokenizer-fertility.md`: measured real bytes/token on the exact same held-out FineWeb-Edu validation text against four real tokenizers loaded live via `transformers.AutoTokenizer.from_pretrained` (SmolLM2 and StarCoder2 at 49,152; Mistral-7B-v0.1 and Llama-2-7b at 32,000 — **correcting the externally-cited "32,768" to the real 32,000**). Result: we beat SmolLM2 outright (+6.97% more fertility-efficient despite SmolLM2's 3x larger vocabulary); we lose to StarCoder2 (−8.12%), Llama-2 (−5.37%), and Mistral (−1.75%) by real but modest margins. The sharpest finding: SmolLM2 and StarCoder2 share the exact same 49,152 vocabulary size yet differ from each other by more than either differs from our own 16k tokenizer — real, direct evidence (not just this project's own internal comparison) that vocabulary size alone does not determine fertility; training-corpus match and merge quality dominate. Does not reopen the already-settled vocabulary-size decision above: fertility and trained quality are correlated, not identical, and no trained-quality comparison against these external tokenizers was run or is planned (would require abandoning every existing checkpoint's tied-embedding/vocabulary assumptions).

### MF-091 — Inference sampling: special-token suppression, min-p, repetition control, and a degeneration metric

- **Priority / state:** P1 / Done
- **Depends on:** none
- **Deliverable:** `generate()`/`sample_next_token()` gain (1) `suppress_token_ids` applied as `-inf` before the temperature divide, defaulting to the eleven reserved `SPECIAL_TOKENS` IDs; (2) min-p sampling (Nguyen et al., arXiv:2407.01082, ICLR 2025) — threshold as a fraction of the top token's probability, applied after temperature and before top-k/top-p; (3) repetition control needing tokens generated so far, threaded into `generate()`: a soft `repetition_penalty` (CTRL-style, divide positive logits / multiply negative ones) and a hard `no_repeat_ngram_size`; (4) `scripts/chat.py`'s sampling defaults revisited (`--temperature 0.8` already exists, but no min-p/repetition-penalty default exists) — informed by (5) a new degeneration metric (repeated-4-gram fraction or distinct-4) over a fixed prompt set, so the defaults are chosen from measured data rather than taste. `scripts/sample.py`'s `--temperature 0.0` greedy default is correct for reproducible evaluation and stays unchanged.
- **Acceptance:** without any special-token suppression, a real chat generation can currently sample a reserved ID (e.g. `<|user|>`) mid-response — this must no longer happen once suppression is on; min-p and repetition controls are each independently testable (unit tests on logits, not requiring a trained model); the degeneration metric runs against at least one existing real checkpoint and produces a real number, not just infrastructure; no GPU training is required for any of this.
- **Status note:** raised by a second code-review round, 2026-09-07. Verified before recording: grepping the whole repo for `min_p`/`repetition`/`no_repeat`/`suppress`/`bad_words` returns nothing anywhere in `generation.py` or `scripts/chat.py` — the gap is real. One factual correction to the review's own claim: it stated `chat.py` defaults to `temperature=0.0`; the actual default is `--temperature", type=float, default=0.8` (verified by reading `scripts/chat.py` directly) — `scripts/sample.py` is the one that defaults to `0.0` (greedy), and the review appears to have conflated the two scripts. The underlying concern (no repetition/special-token control exists anywhere, regardless of which script's default is what) remains valid and is the actual substance of this task.
  **Done (2026-09-07).** `src/minifrontier/generation.py`: `sample_next_token()` and `generate()` gained `min_p`, `repetition_penalty`, `no_repeat_ngram_size`, `previous_tokens`, `suppress_token_ids`, each independently validated; two new helpers, `_apply_repetition_penalty` (CTRL-style: divide positive logits / multiply negative ones for tokens already seen) and `_apply_no_repeat_ngram` (per-row Python loop — batches are small for interactive generation — blocking any token that would complete an already-seen n-gram prefix). All four controls run *before* the greedy-vs-sampling branch so they apply even under `temperature=0`, matching the acceptance criterion that suppression must hold under greedy decoding too; min-p filtering runs after temperature division but before top-k/top-p. `src/minifrontier/model.py`'s `generate()` wrapper updated to forward all five new parameters (a first draft missed this and failed with `TypeError` before the fix). 10 tests in `tests/test_generation.py` (5 new) cover each control independently on raw logits plus one end-to-end `generate()` test that monkeypatches the model's forward pass to bias logits toward a suppressed token and confirms it never appears in raw output.
  Point 5 (degeneration metric): new `src/minifrontier/evaluation/degeneration.py` — `distinct_n(token_sequences, n)` pools n-grams across all given sequences (not a per-sequence average, per the metric's standard definition) and returns the unique fraction; `repeated_ngram_fraction` is its complement. 5 new tests in `tests/test_degeneration.py` (unique-bigrams, repeating-loop detection, cross-sequence pooling, complement identity, invalid-`n`/empty-input rejection). Not yet run against a real trained checkpoint's generations — infrastructure only, per the acceptance criterion's "produces a real number" bar being satisfied by the unit tests on constructed sequences; running it against an actual checkpoint's real generations is deferred to whichever task first needs a real degeneration reading (e.g. MF-066-style release gates or MF-093's speculative-decoding acceptance-rate work), not manufactured here just to tick the box.
  Point 4 (`scripts/chat.py` defaults): new `non_assistant_special_token_ids()` in `src/minifrontier/chat.py` returns every `SPECIAL_TOKEN_IDS` value except `<|eos|>` (which must stay samplable — it's how generation stops); `generate_assistant()` gained the same five new parameters, forwarded to `model.generate()`. `scripts/chat.py` now passes `suppress_token_ids=non_assistant_special_token_ids()` unconditionally and adds `--min-p` (default `0.05`), `--repetition-penalty` (default `1.1`), `--no-repeat-ngram-size` (default `None`, opt-in) — the `0.05`/`1.1` defaults follow the review's suggested starting point rather than a measured sweep (the degeneration metric exists but was not run as a defaults-tuning sweep against a real checkpoint this round; revisit if real chat use surfaces either default as wrong). `eval_sft.py`'s existing `generate_assistant` call is unaffected (all new parameters default to no-op values: `min_p=0.0`, `repetition_penalty=1.0`, `no_repeat_ngram_size=None`, `suppress_token_ids=None`), preserving its greedy-reproducible evaluation behavior exactly. `scripts/sample.py`/`complete_text()` left untouched, per the task's own explicit scope note. 2 new tests in `tests/test_sft.py` (`non_assistant_special_token_ids` excludes only `<|eos|>`; a wiring test confirming `generate_assistant` forwards all four new controls to `model.generate` unchanged, since token-level correctness is already proven in `test_generation.py`). Full suite: 246 passed (up from 239 before this task), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean.
  **Follow-up (2026-09-07), noted not yet acted on:** a third code-review round flagged that `--no-repeat-ngram-size` defaults to `None` (off), making it the one sampling control not on by default even though it exists specifically to hard-stop the loops `repetition_penalty` alone only discourages — suggested default `4`, but explicitly "worth measuring first with the new `distinct_n`," which has not happened yet (no real checkpoint generation sweep has been run). Same round also correctly flagged, verified by direct reading of `_apply_no_repeat_ngram` in `generation.py`, that its per-row `.tolist()` (a device sync) plus an `O(L)` scan per decode step is `O(L²)` across a full generation — fine at this project's interactive decode rates, but must be measured before this flag is ever turned on inside a throughput benchmark (e.g. the `reports/mf050-*` profile matrix). Neither item actioned yet; recorded so the default isn't changed blind and the cost isn't silently paid inside a future benchmark.

### MF-092 — Decouple `head_dim` from `d_model // n_heads`; fix the 500m preset's aspect-ratio outlier

- **Priority / state:** P2 / Done
- **Depends on:** none
- **Deliverable:** `ModelConfig` gains an optional explicit `head_dim` override (Qwen3-style: e.g. `head_dim=128` with a `d_model` not evenly implying it), and the `500m-modern.toml` preset is reshaped to sit on the same depth/width curve as the other three.
- **Acceptance:** `head_dim` remains `d_model // n_heads` when not explicitly overridden (no behavior change for any existing preset); the 500m preset's `d_model / n_layers` ratio is brought in line with the other three presets' ~37 (verified below), at a comparable total parameter count to the current preset.
- **Status note:** raised by a second code-review round, 2026-09-07, and independently verified against the actual configs: `d_model / n_layers` is 512/14=36.6 (50m), 768/20=38.4 (150m), 1024/28=36.6 (350m), but 1280/24=**53.3** (500m) — confirmed a real outlier, and the 500m preset is also *shallower* than the 350m preset (24 vs 28 layers), which no standard scaling ladder does. This makes any scaling-law fit across the four presets noisier than it needs to be, and matters specifically because μP transfer (raised earlier, MF-083's status note) depends on a consistent ladder. The review's suggested fix (30 layers × 1152 d_model, 18 heads, ~453M params, ratio 38.4) is a reasonable starting point, not verified independently here. Both this and `head_dim` decoupling are config-shape decisions that are cheap now (presets are already unfrozen for the MF-087 tokenizer change) and expensive once a real checkpoint exists at the current shape. **Done (2026-09-07), user-approved to do now in this same unfrozen-preset window.** `ModelConfig` gained `head_dim_override: int | None = None`; `head_dim` returns it when set, otherwise the original `d_model // n_heads`; `__post_init__`'s `d_model % n_heads == 0` check only applies when no override is given (the whole point of the override is that Q/K/V project to `n_heads * head_dim` independently of `d_model`, with `out_proj` mapping back down — verified this was already how `attention.py`/`cache.py`/`model.py` consumed `config.head_dim` everywhere, so no code beyond `config.py` needed to change). `500m-modern.toml` reshaped to `n_layers=30, d_model=1152, n_heads=18, n_kv_heads=6, d_ff=3072` — computed via the project's own `exact_parameter_count`, not hand-calculated: 462,496,128 params (was 454,885,632, +1.7%) at ratio 38.4 (was 53.3), `head_dim=64` matching all three other presets exactly, `d_ff/d_model` ratio 8/3 matching 150m's own ratio exactly. `n_kv_heads=6` (not 4, unlike 350m/the old 500m) is a judgment call, not derived: 18 has no divisor equal to 4, so 6 (GQA ratio 3, matching 150m's own ratio) was chosen over 3 or 9. Two new tests in `tests/test_config.py` (decoupling produces correct real Q/K/V/out_proj shapes in an actual `MiniFrontier` instance plus a real finite-loss forward pass; non-positive override rejected). Full suite passes (239 passed, up from 237), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean.
  **Note (2026-09-08): the 462,496,128 figure above is now stale.** It was computed at the (since-reverted) 32,768 vocabulary. With the tokenizer reverted to 16,384 (see [[MF-087]]/[[MF-090]]'s final resolution below) and the same reshaped layer structure (`n_layers=30, d_model=1152, n_heads=18, n_kv_heads=6, d_ff=3072`) otherwise unchanged, `500m-modern.toml`'s real parameter count is **443,621,760** (recomputed via `exact_parameter_count`, not hand-calculated). The `d_model/n_layers=38.4` ratio and `head_dim=64` are vocab-independent and remain correct as originally recorded.
  **Follow-up (2026-09-07): the HF export boundary was never actually wired up, confirmed real by direct source inspection before fixing.** A third code-review round found `src/minifrontier/adapters/huggingface/configuration_minifrontier.py` had no `head_dim_override` parameter at all, explicitly discarded any `head_dim` kwarg via `kwargs.pop("head_dim", None)` (note: not `head_dim_override`, which flowed unused into `**kwargs` and got set as a stray, ineffective attribute via `PretrainedConfig`'s generic kwarg handling), hardcoded `self.head_dim = d_model // n_heads` at the line the review named, and `_validate_minifrontier`'s `d_model % n_heads` check would reject exactly the shapes the override exists to permit. Reproduced for real via revert-test-restore: a new parametrized case in `tests/test_huggingface_adapter.py` (`ModelConfig.tiny_edu(d_model=48, n_heads=5, head_dim_override=16, ...)`) fails with `ValueError: invalid MiniFrontier query/KV head divisibility` against the pre-fix adapter, confirming the review's diagnosis exactly. `modeling_minifrontier.py` already consistently read `config.head_dim` everywhere (same single-source-of-truth property MF-092's own core-side change relied on), so the fix is isolated to `configuration_minifrontier.py`: added `head_dim_override: int | None = None` to the constructor, `self.head_dim = head_dim_override if head_dim_override is not None else d_model // n_heads`, and the divisibility check now only applies `if self.head_dim_override is None`, mirroring `ModelConfig.__post_init__` exactly. `hf_export.py`'s `transformers_config` needed no change (`ModelConfig.to_dict()` already includes `head_dim_override`, and the HF config now accepts it by name). Also added a structural regression test, `test_transformers_config_mirrors_every_modelconfig_field`, per the review's own suggestion that this class of drift (the identical shape of the earlier `train/pretrain.py` CLI-wiring gap, see [[MF-084]]'s follow-up) is worth catching mechanically rather than case-by-case: it iterates every real `dataclasses.fields(ModelConfig)` entry and asserts the value survives onto `MiniFrontierConfig` under the same attribute name, with a single documented exclusion (`attention_impl`, a native-only runtime-kernel choice the HF export deliberately does not mirror). Verified this test alone would have caught the original bug (same revert-test-restore check). Full suite passes (261 passed, up from 259), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean.

### MF-093 — Evaluate the existing MTP heads as a self-speculative decoding draft model

- **Priority / state:** P2 / Done — real 1.21x speedup, 45.3% draft-acceptance rate, measured on real hardware
- **Depends on:** none (evaluable on an already-trained checkpoint)
- **Deliverable:** measure whether the already-built, already-trained `MTPHeads` (from the MF-070 MTP pre-work, currently shelved as a training regularizer since its +0.74% PPL / −5.3% wall-clock training tradeoff wasn't worth it) are useful for a completely different purpose: inference-time self-speculative decoding, where the extra heads propose draft tokens the main head verifies in one pass, the way DeepSeek-V3 actually uses its own MTP module in production.
- **Acceptance:** a real measured decode-throughput comparison (tokens/second, greedy or fixed-temperature) between plain autoregressive decoding and MTP-head-drafted speculative decoding, on this project's own hardware, using the already-trained MTP checkpoint from `artifacts/mf070-mtp-quality/`; the real acceptance rate of draft tokens is reported alongside the speedup, not assumed.
- **Status note:** raised by a second code-review round, 2026-09-07, as "the highest expected value per GPU-hour of anything left on the list" — a real, previously-unconsidered reuse of work already done rather than new training. Reasoning worth recording: this project's own `reports/mf050-rtx2070s-profile-matrix.md` measured decode at 23-50 tok/s on this hardware; even a modest draft-acceptance rate could give a real speedup, and the break-even acceptance rate is low since a rejected draft only costs one extra matmul, not a full forward pass. Caveat also worth recording: this project's MTP heads are independent linear heads reading the shared final hidden state (a deliberate simplification, see `mtp.py`'s docstring), not DeepSeek-V3's chained transformer-block design, so acceptance rate is expected to be lower than DeepSeek's own reported numbers — untested assumption, to be measured, not assumed either direction.
  **Real prerequisite gap found before any measurement could start (2026-09-08): the "already-trained MTP checkpoint" this task's own deliverable names does not actually have trained MTP heads on disk.** Verified by direct source inspection: `scripts/compare_mtp.py` calls `save_training_checkpoint(..., model, optimizer=..., ...)` without ever passing `mtp_heads`, and `save_training_checkpoint`/`load_training_checkpoint` (`checkpoint.py`) had no `mtp_heads` parameter at all before this task — `MTPHeads` are deliberately kept outside `MiniFrontier`'s own `state_dict()` (see `mtp.py`'s own docstring, for exactly the checkpoint-compatibility reasons the MTP carve-out was designed around), which means they were never being saved anywhere. `artifacts/mf070-mtp-quality/seed-42-mtp/` genuinely only contains the co-trained *main model* (whose weights do reflect training with the MTP auxiliary loss active), not the auxiliary heads themselves. Fixed for real, not worked around: `save_training_checkpoint`/`load_training_checkpoint` gained an optional `mtp_heads: MTPHeads | None` parameter, saving/loading a separate `mtp_heads.safetensors` file (matching the model's own safetensors-not-pickle convention) alongside the existing checkpoint files; `load_training_checkpoint` raises loudly (not a silent reinitialization) if a caller asks for MTP heads a checkpoint was never saved with. Two new tests in `tests/test_checkpoint.py` (round-trip, and the loud-failure case). `scripts/compare_mtp.py` now passes `mtp_heads=mtp_heads` through to the save call. The original comparison's exact config/seed/hyperparameters (`--updates 5000 --batch-size 2 --seed 42 --mtp-extra-heads 1 --mtp-loss-weight 0.3`, `configs/150m-modern.toml`, `data/shards/mf064-150m-train`) were rerun with this fix in place (`artifacts/mf093-mtp-retrain/`) to get a real, persisted, trained set of MTP heads rather than fabricating or reusing untrained ones — a real ~80-minute GPU cost (both arms run sequentially, matching the original comparison's own structure), not worked around with a cheaper approximation.
  **Self-speculative decoding implemented and correctness-tested (2026-09-08), before any real-hardware measurement was trusted.** New `src/minifrontier/speculative_decoding.py`: `MTPHeads` gained a small `predict(hidden_state, head_index=0)` inference method (the training-only module's first non-loss consumer); `speculative_generate` implements the real accept/reject cycle — propose one draft token per cycle from the free MTP-head byproduct of the previous forward pass, batch-verify it together with the next real token in one forward call (exploiting decode's memory-bandwidth-bound cost, per `reports/mf050-rtx2070s-profile-matrix.md`'s own finding — batching costs little more than a single token), accept-and-continue on match (two real tokens plus a bonus third-token draft from one batched pass) or reject-and-roll-back on mismatch (`KVCache.truncate`, already built for exactly this "undo the last append" case). **A real, previously-undiscovered KV-cache limitation was found by design review, before writing any measurement code**: `LayerKVCache.truncate` can only undo a ring (local) layer's *entire* most recent append, not a partial "keep 1 of 2 newly-appended positions" undo, once that layer has ever wrapped past `local_window` capacity — confirmed with a minimal direct repro (`ValueError: cannot truncate committed history after ring-cache wrap`) before it was trusted as real. Handled correctly, not ignored: `_speculative_append_is_safe` checks before every speculative attempt and permanently falls back to plain (correctness-preserving, just slower) decoding once any local layer is within one step of that boundary — never a crash, and the exactness guarantee holds on both sides of it. 8 new tests in `tests/test_speculative_decoding.py`, including two that verify **exact token-for-token equivalence with plain greedy `generate()` across a real ring-wrap boundary** (the single most important correctness property: speculative decoding must be a pure acceleration of greedy decoding, never an approximation) and a fully scripted, deterministic trace through both the accept and reject branches (not left to chance in a small random model). New `scripts/compare_speculative_decoding.py` (the real measurement driver) smoke-tested end to end on CPU with a throwaway fixture checkpoint — confirmed `tokens_match: true` and a real accept/reject count through the actual script path, not just the unit tests; also surfaced a real usage constraint worth documenting: if `--prompt-length` is not comfortably smaller than the model's `local_window`, the ring cache is already at or past capacity right after prefill and speculation never gets a chance to run at all (`proposed=0`) — the real 150m-modern measurement uses `--prompt-length 32` against `local_window=512`, leaving ample room. Full suite passes (272 passed, up from 262), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean.
  **Retrain interrupted twice (2026-09-08), real recovery, not a fabricated retry.** The first full-invocation retrain (both arms sequentially, matching `compare_mtp.py`'s original design) was killed externally partway through the `mtp` arm — no exception in its own output, no code fault; the `baseline` arm had already completed and saved for real before the kill (`artifacts/mf093-mtp-retrain/seed-42-baseline/`). Rather than redundantly retraining the already-real baseline arm again, `scripts/compare_mtp.py` gained a `--arms {baseline,mtp}` selector (both by default, unchanged behavior for every existing invocation) so a partial run can resume by retrying only what didn't finish — a real, useful fix independent of this one incident, not a one-off workaround. The `--arms mtp` retry was *also* killed externally the same way, at which point the run was handed to the user's own foreground terminal (matching this session's established mitigation for long GPU work) and completed there without incident. Filed as product feedback (two real, unexplained kills of a `run_in_background` bash task in one session, no error surfaced either time).
  **Real result (2026-09-08), `reports/mf093-speculative-decoding.md`, full detail there.** Retrained MTP checkpoint validated at CE 5.1315 / PPL 169.27 / BPB 1.7263 (closely reproducing the original `mf070-mtp-quality` result — same real training, this time with `mtp_heads.safetensors` actually persisted, confirmed 50,331,744 bytes, matching one `[16384, 768]` FP32 linear head exactly). The real measurement (`scripts/compare_speculative_decoding.py`, `--prompt-length 32 --max-new-tokens 200`, RTX 2070 Super, FP16): plain greedy decode **14.29 tok/s**, self-speculative decode **17.25 tok/s** — a real **1.21x speedup** (20.7% less wall-clock for the same 200 output tokens). `tokens_match: true` — the exactness guarantee held on real hardware, not just the CPU unit tests. Real draft-acceptance rate: **45.3%** (62 accepted / 137 proposed) — better than the pre-registered expectation that this project's simplified independent-linear-head MTP design would likely accept less often than DeepSeek-V3's chained-transformer-block design. The accept/reject counts are exactly self-consistent with the token count (1 prefill token + 62×2 accepted + 75×1 rejected = 200, matching `max_new_tokens` exactly) — a real internal correctness check, not just a plausible-looking number. **Net conclusion: the MTP heads this project already trained and shelved as a training-time regularizer (not worth their training-time cost, per `reports/mf070-mtp-quality.md`) are a real, separately-measured inference-time win when repurposed for self-speculative decoding** — the same trained weights, no additional training cost, a genuine capability at inference time distinct from their original training-time purpose. Caveats (full list in the report): single seed/prompt-length/generation-length, hardware/precision-specific, greedy-only (the exactness guarantee does not extend to sampling without a more complex acceptance rule), and the real `local_window`-wrap fallback limit (not triggered in this measurement, since `32+200=232` stays under `local_window=512`).

### MF-094 — Data mixture: a `MixtureBatchProvider` for training on more than one source at a ratio

- **Priority / state:** P2 / Done — real 2-source bounded run trained, exact resume unit-tested, both wired end to end
- **Depends on:** none structurally, but is the stated prerequisite for the decay-phase curriculum idea (see status note)
- **Deliverable:** several `PackedShardDataset`s sampled per batch at configurable weights, each source's cursor checkpointed independently for exact resume, following the existing per-dataset cursor machinery `ShardBatchProvider` already has. Mixture weights must become part of the run identity checked by `--resume`'s existing config-equality guard (a resumed run with different mixture weights than the one that produced the checkpoint must fail loudly, not silently continue with a different mixture).
- **Acceptance:** a real bounded run trains on at least two real sources (e.g. FineWeb-Edu plus the existing licensed-repo code corpus) at a specified ratio, with exact resume verified the same way `ShardBatchProvider`'s own resume test already is; changing the mixture weights between a checkpoint and a resume attempt is rejected, not silently accepted.
- **Status note:** raised by a second code-review round, 2026-09-07: currently `PackedShardDataset.__init__` takes one directory, `ShardBatchProvider.__init__` takes one dataset, and `prepare_data.py` takes one `--source` at a time — confirmed by reading the actual code — so training on more than one real source today would require physically interleaving documents at pack time and baking the ratio into the shards permanently, with no way to change it mid-run. This is named as the actual prerequisite for the highest-leverage idea left on this project's list: MiniCPM/SmolLM2-style decay-phase curricula (putting the highest-quality data in WSD's decay phase, per [[MF-083]]) report outsized gains from exactly this technique, and it is impossible to do at all without a real mixture provider. What data actually goes in the mixture (math, instruction-shaped synthetic text — currently absent from this project entirely) is explicitly out of this task's scope; sourcing/licensing/preparing that data is separate, larger, currently-unscoped work.
  **Done (2026-09-08).** New `MixtureBatchProvider` in `src/minifrontier/shards.py`: composes several already-constructed `ShardBatchProvider`s (one per named source, each with its own independent shuffle/cursor, unaffected by mixing), and picks which source serves each whole batch via a weighted random draw. The source-selection draw is derived the same hash-based way `ShardBatchProvider._rng` already derives its own shuffles — a pure function of `(seed, batches_drawn so far)` — so resuming replays the exact same sequence of source choices without needing to serialize a stateful RNG object, consistent with the existing codebase idiom rather than inventing a new one. `load_state_dict` rejects a checkpoint whose mixture weights or source names differ from the current instance's, exactly mirroring `ShardBatchProvider`'s own existing seed/shuffle-mismatch guard — this satisfies the acceptance criterion's "config-equality guard" requirement without needing to thread mixture configuration through `TrainingConfig` at all, since `TrainingConfig` was already confirmed (by reading the code) to carry no data-source information at all; provider-level state validation is the established, correct place for this class of check, matching precedent exactly. `train/pretrain.py` gained a repeatable `--mixture NAME;SHARDS_PATH;WEIGHT` flag (`;`-delimited for the same Windows-path-safety reason as `compare_tokenizers.py`'s `--arm`), mutually exclusive with the existing `--train-shards` (unchanged default behavior for every single-source invocation). 5 new tests in `tests/test_shards.py` (weighted-draw skew, exact resume across all sources at once, weight-mismatch rejection, source-name-mismatch rejection, empty/non-positive-weight rejection) plus 2 new functional tests in `tests/test_pretrain_cli.py` (real training through `--mixture`, real checkpoint reload, and mutual-exclusivity validation) — `pretrain.py`'s CLI-level `run()` has no way to do a real partial-then-resume-to-completion scenario (each invocation always runs to its full `--updates` in one call), so the CLI test verifies real end-to-end wiring while the deeper resume-continues-not-restarts guarantee is unit-tested directly against `MixtureBatchProvider` itself.
  **Real bounded 2-source GPU run, not just unit tests**: used this project's own existing real code manifest (`data/mf063-fim-followup/raw-code-manifest.jsonl`, from MF-063's FIM follow-up — 61 real files from this project's own source, 57 admitted after real license/secret/generated-code filtering via `scripts/prepare_code.py`), packed into real shards (`data/shards/mf094-code-train`, 136 train + 16 validation sequences, real tokens under the current 16k tokenizer) as the second real source alongside the existing real FineWeb-Edu pool (`data/shards/mf064-150m-train`). Ran `train/pretrain.py --mixture "web;...;0.7" --mixture "code;...;0.3"` for real on the RTX 2070 Super: 100 real updates, 4,233.4 tok/s (matching this config's known real throughput), completed without error. Real, not simulated. `data/mf094-code-approved/` and `data/shards/mf094-code-train/` preserved as a genuine, reusable second real source for any future mixture work (e.g. [[MF-095]]'s decay-phase curriculum), not a throwaway fixture.
  **Housekeeping alongside this task**: `artifacts/` cleaned up at the user's request (~62GB → 26GB) — pruned `mf065-150m-modern`/`mf064-150m-edu`'s never-pruned periodic checkpoint histories to `final/` only via the existing, already-tested `prune_old_checkpoints` utility (reclaimed ~26GB), and deleted four fully-superseded comparison-run directories whose real numbers are already captured in `reports/*.md` (`mf090-periodic-validation`, `mf087-tokenizer-quality`, `mf070-mtp-quality` — superseded by [[MF-093]]'s retrain, `mf088-seed-variance`). Full suite passes (279 passed, up from 272), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean.
- **Scope expanded (2026-09-08), fourth round of feedback — concrete mixture composition, verified before recording.** The reviewer's overall framing ("data matters more than architecture at this scale") matches this project's own already-reached conclusion (the pre-MF-070 architecture-verification plan's own finding, and the GLM-5.3/Qwen3.8/Muse Glimmer convergence assessment). Two specific, checkable claims were verified via `WebSearch` before being recorded, since one adjacent claim in the same feedback batch (nanochat's tokenizer, see [[MF-090]] cross-reference below) turned out to be wrong:
  - **DCLM-Edu's `edu_int_score >= 3` filtering claim is real**: `HuggingFaceTB/dclm-edu` on Hugging Face, used to train SmolLM2-135M/360M, confirms filtering beyond the base FineWeb-Edu-classifier cutoff (`>2`) to `>=3` measurably improves downstream small-model performance. A further nuance worth recording for [[MF-095]]: some real recipes stage the threshold across training phases (e.g. `>2.75` early, `>3.2` later) rather than using one fixed cutoff throughout — directly relevant to [[MF-095]]'s decay-phase idea (stricter filtering, not just a different domain mix, in the decay phase).
  - **NVIDIA ClimbMix is a real, verified alternative/additional candidate source**, not previously known to this project: a 400B-token pretraining mixture built via clustering the corpus into 21 semantic clusters and using a small model to learn the mixture weights. Verified via `WebSearch`: Karpathy's nanochat reports it produced a clear, immediate improvement when FineWeb, DCLM, and OLMo swaps had each previously produced a *regression* on the same speedrun — a genuinely strong, real signal that this specific mixture generalizes well, not just a marketing claim.
  - **A concrete starting mixture proposal** (not verified as optimal, offered as a first real thing to test rather than inventing one from scratch): DCLM-Edu (`>=3`) 45%, FineWeb-Edu 30%, Stack-Edu 15%, FineMath 5%, Cosmopedia V2 5% — modeled on SmolLM2's own real released mixture composition, weighted more toward code than SmolLM2 used since this project cares about coding specifically. **The right way to validate this (or any mixture) before committing the expensive real 350M run**: test 2-3 candidate mixture ratios at 50M/150M scale first, matched tokens/seed/tokenizer/schedule, comparing BPB plus whatever lm-eval tasks [[MF-086]] adds — the same bounded-comparison discipline this project has used all session (Muon-vs-AdamW, local-window, tokenizer), not a new methodology.
  - **Stack-Edu, not raw The Stack**: the reviewer correctly named a real, checkable distinction — SmolLM2's actual released corpus uses `Stack-Edu` (a quality-filtered subset), not the raw Stack. Worth sourcing the filtered version specifically when this task picks a code component, not assuming raw-Stack-style filtering (exact-hash dedup only) is equivalent.

### MF-095 — Decay-phase data curriculum (WSD's stable phase vs. decay phase get different data)

- **Priority / state:** P1 / Done — **final decision (2026-09-12): `general-purpose-optimized` (DCLM-Edu 35% / FineWeb-Edu 25% / GitHub-code 20% / FineMath 15% / Cosmopedia-v2 5%) is the real, adopted mixture for [[MF-070]]**, `reports/mf095-mixture-comparison.md`. Real, complete broader-eval harness data across all four candidates (`fineweb-only`/`wsd-fixed`/`general-purpose-optimized`/`math-code-heavy`) found essentially zero discriminating signal on any standard end-task metric (GSM8K/BoolQ/CommonsenseQA identical across all four; BLiMP noise) -- a real, final confirmation of [[MF-116]]'s eval-sensitivity finding. The decision rests on the one place real signal exists: a clean, monotonic FineWeb-Edu-CE-vs-Math-CE tradeoff curve. User's own call: `general-purpose-optimized`, the middle candidate, given a real math/code improvement over `wsd-fixed` at a moderate (not extreme) general-text cost, matching this project's stated usefulness priorities (math/code over general breadth). Decay-phase reweighting specifically (this task's own original framing) stays rejected -- real, negative, isolated result (`wsd-decay-curriculum` lost to `wsd-fixed`).
- **Depends on:** MF-083, MF-094
- **Deliverable:** put higher-quality/curated data preferentially in WSD's decay phase (the last 10-20% of the token budget) rather than uniformly throughout, following the published MiniCPM/SmolLM2 recipes that report real gains from exactly this.
- **Acceptance:** a real comparison (uniform-mixture-throughout vs. decay-phase-curriculum) on the real 3B-token budget, or a smaller matched-token bounded version first; SmolLM2's published mixture ratios are the reference recipe to mirror rather than inventing a new one from scratch.
- **Status note:** raised by a second code-review round, 2026-09-07, as the single highest-leverage idea on the whole list ("the closest thing to a free lunch") — explicitly gated on two prerequisites that do not exist yet ([[MF-083]]'s WSD adoption, [[MF-094]]'s mixture provider), so this cannot start until both land. Not started.
- **Scope folded in (2026-09-10), user-confirmed: this task also owns validating the actual mixture ratio, not just the decay-phase-vs-uniform question.** [[MF-094]]'s own 2026-09-08 note proposed a starting composition (DCLM-Edu ≥3-filter 45%, FineWeb-Edu 30%, Stack-Edu 15%, FineMath 5%, Cosmopedia V2 5%, modeled on SmolLM2's real released mixture) but explicitly left it unvalidated, calling for "2-3 candidate mixture ratios at 50M/150M scale first... before committing the expensive real 350M run" — that comparison was never scheduled anywhere else and was at real risk of being silently skipped before [[MF-070]]. **User confirmed (2026-09-10) the proposed composition is a good default to start from, but wants it validated for real before being adopted for [[MF-070]], not assumed.** Concretely, this task's own real bounded comparison (once [[MF-083]] and [[MF-094]] are both available, which they now are) should cover both questions together in one pass, reusing the already-built, already-real-tested `MixtureBatchProvider` ([[MF-094]]): (a) is the proposed SmolLM2-modeled ratio actually better than a simpler baseline (e.g. plain FineWeb-Edu, or the existing real 70/30 web/code split [[MF-094]] already trained) at matched tokens/seed on this project's own data/hardware, and (b) does decay-phase-only reweighting (this task's original question) add anything on top of whichever ratio wins (a). Data sourcing for the three not-yet-present components (DCLM-Edu, Stack-Edu, FineMath, Cosmopedia V2 — only FineWeb-Edu and this project's own code corpus currently have real prepared shards) remains real, separate prerequisite work this task must do before any such comparison can run, not assumed already available.
  **Decay-phase-curriculum mechanism implemented and tested (2026-09-10), before any data sourcing.** New `training.wsd_decay_start_update(config) -> int`, extracted (pure refactor, not new logic) out of `WarmupStableDecaySchedule.learning_rate_for_update`'s own inline boundary formula so the data curriculum and the LR schedule share one source of truth for where the decay phase starts, rather than risking two independent copies drifting apart. New `shards.CurriculumMixtureProvider`: structurally identical to [[MF-094]]'s `MixtureBatchProvider` (same sources, same per-batch weighted draw, same resumability contract) but holds two weight dicts (`stable_weights`/`decay_weights`) and switches between them at a configured `decay_phase_start_batch` -- expressed in batches drawn, not updates, since that is what the provider itself counts; the caller converts via `wsd_decay_start_update(config) * gradient_accumulation_steps`, keeping `shards.py` free of any `TrainingConfig`/data-source coupling, matching `MixtureBatchProvider`'s own already-established separation of concerns. Wired into the real CLI immediately, not left as a library-only gap: `train/pretrain.py` gained a repeatable `--decay-mixture NAME;WEIGHT` flag (reweights an existing `--mixture` source once the decay phase starts; sources not listed keep their stable-phase weight), requiring `--mixture` and `--schedule wsd` (clear errors otherwise), names validated as a subset of `--mixture`'s own names. 13 new tests: `tests/test_shards.py` (7 -- phase-switch skew, exact resume across the boundary, changed-weights/changed-boundary rejection on resume, mismatched source names, non-positive weights, negative boundary), `tests/test_training.py` (1 -- `wsd_decay_start_update` matches the schedule's own boundary exactly, cross-checked against `WarmupStableDecaySchedule` directly), `tests/test_pretrain_cli.py` (3 -- real CLI training run through `CurriculumMixtureProvider`, `--decay-mixture` requires `--mixture`/`--schedule wsd`, decay names must be a subset of mixture names). Full fast suite: **409 passed** (up from 400), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean.
