The stated ceiling still applies exactly as recorded above: distillation fills a small model's existing capacity more efficiently, it does not raise the ceiling set by parameter count and architecture — a well-distilled 350M-1B model can beat generic 3-7B GGUFs on its specific target domain, but not on broad knowledge or open-ended reasoning outside that domain. Proposed pipeline shape for whenever this is picked up: solid base pretrain (already this project's plan) → distillation on teacher-generated synthetic data (explanations, rationales, FIM, corrected trajectories, matching this project's existing domain focus) → focused SFT + preference optimization. No new decision required by this note; it sharpens the case already recorded above for revisiting this seriously once a real 350M checkpoint exists.
- **Candidate teacher models recorded (2026-09-08), explicitly deferred to after [[MF-070]] — a hardware note attached, not independently verified.** Four candidates for generating distillation/synthetic data (not for training — this project never trains the teacher): Qwen3/3.6/3.8 27B-32B dense at Q4_K_M/Q5_K_M GGUF (general-purpose, coding, structured output); DeepSeek-R1-Distill-Qwen-32B at Q4_K_M (long step-by-step reasoning traces, math/formal explanations); Qwen3 30B-A3B or similar MoE variants at Q4_K_M (~3B active params/token, faster bulk generation at comparable quality); Gemma 3/4 27B, Muse Glimmer 30B (if a GGUF exists, notable for agentic/tool-use trajectories specifically), or Phi-4 14B as a smaller fallback. **Real hardware mismatch worth flagging plainly**: every one of these needs roughly 17-22GB of VRAM even at Q4_K_M — this project's own documented training hardware throughout this entire session is an RTX 2070 Super with **8GB** VRAM (see `AGENTS.md`'s own hardware-aware precision guidance, calibrated specifically for that card). Running a teacher model for data generation does not need to happen on the same card MiniFrontier itself trains on — it is a separate, one-time, offline step — but it does mean this project's own 8GB card cannot be the machine that generates the distillation data; a different, more capable machine (rented cloud instance, a separate local machine, or a lower-VRAM fallback like Phi-4 14B if only ~10-12GB is realistically available) would be needed for that step specifically. Not a blocker for recording this list, but worth resolving explicitly (which machine actually generates the data) before this task is picked up for real, since it is not something the project's existing frozen-hardware assumptions already cover.
- **Two more concrete implementation details recorded (2026-09-09), explicitly deferred to after [[MF-070]] — largely restating what's already recorded above, plus two genuinely new specifics worth keeping.** Most of this round's feedback (teacher logit-KL loss with temperature scaling, a three-stage pretrain-then-SFT-then-DPO pipeline, expected capability-by-size table) restates the pipeline shape and RL-adjacency/licensing caveats already recorded above — not repeated here. Two specifics are new and worth keeping for whenever this is actually scoped: (1) **storage engineering for logit distillation**: if full-vocabulary teacher logits were ever stored for offline KL distillation, the resulting I/O would be prohibitive at this project's own token scale — storing only the teacher's top-K (e.g. K=64) logits/token-indices and renormalizing before computing the KL term is the standard mitigation, worth remembering if/when a logit-distillation stage (as opposed to plain text-output SFT-style distillation, this task's own current framing) is ever scoped for real; (2) **hidden-state alignment as a candidate mid-training stage** (projecting the student's hidden state to the teacher's via an MSE loss before the LM head) — a real, distinct technique from both text-output distillation and logit-KL distillation, not previously recorded here, worth naming as a candidate stage if this task is ever scoped in full. Neither changes this task's own scope-decision gate (RL-adjacency, provenance/licensing) or its still-not-started status.
- **A real, concrete precedent for the small-model side specifically, verified (2026-09-10) directly from Qwen3's technical report (arXiv:2505.09388), not assumed.** Qwen's own small dense models (0.6B/1.7B/4B/8B/14B) are not just pretrained and shipped — they go through an explicit **Strong-to-Weak Distillation** stage: off-policy distillation from teacher outputs (both "think"/"no-think" modes combined), then on-policy distillation where the student generates its own sequences and is fine-tuned to align its logits with a 32B or 235B-A22B teacher's via KL divergence. This is real, current, lab-verified confirmation — from a team with effectively unlimited compute, not one that needs the shortcut — that even they treat a dedicated small-model distillation stage as worth doing rather than only scaling down the same big-model recipe. Directly strengthens the case already recorded above; does not change this task's own scope-decision gate.
- **Five more named candidate techniques recorded (2026-09-10), not independently verified, explicitly deferred to after [[MF-070]] like everything else in this task.** External feedback named five specific 2026-era distillation refinements worth keeping as candidates for whenever this task is actually scoped, none acted on now: (1) **confidence-gated / entropy-routed KD** — distill from the teacher only where the teacher itself is confident, fall back to plain next-token CE otherwise, intended to protect factual recall while still transferring reasoning; (2) **on-policy / reverse-KL distillation after an initial off-policy phase** — the same two-phase shape [[MF-093]]'s Qwen3 verification above already confirms as real (off-policy then on-policy KL alignment), not new information but corroborating the sequencing; (3) **curriculum / two-stage reasoning distillation** — build internal competence first, only externalize a written chain-of-thought afterward; (4) **skill/memory distillation** — compact natural-language "skill documents" or hierarchical teacher memory rather than raw output pairs, framed as especially data-efficient for agentic/tool-use domains (this project's own explicit target domain per this task's earlier framing); (5) **mid-training KD** — running distillation earlier than a final-stage-only pass, on the claim that it favors reasoning transfer over pure memorization. None of these five have been checked against a primary source the way the Qwen3/DeepSeek-V4/gpt-oss claims elsewhere in this backlog have been — recorded as named candidates worth a real verification pass whenever this task is actually picked up, not as confirmed facts. Does not change this task's own scope-decision gate or still-not-started status.
- **Real, concrete primary-source findings from TRL's own actual DPO/distillation/reward trainer code (2026-09-17)**, read directly (`reference/trl-main.zip`, unzipped and read, not summarized from memory) specifically to ground this task's own eventual scope in real numbers/constraints rather than secondhand claims -- same discipline as the Qwen3/[[MF-137]]/[[MF-144]] passes above.
  - **Real default hyperparameters**: `DPOTrainer`'s own `DPOConfig` defaults to `beta=0.1`, `learning_rate=1e-6` (66x lower than TRL's own SFT default of `2e-5`, already on record via [[MF-137]]/[[MF-142]]), `loss_type="sigmoid"` -- confirmed in the real code (`dpo_trainer.py`) as the standard Bradley-Terry loss, `-F.logsigmoid(beta * (chosen_logratio - rejected_logratio))`, matching the original DPO paper exactly. Real, concrete starting numbers for whenever this task is scoped for real.
  - **`precompute_ref_log_probs` -- a real, directly relevant VRAM-saving technique given this project's own tight 8GB card.** DPO ordinarily needs a policy model *and* a frozen reference model resident simultaneously; this option precomputes the reference model's log-probs once before training starts, then the reference model no longer needs to stay in GPU memory during the actual training loop -- roughly halving DPO's steady-state VRAM requirement after one upfront pass. A real, concrete answer to a question this task's own scope-decision gate would otherwise have to raise from scratch.
  - **A real, hard blocking constraint for the "distillation" half of this task's scope, found directly in the code, not assumed**: TRL's own `DistillationTrainer` requires `student_vocab_size == teacher_vocab_size`, raising `ValueError` otherwise -- confirmed at `distillation_trainer.py:722-732`. Token-distribution-matching (logit-KL) distillation structurally requires a shared vocabulary. **MiniFrontier's own from-scratch 16,384-token byte-BPE tokenizer will never match any existing real open-weight teacher's vocabulary** (Llama/Qwen/etc. all use different, larger BPE vocabs) -- so plain logit-KL distillation against an off-the-shelf teacher, as this project might naively assume, is not viable as-is. The error message itself names the real, citable alternative: **"GOLD" for cross-tokenizer distillation** -- not implemented in this file, a separate technique that would need its own real research pass before this task assumes logit-matching distillation is even on the table. This does not affect the plain text-output SFT-style distillation this task's own deliverable already describes (using a teacher's *text* outputs as training data, not its logits) -- that path has no vocabulary constraint at all, since it's just ordinary SFT-shaped data from a different source.
  - **TRL's real `DistillationTrainer` is on-policy (GKD-style) by default, not the simpler offline approach this task's own deliverable currently describes** -- a real, distinct axis this task will need to explicitly decide on, not assume. On-policy: the student generates its own completions, and the teacher scores those exact completions (loss is a Generalized Jensen-Shannon Divergence, `beta` interpolating forward-KL at 0.0 to reverse-KL/mode-seeking at 1.0, default `beta=1.0`). Offline (this task's own current framing): a static, once-generated teacher corpus used as ordinary training data. Both are real, named, implemented approaches -- worth deciding explicitly which one this task means, rather than treating "distillation" as one undifferentiated idea.
  - **Independent validation of this project's own [[MF-084]] chunked-loss design, from an unrelated real codebase**: TRL's `_chunked_divergence_loss` (`distillation_trainer.py`) uses the exact same memory pattern MiniFrontier's own chunked cross-entropy already uses -- never materialize full `[B, S, vocab]` logits, process valid positions in chunks, peak memory `2 * chunk_size * vocab` instead of `2 * batch * seq * vocab`. Confirms this is a legitimate, independently-arrived-at real-world pattern, not a one-off design choice.
  - **A third, independent primary source for [[MF-133]]'s logit-soft-capping lead** (after grok-1's raw code and Gemma3's config field, per [[MF-144]]): the same chunked distillation-loss function explicitly supports `student_final_logit_softcapping`/`teacher_final_logit_softcapping` (`softcap * tanh(logits / softcap)`, TRL's own comment calls this "Gemma-style").
  - **Minor context, low priority**: `RewardTrainer`'s own default `learning_rate=1e-4` -- a third distinct LR regime (SFT `2e-5`, DPO `1e-6`, reward-model `1e-4`). Low relevance since this task's own likely real scope (DPO over existing preference pairs) doesn't need a separately-trained reward model at all.
  - **Confirmed real and complete, correctly out of scope for adoption**: `GRPOTrainer`/`RLOOTrainer` both exist as real, complete implementations in TRL -- confirms production RL post-training is genuinely implemented there, but `AGENTS.md` already bans RL/GRPO from MiniFrontier's core; not investigated further, consistent with this task's own already-recorded RL-adjacency framing above (DPO itself is not classically RL, and stays the real candidate; GRPO/RLOO do not).

### MF-099 — A small `plan_run.py`-style throughput/ETA estimator

- **Priority / state:** P3 / Done
- **Depends on:** none
- **Deliverable:** a small CLI that takes a `ModelConfig`, a measured or assumed tokens/second figure, and a target token budget, and prints estimated update count, wall-clock time, and calendar days — replacing the ad hoc inline arithmetic this project's own reports have repeated by hand all session (e.g. "82 days of continuous GPU time," computed manually in `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_DECISIONS.md`'s 3B-token-target reconsideration).
- **Acceptance:** given a config, a `--tokens-per-second` value (or `--from-run-json` to read a real measured value from an existing `run.json`/`trainer_state.json`), and a `--target-tokens` value, the tool prints the same real arithmetic every prior report computed by hand, with no new estimation logic invented (reuses `exact_parameter_count`/existing config plumbing from `scale.py` where relevant).
- **Done (2026-09-09).** New `scripts/plan_run.py`: `estimate_run(target_tokens, tokens_per_second, batch_size, sequence_length, gradient_accumulation_steps)` computes `tokens_per_update = batch_size * sequence_length * gradient_accumulation_steps`, `estimated_updates = ceil(target_tokens / tokens_per_update)`, and wall-clock/calendar-day figures from `tokens_per_second` — the same arithmetic every prior report already computed by hand, no new estimation logic. `--from-run-json` reads a real `tokens_per_second` field from an existing `run.json` (verified: `trainer_state.json` alone has no wall-clock field to derive one from — `run.json` is the file that actually carries it, confirmed by inspecting a real one from this session's own MF-081 arms). `--sequence-length` defaults to the loaded `ModelConfig.max_seq_len`; `parameter_count` (via `exact_parameter_count`, not hand-calculated) is included in the output for reference. 9 new tests in `tests/test_plan_run.py` (arithmetic correctness against hand-computed values, gradient-accumulation accounting, five non-positive-input rejections, real-field/missing-field `--from-run-json` cases). Real CLI smoke against `configs/350m-modern.toml` and this session's real `artifacts/mf081-gqa-sweep/1kv/run.json` (4001.9 tok/s) produces a real, sane estimate (732,422 updates, ~8.68 calendar days for a 3B-token target at that throughput — illustrative only, not this project's real 350M throughput). Full fast suite: 346 passed (up from 337), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean.
- **Status note:** raised by a fourth round of external feedback as a "pure engineering win." Verified before recording: neither `scripts/preflight_scale.py` (hardcoded to the 350m/500m OOM-safety accounting specifically) nor `src/minifrontier/scale.py`'s existing functions provide this general config-agnostic estimate — confirmed by reading both. Genuinely small and low-risk; the only reason it is not marked higher priority is that the arithmetic it replaces is simple enough that manual computation has not actually caused an error yet, per this project's own report history.

### MF-100 — Test a GPT-4-style regex pre-tokenizer against this project's current byte-level BPE pre-tokenization

- **Priority / state:** P1 / Done — raised from P2, user decision (2026-09-11): joined packing/mixture in the real pre-[[MF-070]] validation gate. Real code (`o200k`/`individual` implemented in `train_byte_bpe`, 7 new tests, full suite 450 passed, ruff clean) + real stage-1 fertility check for all 4 candidates, `reports/mf100-fertility-triage.md` (individual-digit splitting failed the fertility gate, +2.88% more tokens, excluded from stage 2; gpt4-style and o200k-style both passed, +0.73%/+0.75% fewer tokens). **Stage 2 (real GPU quality comparison, 2026-09-12), `reports/mf100-stage2-comparison.md`: gpt4-style wins for real** (−0.162% relative BPB, the correct cross-tokenizer-comparable metric, corroborating its own fertility win — a real, small, ~3.5x-noise-floor improvement) and **becomes the Modern-only default pre-tokenizer regex**, per this task's own acceptance criterion. o200k does not win (+0.268% relative BPB, a real regression despite its fertility win — mirrors [[MF-087]]/[[MF-090]]'s own earlier fertility-vs-quality divergence at the vocabulary-size axis). Edu is unaffected (never had this variable). The separately-scoped informational StarCoder2 arm was not run — not required to close this task's own real scope, left as an open, lower-priority curiosity if ever revisited.
- **Depends on:** none (reuses the tokenizer/fertility/comparison tooling already built for [[MF-090]])
- **Deliverable:** train a real tokenizer variant using a fuller GPT-4-style pre-tokenization regex (splitting on contractions, whitespace runs, letter/number/punctuation boundaries all at once — the pattern nanochat's own `rustbpe`/`tiktoken` export uses, verified via `WebSearch` to real-source-code precision, not assumed) at the current frozen 16,384 vocabulary, compared against this project's actual current pre-tokenizer. **Correction (2026-09-09):** this project's pre-tokenizer is not splitting-rule-free — `tokenizer.py` already runs `ByteLevel(add_prefix_space=False, use_regex=True)`, i.e. GPT-2's own regex-based pre-split (contractions, letter runs, digit runs, punctuation runs, whitespace) before the byte-level pass, confirmed by reading the actual code, not assumed from the name. The real, still-untested gap is GPT-2's regex vs. GPT-4's more refined one (e.g. capped-length digit runs, case-insensitive contractions), not "some splitting" vs. "none" as earlier phrasing here implied.
- **Acceptance:** the same two-stage discipline already used for MF-090 — first a real, no-GPU fertility check (`scripts/measure_tokenizer_fertility.py`) on real held-out text to see whether the regex changes bytes/token at all before spending GPU time; only if fertility is neutral-or-better (or if the question is judged worth answering regardless) does a real trained-quality comparison (`scripts/compare_tokenizers.py`, matched tokens/seed/schedule) follow. **Explicit default: stay on plain byte-level BPE with no pre-tokenizer regex unless this test shows a real, measured improvement.** If it does win, **the split-by-preset outcome the user asked for**: the GPT-4-style tokenizer becomes the default for Modern only; Edu keeps the current plain-`ByteLevel` tokenizer as its default. This is a real, tested outcome to adopt, not a change to be made on a plausible-sounding citation.
- **Status note:** raised directly by the user after a fourth round of external feedback's tokenizer claims were checked and found inaccurate (nanochat's vocabulary is 65,536, not "~32K," and it is still byte-level BPE, not a "more sophisticated" algorithm — see the correction note below). The one part of that feedback that *is* a real, distinct, untested idea: nanochat's GPT-4-style regex pre-tokenizer is a genuinely richer pre-tokenization rule than either this project's current GPT-2-style regex pass (see the corrected deliverable note above) or its own (evaluated-and-reverted) digit-only `digit_split` parameter — it is a different lever than vocabulary size, orthogonal to the [[MF-087]]/[[MF-090]] question, and has never been tested here in any form. **A per-preset tokenizer split is a real scope change, flagged, not yet decided.** `AGENTS.md`'s frozen V1 spec currently commits to "one byte-level BPE tokenizer for every model size, so a tokenizer trained once can be compared across experiments" — if Edu and Modern end up on genuinely different tokenizers, that line has to be rewritten and recorded as a decision (per `AGENTS.md`'s own governance), not silently applied via this task's own acceptance criterion. The direct-comparability rationale the single-tokenizer decision was originally built on (raw token-level perplexity comparisons between presets) would need to lean on bits-per-byte instead for any Edu-vs-Modern comparison after a split — which this project already has standing infrastructure for, built specifically for cross-tokenizer comparison during [[MF-087]]/[[MF-090]] (`evaluate_token_batches`'s BPB metric, `scripts/compare_tokenizers.py`), so the mechanism to make a split workable already exists; it just was never previously needed since every preset shared one tokenizer. [[MF-066]]'s existing "canonical teaching comparison" methodology would need this same BPB-not-PPL adjustment if a split is adopted. Not started; low urgency relative to [[MF-093]]/[[MF-094]]/[[MF-081]]/[[MF-082]]/[[MF-086]]/[[MF-083]] since it is a refinement of an already-good-enough tokenizer, not a blocker for anything — the user's own framing ("we will stay BPE if not other cutting edge other real improvement, but should test") matches this task's acceptance criterion exactly: test once, keep the current default unless the test says otherwise, and record the per-preset split explicitly in `AGENTS.md` if and only if the real test supports it.
- **User question answered (2026-09-09): don't switch to an external production tokenizer (StarCoder2/Llama-2/Mistral/SmolLM2) wholesale.** Asked directly, after [[MF-090]]'s external-fertility follow-up (`reports/mf090-external-tokenizer-fertility.md`) showed real fertility losses against StarCoder2 (−8.12%), Llama-2 (−5.37%), and Mistral (−1.75%). Answer: adopting one of those tokenizers wholesale would conflate three variables at once (vocabulary size, training corpus, merge quality), and this project already has a direct, in-house, 3x-reproduced result pointing the other way — [[MF-087]]/[[MF-088]]/[[MF-090]] retrained *this project's own* tokenizer at 32k on *this project's own* corpus, measured it as *more* fertility-efficient than 16k, and it still lost on real trained BPB, with the gap widening rather than closing across the training budget (not a short-budget artifact). Wholesale-adopting e.g. StarCoder2's 49,152-token tokenizer would repeat that same experiment (bigger vocab, better fertility) with an extra uncontrolled confound (foreign corpus/merges) layered on top, while also forcing every existing checkpoint's tied embeddings to be abandoned and adding real embedding-table VRAM cost on this project's already-tight 8GB reference card. [[MF-090]]'s own report already states the governing distinction explicitly: fertility and trained quality are correlated, not identical, and no trained-quality comparison against these external tokenizers was run or planned. The two real, still-open, own-corpus/own-vocab levers worth pursuing instead are this task (GPT-4/nanochat-style regex pre-tokenization) and [[MF-102]] (untied embeddings, input-vocabulary-only scaling) — both already scoped below, both explicitly lower-priority than finishing [[MF-081]]/[[MF-082]]/[[MF-086]]/[[MF-083]] first, per this task's own already-recorded priority ordering above.
- **Stage 1 done for real (2026-09-10), positive result — `reports/mf100-fertility-triage.md`.** `train_byte_bpe` gained `pretokenizer: Literal["gpt2","gpt4"] = "gpt2"` (frozen default unchanged); `"gpt4"` is the real cl100k_base pattern, verified against tiktoken's own primary source (`openai/tiktoken`'s `tiktoken_ext/openai_public.py`, not a secondary summary — a `WebSearch` summary of this exact pattern misquoted the digit clause as `\p{N}{2,}`, caught only by checking the primary source), with PCRE possessive quantifiers ported to plain greedy equivalents since `tokenizers`' own `Regex` silently mis-parses the possessive suffix (verified directly: `\p{N}{1,3}+` failed to cap digit runs at all, `\p{N}{1,3}` correctly capped at 3). Requires `digit_split="none"` (rejected otherwise) — the GPT-4 regex already caps digits itself, and stacking the project's own separate digit rule on top was never tested. `scripts/train_tokenizer.py --pretokenizer` exposes it. 7 new tests in `test_tokenizer.py`.
  **A real backward-compatibility bug was found and fixed along the way, not routed around.** Building the real gpt2-vs-gpt4 comparison tokenizers surfaced that `data/tokenizer` (the current production tokenizer, predating [[MF-103]]'s `<|eot|>`/`<|file_sep|>`/`<|repo_name|>`) failed to load at all once `SPECIAL_TOKENS` grew to 14 — `_validate_special_tokens` required every current-contract token to be present, so any older, smaller-contract tokenizer (including this project's own real production one, and by the same logic every already-published release's bundled tokenizer) would have become permanently unloadable the moment this kind of change ships. Fixed: a token absent from a loaded tokenizer is now tolerated (predates that token, no drift possible); a token present at the wrong ID is still rejected unconditionally, unchanged. 2 new regression tests. Full fast suite: 369 passed (up from 359), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean.
  **Real fertility result**: two tokenizers trained on the identical real corpus (`data/tokenizer-corpus.jsonl`), same 16,384 vocab, same 14-token contract, differing only in pre-tokenization regex — `gpt4-14tok` measured 433,036 tokens vs `gpt2-14tok`'s 436,231 on the same 1,869,374-byte real held-out sample (`data/shards/mf064-150m-train/validation`, decoded via `data/tokenizer`): **+0.74% more fertility-efficient**, a real positive result, not merely neutral. Per this task's own acceptance criterion ("only if fertility is neutral-or-better... does a real trained-quality comparison follow"), **stage 2 (a real bounded matched-token GPU comparison) is now warranted** — not run in this pass, queued behind the GPU arms already ahead of it ([[MF-101]]/[[MF-083]]/[[MF-095]]/[[MF-097]]).
  **Research pass (2026-09-10), user-requested: what can be learned from StarCoder2's/Llama's actual tokenizer source, without adopting either wholesale.** Verified directly against StarCoder2's real `tokenizer.json` (`bigcode/starcoder2-15b` on HuggingFace) and its paper (arXiv:2402.19173): its real pre-tokenizer is `Sequence([Digits(individual_digits=True), ByteLevel(...)])` — GPT-2-style ByteLevel (not GPT-4/cl100k), preceded by a digit-splitter that isolates **every single digit**, not grouped runs. This is a genuinely different, untested granularity from [[MF-087]]'s own reverted digit-splitting experiment (`Split(Regex(r"\d{1,3}"), behavior="isolated")`, grouped up to 3 digits) — `individual_digits=True` is a stock class already in the same `tokenizers` library this project already depends on (`tokenizers.pre_tokenizers.Digits`), no new library needed. Llama/Llama-2's real tokenizer (`meta-llama/llama` GitHub, SentencePiece-based) also splits digits individually via a training-time flag, plus byte-fallback for rare code points — the latter has no analog worth adopting here, since pure byte-level BPE already covers all 256 byte values by construction; SentencePiece's fallback solves a problem this project's tokenizer design doesn't have. **Real caveat surfaced, worth weighing before reading too much into any fertility gap**: StarCoder2's tokenizer was trained on code-specific Stack-v1 data, while [[MF-090]]'s fertility comparison ran against this project's general-prose validation sample — part of the measured −8.12% gap could be domain mismatch, not purely a technique gap, tempering how much any regex/digit change alone could realistically close. **Net, real candidate for this task's own scope**: an isolated single-digit-splitting arm (`Digits(individual_digits=True)`, own 16k vocab/corpus, no other change) is a genuinely new, cheap, own-corpus-isolated test worth adding alongside the gpt2-vs-gpt4-regex arms already planned — MF-087's own status note already left individual-digit splitting's real effect untested (it only tested grouped ≤3-digit runs). Not implemented or scheduled yet, recorded as a candidate stage-2 addition.
  **Wider survey (2026-09-10), user-requested: Qwen2.5, DeepSeek-V3, GPT-4o/o200k_base, Gemma 2/3, Mistral Tekken, SmolLM2.** Two more independent, primary-source-verified confirmations that individual-digit splitting is a broad industry convention, not an isolated choice: **Mistral Tekken**'s real regex uses bare `\p{N}` (one digit at a time) rather than `\p{N}{1,3}` (secondary-source-triangulated, not a fetched primary file); **SmolLM2**'s real `tokenizer.json` (fetched from HuggingFace) uses the exact same `Sequence([Digits(individual_digits=true), ByteLevel(...)])` combination as StarCoder2 — notable since SmolLM2 targets a similarly small model-size regime to this project. That makes four independent real-source confirmations total (StarCoder2, Llama, Tekken, SmolLM2) of the same digit-granularity convention already recorded above. **A second, genuinely new candidate found**: **GPT-4o's real o200k_base pattern** (fetched directly from `tiktoken`'s own `tiktoken_ext/openai_public.py`) differs from cl100k_base (which this project's own gpt4-style pretokenizer already replicates) in a real, verifiable way — explicit case-transition splitting (separate alternatives for an uppercase/titlecase run followed by lowercase and vice versa, e.g. `[\p{Lu}\p{Lt}\p{Lm}\p{Lo}\p{M}]*[\p{Ll}...]+`) instead of cl100k's single undifferentiated `\p{L}++` clause — a real, more refined rule for camelCase/PascalCase-style word boundaries, directly portable into the same `Split(Regex(...), "isolated")` step `tokenizer.py` already has (just a different regex string), not a vocab-size effect. Qwen2.5's real tokenizer.json turned out to be a plain, uncapped-digit GPT-2-style regex with nothing new; DeepSeek-V3's real tokenizer.json could not be fetched (too large, truncated twice) so its "merge punctuation+linebreak for multilingual compression" claim stays secondary-source-only and of unclear portability; Gemma's real config is gated (401) and is SentencePiece Unigram regardless, a different algorithm family, not portable into this project's BPE pipeline. **Net verdict across both research passes**: two concrete, primary-source-verified, `tokenizers`-library-portable candidates worth a real isolated fertility check before any GPU time — (1) individual-digit splitting and (2) the o200k_base case-transition regex — nothing else surveyed offered a new, verified, portable technique beyond these two. Neither implemented or scheduled yet.
  **Stage-2 scope decided (2026-09-10): both candidates join this task's own GPU comparison, not just the fertility check.** This task's stage 2 (real bounded matched-token GPU comparison) now covers four tokenizer variants against the current gpt2-style baseline, all on this project's own frozen 16,384 vocab/corpus (own-corpus isolation preserved throughout): (a) current gpt2-style regex [baseline], (b) gpt4-style/cl100k-style regex [already planned], (c) individual-digit splitting (`Digits(individual_digits=True)`, stacked with the current or gpt4-style regex -- exact stacking choice left to implementation time), (d) o200k_base's case-transition regex. Per this task's own acceptance criterion, each new variant still needs its own real stage-1 fertility check first (`scripts/measure_tokenizer_fertility.py`) before earning a GPU slot -- (c) and (d) are recorded as candidates here, not yet fertility-checked. Not started; queued in its existing position behind [[MF-101]]/[[MF-083]]/[[MF-095]]/[[MF-097]].
  **Stage-2 scope narrowed by real evidence (2026-09-11), `reports/mf100-fertility-triage.md`.** (c) and (d) both got their own real stage-1 fertility check, all four candidates measured together in one run on the identical corpus/vocab/contract. Result: (c) individual-digit splitting is a real, measured fertility **cost** (+2.88% more tokens vs. the gpt2 baseline, not neutral-or-better) and is excluded from stage 2 per this task's own acceptance rule -- it stays implemented and tested (`tokenizer.py`, `train_byte_bpe(digit_split="individual")`, 7 new tests) as an available-but-not-recommended option, same status as the two earlier grouped-digit-split modes MF-090 evaluated and didn't adopt. (d) o200k_base passes, marginally ahead of gpt4-style even (+0.75% vs. +0.73% fewer tokens). **Stage 2 is therefore three real arms, not four**: (a) gpt2 baseline, (b) gpt4-style, (d) o200k-style -- plus the separately-scoped informational StarCoder2 arm below. Not run yet; queued behind [[MF-095]]'s broader-eval follow-up.
  **Scope addition (2026-09-10), user-proposed: a third stage-2 arm using StarCoder2's actual real tokenizer, to settle the "should we ever switch externally" question with real trained-quality evidence rather than fertility alone.** [[MF-090]]'s real fertility loss against StarCoder2 (−8.12%) was measured only against this project's older gpt2-style tokenizer; since gpt4-style is only +0.74% better, the gap to StarCoder2 is almost certainly still large (extrapolated, not independently measured) — worth a real, direct, matched-wall-clock BPB check rather than continuing to reason from fertility deltas alone, given the real cost that a genuine adoption would carry (see below). **Corrected premise, worth recording plainly: adopting StarCoder2's tokenizer does not itself require abandoning tied embeddings** — vocab size and embedding-tying are independent axes; a 49,152-vocab model can still use tied embeddings (just a larger shared table, real but modest extra VRAM, no architecture change). What a real vocab-size change *does* cost is every existing checkpoint's compatibility (same class of migration cost [[MF-087]]'s 32k revert already paid once) — untying itself is [[MF-102]]'s own separate, independently-gated question, not a forced consequence of this one. **Design constraint carried over from [[MF-090]]'s own already-recorded reasoning**: a direct "our tokenizer vs. StarCoder2's real tokenizer" arm confounds three variables at once (vocab size, training corpus, merge quality) — the exact reason [[MF-090]]/this task chose to isolate variables on this project's own corpus instead. **Resolution: add it as a third stage-2 arm, but explicitly labeled as a confounded/informational data point, not a clean causal test** — (a) current gpt2-style/16k/tied [existing baseline], (b) gpt4-style/16k/tied [this task's own planned arm], (c) StarCoder2's real tokenizer/49,152-vocab, embeddings still tied, matched wall-clock, real BPB. If (c) doesn't meaningfully beat (b), the question is closed with no further action. If it does, that becomes the real, first piece of trained-quality (not fertility-only) evidence that would make pursuing [[MF-102]]'s untied-embeddings work, or a real per-preset external-tokenizer adoption decision, worth its own real cost — neither adopted here, just the arm added to this task's own stage-2 scope.
  **SentencePiece-the-algorithm checked (2026-09-12), partially, honestly not fully isolated.** Llama-2's real SentencePiece BPE tokenizer already measured 5.37% more fertility-efficient than ours ([[MF-090]]'s external comparison above) -- but that number is confounded with vocab size (32,000 vs. our 16,384) exactly like every other external comparison this project has made, never isolated to "is SentencePiece's own algorithm better." What's real and checkable independent of that confound: SentencePiece's core BPE merge algorithm is the same frequency-based greedy merging ours does -- its real, structural differences are default NFKC Unicode normalization (a lossy transform) and native whitespace-as-symbol handling, not a smarter merge method. MiniFrontier's pure byte-level BPE has *exact* round-trip decode by construction, already verified (`tests/test_tokenizer.py`'s Hebrew/Japanese/code round-trip cases) -- a real, structural advantage on that one specific axis, not measured against a confounded number. Not treated as a real gap; no bounded test scoped, since isolating this properly would need a same-vocab/same-corpus SentencePiece-vs-`tokenizers`-library comparison nobody has built.
  **Final follow-up closed for real (2026-09-13), `reports/mf100-gpt4-digitsplit-comparison.md`: no digit-split mode beats the adopted gpt4-style-without-digit-split default.** After gpt4-style regex was adopted (above), the remaining open question was whether stacking digit-splitting on top of it helps further -- a real 4-arm wall-clock-matched GPU comparison (gpt4-baseline vs. gpt4-nolead vs. gpt4-leadspace vs. gpt4-individual, all at the frozen 16,384 vocab) answered it: all three digit-split modes are real BPB *losses* relative to the no-digit-split baseline (nolead +0.517%, leadspace +0.466%, individual +1.691% -- individual clearly the worst, consistent with its already-known fertility cost from stage 1). All three losses are well above this project's own measured single-config noise floor (~0.046-0.2%). **`digit_split="none"` stays the adopted default for Modern's gpt4-style tokenizer, unchanged** -- this closes the tokenizer regex/digit-split investigation this session's own pre-[[MF-070]] gate was waiting on before [[MF-108]]/[[MF-102]]/[[MF-119]]/[[MF-118]] could proceed.
  **A fifth round of external feedback checked (2026-09-12), mostly not actionable.** Two batches claiming StarCoder2 is beaten by o200k_base/Qwen3/Llama-4/DeepSeek-V3/Gemma's larger vocabularies (256k for Gemma), then a follow-up naming DeepSeek-Coder-V1/a "custom tiktoken profile"/StarCoder2/"custom SentencePiece with pinned whitespace tokens" as closer philosophical matches to this project's own approach. Two real vocab-size figures were independently verified (Qwen3: 151,936 via its real `config.json`; o200k_base: 200,000, sourced) -- the underlying causal narrative was not: the same feedback pattern already debunked twice this session (SmolLM2 vs. StarCoder2 at the identical 49,152 vocab, and today's own individual-digit-splitting isolation test) directly contradicts "bigger vocab wins," and several of the specific claims (DeepSeek-Coder-V1 "explicitly praised," CodeLlama "modified byte-pair merge priorities") are vague/unsourced and don't match documented reality (CodeLlama reuses Llama 2's unmodified SentencePiece BPE on a code-heavy corpus, not a novel merge mechanism). **A real, decisive, independent reason none of the large-vocabulary options apply regardless of the fertility question**: with this project's tied embeddings, any of these vocab sizes would make the embedding table alone bigger than (o200k, Gemma) or comparable to (Qwen3) MiniFrontier's *entire* current 150M parameter budget. One piece of this feedback *was* worth checking rather than dismissing outright -- see [[MF-119]] above, found by verifying it directly rather than trusting the claim.

### MF-101 — Sweep real batch-size / gradient-accumulation to check for unclaimed training throughput (MFU)

- **Priority / state:** P1 / Done
- **Depends on:** none ([[MF-084]]'s chunked cross-entropy already removed the memory pressure that made a larger batch risky)
- **Deliverable:** a real, bounded sweep of `train/pretrain.py --batch-size ∈ {2, 4, 8} × --accumulation-steps ∈ {1, 8, 32}` (already-existing, already-tested CLI flags — `--accumulation-steps` has never been exercised in any real run this project has recorded), ~200 updates each, recording real tokens/second and peak VRAM per combination on `150m-modern.toml`.
- **Acceptance:** the sweep reports real measured tok/s and peak VRAM for every combination, not an estimate; if a larger effective batch (batch × accumulation) measurably increases tokens/second at the same wall-clock cost, that combination becomes the new default for [[MF-070]]'s eventual 350M run and the real 3B-token release run, recorded as a decision; if it does not, the current `batch_size=2, accumulation_steps=1` default is confirmed rather than assumed adequate.
- **Status note:** raised by a fourth round of external feedback, with a specific and largely verified argument. Confirmed by direct calculation from this project's own already-recorded real numbers (`reports/mf088-seed-variance.md`): 3,700 updates over 7,570,200 tokens = exactly 2,046 tokens per optimizer update (`batch_size=2 × sequence_length=1024`), a real, small effective batch by any published small-model convention (commonly-cited references put 125M-scale pretraining batches around 0.25M-2M tokens). The feedback's derived "~14% MFU" figure used a claimed RTX 2070 Super peak of 28.7 TFLOPS (FP16, tensor cores) that could **not** be independently confirmed: a `WebSearch` check found this card's commonly-cited FP16 figure is **18.1 TFLOPS** — which may itself describe doubled-FP32 shader throughput rather than true tensor-core matmul throughput, so neither number should be trusted as the peer FLOPS ceiling without a real measurement. Recomputing the reviewer's own "925 MFLOP/token × 4,206 tok/s = 3.89 TFLOP/s achieved" arithmetic (independently verified as internally consistent) against the two candidate peaks gives **21.5% MFU at 18.1 TFLOPS** versus the reviewer's claimed **13.6% at 28.7 TFLOPS** — a real, material difference in how much headroom is actually being left on the table (roughly 1.6-2.1x realistic upside at 18.1 TFLOPS' "35-45% MFU" band, not the reviewer's claimed 2.5-3x). The qualitative claim — a starved batch size plus never-exercised gradient accumulation is a real, plausible, and *cheap to check* candidate for real unclaimed throughput — does not depend on resolving the exact peak-FLOPS number, which is why this task's acceptance criterion is a real measured sweep, not a theoretical estimate either party's math should be trusted for. `learning_rate`/`warmup_updates` retuning (the reviewer's own two smaller, correctly-sequenced follow-on points — LR must move with batch size, and `warmup_updates=100` is ~0.02% of a real 3B-token run versus the standard 1-2%/~2,000-step convention) is explicitly deferred until after this sweep settles the batch-size question, not run in parallel with it. Not started; the reviewer's own framing ("under an hour... the highest-value hour left in the project") is plausible given how cheap [[MF-096]]'s comparable attention-impl sweep turned out to be in practice, but not independently re-verified here.
- **Done (2026-09-10), `reports/mf101-batch-size-sweep.md`: real result, "under an hour" turned out wrong for the full 3x3 grid, but the actual answer arrived from 5 completed arms plus one decisive failure.** Built `scripts/sweep_batch_size.py`, deriving per-arm update counts from a fixed ~400K-token budget rather than a uniform raw update count (`accumulation_steps` multiplies real compute per update up to 128x across this grid — verified directly after an initial uniform-count design stalled for 20+ minutes on the most expensive combination). Real results on `150m-modern.toml`, RTX 2070 Super, FP16, seed 42: `batch_size=2` is healthy at every accumulation level (5,159-5,662 MB peak, well under the 8GB card), with tokens/second actually *rising* slightly as accumulation grows (3,997 → 4,148 → 4,374 tok/s). **`batch_size=4` collapses into real VRAM thrashing**: peak memory jumps to 8,308-8,789 MB (at/over the physical ceiling), throughput falls to 643.3 tok/s (accum=1, an 84% drop) and 412.6 tok/s (accum=8, a 90% drop) — and `batch_size=4, accumulation_steps=32` **never completed a single logged update in 49 real minutes** before being killed, the worst-case combination apparently pushing far enough into thrashing that even a 10-update floor couldn't finish. `batch_size=8` was never attempted — a reasoned call, not an oversight: batch_size=4 already exceeds the physical VRAM ceiling and its worst combination failed to complete at all, so batch_size=8 (double the memory pressure again) would predictably be at least as bad for no plausible new information. Confirms this project's own already-documented hardware finding (`AGENTS.md`'s home-GPU notes: "8GB ceiling silently pages rather than OOMs") at a scale (real training arms, not a synthetic probe) it had not previously been measured at.
  **Decision for [[MF-070]]: `batch_size=2` confirmed as the right default** — not because no larger effective batch would help in principle, but because this reference card physically cannot hold `batch_size=4`+ at this model size without catastrophic thrashing. Within the healthy batch_size=2 arms, higher `accumulation_steps` (8 or 32) gives a real, free throughput win (+3.8%/+9.4%, identical peak memory) — worth adopting for [[MF-070]]'s eventual 350M run, though that run's own real VRAM headroom at 350M scale (larger activations) is untested here and could behave differently. `learning_rate`/`warmup_updates` retuning stays explicitly deferred, unchanged from the original framing above.
  **A real operational lesson from this same task, worth recording**: the first live-monitoring pass of the resumed sweep mistakenly killed a genuinely-progressing arm (`b4-a1`) after misreading `nvidia-smi`'s compute-apps listing (a real GPU-active process was present but easy to miss among many system-process rows with `[Insufficient Permissions]`/`[N/A]` memory fields) — recovered by adding a skip-already-completed-arm check to the sweep script and resuming rather than redoing the 3 already-real arms. The second, later "is this actually stalled" call (on `b4-a32`, ~49 minutes with the same PID alive and zero progress) used a fixed time threshold agreed with the user in advance and turned out correct — the process was genuinely never going to finish. Full fast suite unaffected by this task (no source changes, only `scripts/sweep_batch_size.py` and its own tests, already recorded as passing).
  **Two real, precise gaps found in this task's own methodology (2026-09-14), external review, both independently verified against actual code/config rather than trusted:**
  - **Gap 1, confirmed by direct code read: `scripts/sweep_batch_size.py`'s own `run_arm` never passes `--loss-chunk-size` or `--activation-checkpointing`** — its real, complete `subprocess.run` argument list was read directly; neither flag appears anywhere in it. [[MF-084]] shipped chunked cross-entropy specifically to remove the `[B, S, vocab_size]` logits tensor (and its FP32-upcast gradient) from peak VRAM, and it has never actually been exercised in any real VRAM measurement this project has made, including this task's own decisive `batch_size=4` rejection. Rough real order-of-magnitude estimate for what that tensor alone costs at `batch=4, seq=1024, vocab_size=16384`: ~268 MB FP32 logits + ~268 MB gradient + ~134 MB from the FP16 autocast copy ≈ **670 MB** (not independently re-derived byte-for-byte here, but the right order of magnitude for a `4×1024×16384×4-byte` tensor). Subtracting that from the real measured `batch_size=4` peak (8,308 MB) lands close to 7.6 GB — comparable to, not obviously above, the level `head_dim=96` already runs at successfully. **This means "batch_size=4 physically exceeds this card" is a real, correctly-measured conclusion for the exact configuration tested (no chunked loss), but remains untested for the configuration this project actually ships (`--loss-chunk-size` available and unused).** A real, cheap, not-yet-run follow-up: re-run the `batch_size=4` arm(s) with `--loss-chunk-size` set and see whether it survives.
  - **Gap 2, confirmed by direct inspection: every VRAM number behind every frozen-preset decision this session (this task's own sweep, [[MF-108]]'s `head_dim=96` adoption) was measured at `sequence_length=1024`, while every frozen preset declares `max_seq_len=2048`.** Directly relevant, not theoretical: MF-070's own scale-check probe (below) initially made this exact real mistake before being caught and corrected. Activation memory scales roughly linearly with sequence length for the local/windowed attention layers, and more than linearly for global/full-attention layers' own score matrix -- so a config measured comfortably at 1024 is not thereby confirmed safe at the 2048 it is actually declared to run at, and the failure mode if it is not (this project's own documented "silently pages rather than OOMs" behavior) would not surface as a crash, it would surface as a multi-day run quietly proceeding at a fraction of expected speed. [[MF-070]]'s own corrected scale-check probe (below) is the first real measurement in this project actually taken at the declared `2048`, not `1024` -- worth revisiting this task's own `batch_size=4` rejection, and [[MF-108]]'s own `head_dim=96` VRAM numbers, against a real 2048-context measurement once one exists, rather than assuming either transfers unchanged from the 1024 figures already on record.

### MF-102 — Untied embeddings plus input-vocabulary-only scaling as a real explanation and fix for MF-090's regression

- **Priority / state:** P1 / Done (reverted, 2026-09-13) — see the final status note at the end of this entry for the full outcome. Originally: raised from P2, user decision (2026-09-12), joined the pre-[[MF-070]] gate, and had to run **before** [[MF-119]]'s tokenizer regeneration, not after. Real reason, not just ordering preference: if this task's own real test found a larger *input* vocabulary genuinely helps, [[MF-119]]'s regeneration should build the real production tokenizer at that larger size directly -- resolving this first would have avoided regenerating the tokenizer twice.
- **Depends on:** none structurally, but directly follows up [[MF-087]]/[[MF-090]]'s already-closed tokenizer-revert decision
- **Deliverable:** test whether MF-090's real, reproduced 32k-vocabulary BPB regression (see [[MF-087]]'s final resolution) was actually a *tied-embeddings* artifact rather than a vocabulary-size artifact: retrain with `tie_embeddings=false` and a larger *input* vocabulary/embedding table while keeping the *output* (`lm_head`) vocabulary at the current frozen 16,384, per the Over-Tokenized Transformer recipe (arXiv:2501.16975, ICML 2025).
- **Acceptance:** a real bounded comparison (matched tokens/seed/schedule, same methodology as every other `compare_tokenizers.py`-style test this project has run) between (a) the current tied 16,384 baseline, (b) untied with both sides at 16,384 (isolates the pure cost/benefit of untying alone, at zero vocabulary change), and (c) untied with a larger input vocabulary (e.g. 32,768 or larger) but output held at 16,384 (the paper's actual proposed configuration). If (c) beats (a) on held-out BPB, this is a real, adopted architecture change requiring an `AGENTS.md` decision record (tied embeddings is currently a frozen default, `ModelConfig.tie_embeddings: bool = True`); if not, MF-090's regression is confirmed as a vocabulary-size effect independent of tying, closing this question.
- **Status note:** raised by a fourth round of external feedback, and the paper's actual claims were verified via `WebSearch` before recording (not merely trusted from the citation): "scaling up input vocabulary solely keeps improving models, while larger output vocabulary may be harmful to smaller models... expanding the input vocabulary incurs almost no additional computational cost, whereas expanding the output vocabulary significantly increases the training overhead for smaller models" — an accurate, ICML-2025-accepted, real paper (confirmed via arXiv/HuggingFace/OpenReview listings), not a fabricated or misremembered citation. The mechanism argument is real and specific: this project's `tie_embeddings=true` forces the token-embedding table and `lm_head` to be the exact same tensor, so MF-090's 16k-vs-32k comparison could never distinguish "the larger vocabulary itself is the problem" from "the larger *output* vocabulary specifically overwhelms a 150M-parameter model's classification capacity, independent of the input side" — precisely the failure mode this paper's own ablations describe. This is a genuinely new, well-supported candidate explanation for a result this project already has real data on but no verified mechanism for (MF-087's fertility triage ruled out digit-splitting and raw tokenization inefficiency; this proposes a third, more specific, better-evidenced mechanism than the previously-recorded Zipf/short-budget-artifact hypothesis, which [[MF-090]]'s own periodic-validation curve already argued against). Not started; a real architecture change if it wins, not merely a documentation note like the SuperBPE item below.
- **Cross-referenced (2026-09-09) from the same "should we switch tokenizers" question answered on [[MF-100]]**: this task, alongside [[MF-100]], is the recorded reason a wholesale switch to an external production tokenizer (StarCoder2/Llama-2/Mistral/SmolLM2) is not adopted despite [[MF-090]]'s real fertility losses against all three — see [[MF-100]]'s dated note for the full reasoning. Both stay queued behind [[MF-081]]/[[MF-082]]/[[MF-086]]/[[MF-083]].
- **Real correction (2026-09-13), found before any code was written: this task's own framing does not match what the cited paper actually does.** Fetched the real method section (`arxiv.org/html/2501.16975v1`, quoted directly, not paraphrased) rather than trust the secondhand "untie embeddings, bigger input vocab, output stays 16,384" summary this task was originally recorded from. The paper's real mechanism: the base unigram embedding/`lm_head` pair is untouched and **stays tied** ("implemented consistently with the original Transformer to align with the tied weight design") -- what actually scales is a **separate, additive, hashed n-gram embedding table**: at each position, the current token plus the preceding `n-1` tokens are combined via `f(z_1,...,z_n) = sum(z_i * p^(i-1))` (p >= vocab_size), reduced modulo a hash-table size `m`, and looked up in that order's own small table, summed into the input embedding. There is no bigger tokenizer and no untying involved in the paper's own input-scaling side at all.
- **Both the real mechanism and the originally-described (simpler, not-what-the-paper-does) idea are now implemented and tested, per explicit user direction to compare both rather than pick one blind.** `ModelConfig` gained two independent, Modern-only-gated, off-by-default fields: `input_vocab_size` (decouples the input embedding row count from `vocab_size`, forcing `tie_embeddings=False` when different -- the originally-described idea) and `ngram_embedding_orders`/`ngram_hash_vocab_size` (the real paper mechanism, additive hashed n-gram tables, base embedding untouched). `MiniFrontier` gained a `_ngram_embeddings` method (hash-formula verified by hand-checked unit test against the paper's own equation) and an `ngram_tables` `nn.ModuleList`, summed into the input embedding in `forward`. 22 new tests across `tests/test_config.py`/`tests/test_model.py` (validation combinations, Edu-rejection, exact hash values, a real forward-output-changes check, prefill-vs-full-sequence-forward parity). `pytest tests/`: 507 passed (up from 486).
  - **A real, disclosed scope limit, not a silent gap**: `ngram_embedding_orders` does not yet support incremental cached decode (only full-sequence training/prefill) -- computing a position's hash needs its real preceding tokens, which for a continuation call live before that call's own `tokens` tensor, not tracked anywhere today. `forward` raises `NotImplementedError` loudly on that combination rather than hashing against sentinel padding standing in for history it doesn't have; verified by a real test that prefill (`start_pos=0`) works and continuation (`start_pos>0`) raises.
  - **HF-adapter mirroring deliberately deferred, not forgotten**: unlike [[MF-081]]/[[MF-082]]'s items (mirrored into the HF adapter as part of the same implementation pass), these two fields are NOT yet mirrored into `configuration_minifrontier.py`/`modeling_minifrontier.py`. Checked this is safe, not silently wrong: `MiniFrontierConfig.__init__` has an explicit parameter list (not a blind passthrough), and any attempt to actually export/reload a checkpoint trained with either field would hit a real shape mismatch or unexpected/missing state-dict keys -- HF's own `from_pretrained` fails loudly on both, not silently. Deferred because nobody will export a throwaway bounded-ablation checkpoint via the HF adapter, and building full parity for a mechanism that may not survive its own bounded test would be premature.
  - **The "originally described" arm turned out to have its own unresolved data-pipeline gap, found before building its scratch config**: if `input_vocab_size=32768` but training data is still tokenized with the existing 16,384-vocab tokenizer, every real token ID stays below 16,384 -- the extra embedding rows would never be indexed or trained, testing nothing. Making it meaningful needs an actual 32k-vocabulary tokenizer feeding real input IDs into that range, but then the prediction *target* also comes from that same 32k stream while `lm_head` only has 16,384 output classes -- no defined reconciliation exists (would need an invented remapping scheme). **User decision (2026-09-13): drop this arm rather than invent a remapping scheme** -- keep only the real, well-defined comparison: tied 16k baseline vs. untied-same-16k (isolates untying's own cost, no vocab change) vs. bigram-hash vs. bigram+trigram-hash.
  - **Real, disclosed parameter deltas computed for the three ready arms** (matching [[MF-108]]'s own "disclosed cost, not matched-parameter" discipline), against the real 138,630,640-parameter `150m-modern.toml` baseline: untied-same-vocab and bigram-only both add exactly **+12,582,912 (+9.077%)** -- an extra 16,384x768 table either way, coincidentally identical magnitude; bigram+trigram adds **+25,165,824 (+18.153%)**, two tables. Three ready-to-run scratch configs, same recipe as every other queued [[MF-081]]-class arm (`data/shards/mf064-150m-train`, seed 42, batch=2, 5,000 updates), queued for **after [[MF-108]]'s own GPU arm** (explicit user instruction, 2026-09-13: code now, run later, don't contend for the GPU):
    ```
    ./.venv/Scripts/python.exe train/pretrain.py --config configs/scratch-mf102-untied-same-vocab.toml --train-shards data/shards/mf064-150m-train/train --output artifacts/mf102-untied-same-vocab --updates 5000 --batch-size 2 --seed 42 --device cuda
    ./.venv/Scripts/python.exe train/pretrain.py --config configs/scratch-mf102-ngram-bigram.toml --train-shards data/shards/mf064-150m-train/train --output artifacts/mf102-ngram-bigram --updates 5000 --batch-size 2 --seed 42 --device cuda
    ./.venv/Scripts/python.exe train/pretrain.py --config configs/scratch-mf102-ngram-bigram-trigram.toml --train-shards data/shards/mf064-150m-train/train --output artifacts/mf102-ngram-bigram-trigram --updates 5000 --batch-size 2 --seed 42 --device cuda
    ```
    Baseline for comparison is the same shared unmodified `150m-modern.toml` run already used by [[MF-081]]/[[MF-082]]/[[MF-108]] -- no need to retrain it. Evaluate each via `scripts/eval_checkpoint.py` against `data/shards/mf064-150m-train/validation`, same recipe as every other bounded comparison this session.
  - **Real user caution recorded (2026-09-13), a stricter bar than a plain win/loss result**: "after the minimal small test for results, i think we should revert it, only that ngram/embedding change we did... it touched too many parameters/places in code, i thought its an isolated feature, the previous way was more readable" -- **clarified immediately after: "i meant also the untie embedding, reverting the mf-102 task" / "after the test."** The plan, as given: run a small bounded test first, then revert this whole task's new code -- both `input_vocab_size` and `ngram_embedding_orders`/`ngram_hash_vocab_size` in `config.py`/`model.py`, plus their tests -- regardless of whether either shows a small win, given the real code-footprint cost across both features. A [[feedback_experimental_feature_footprint]]-class memory was saved recording this as a durable preference for future bounded-ablation features in this codebase.
  - **Small CPU smoke test run for real (2026-09-13), then reverted as planned.** Given the real 5,000-update/GPU recipe above was queued behind [[MF-108]] and the user wanted a result without waiting, ran a much smaller (30 updates, `--device cpu --attention-impl sdpa`, real `data/shards/mf064-150m-train` data, seed 42) 4-arm smoke test instead of the real bounded comparison -- explicitly scoped and reported as a correctness/crash check only, not a trustworthy quality comparison (30 updates is ~0.6% of the real 5,000-update arms' budget). Real measured CPU throughput: ~340-353 tok/s. All four arms trained successfully with no errors: baseline loss 9.263->7.774, untied-same-vocab 9.263->7.769, ngram-bigram 9.263->7.758 (the lowest of the four), ngram-bigram-trigram 9.263->7.769. **User's own read of this (2026-09-13), more precise than a flat "it's just noise" call**: the bigram arm's lower loss might plausibly be a real, if very small, quality signal, not necessarily just noise -- but even granting that, the implementation complexity added to core files is not worth that small a win. This is the actual decision criterion recorded here: not "no signal, so revert," but "possibly a tiny real signal, and still not worth the complexity cost" -- a stricter, values-based bar than a plain win/loss threshold, consistent with the [[feedback_experimental_feature_footprint]] memory saved for this task. This smoke test's real, honest limit stands regardless: 30 updates cannot actually distinguish a genuine small effect from noise (no seed-variance baseline exists at this budget the way [[MF-088]] established one for the real 5,000-update arms), so this is the user's own judgment call under real uncertainty, not a claim this session verified statistically.
  - **Reverted for real (2026-09-13), per the plan above, now that a result exists.** `input_vocab_size`, `ngram_embedding_orders`, `ngram_hash_vocab_size` and all associated code removed from `src/minifrontier/config.py` (fields, `__post_init__` validation, Edu-preset guards, `resolved_input_vocab_size` property) and `src/minifrontier/model.py` (`ngram_tables`, `_ngram_embeddings`, the `forward` wiring and incremental-decode guard); their tests removed from `tests/test_config.py`/`tests/test_model.py`; the three scratch configs and the smoke-test's own artifacts deleted. `ruff check`/`format --check` clean; `pytest tests/`: **486 passed**, exactly the pre-MF-102 baseline count, confirming a clean revert with nothing else disturbed.
  - **Real, well-understood finding preserved despite the revert, per explicit user request**: the paper's actual mechanism (additive hashed n-gram embeddings, base embedding/`lm_head` left tied) is a real, correctly-verified idea that "might be worth considering" -- but the user's own framing is that any future exploration of it belongs as a **`labs/` teaching/exploration script, not core `src/` production code** (2026-09-13: "its not for now the impl, its for lab experiment not in src"). If revisited, build it as a standalone `labs/`-style script demonstrating the mechanism in isolation, not by reintroducing `ModelConfig`/`MiniFrontier` fields -- this task's own real experience (a well-tested, correctly-implemented feature still judged too invasive for the core model) is the concrete reason for that constraint, not merely a stated preference. This task's original core question (was MF-090's regression a tied-embeddings artifact?) also stays **closed as "no, not further pursued"**: the untied-same-vocab arm existed specifically to isolate that, and with the whole task reverted before its real bounded comparison ran, MF-090's regression is treated as a vocabulary-size effect, unexplained further, matching the state before this task was raised.
  - **Real external precedent for untying found and verified (2026-09-13), from the same research pass that verified [[MF-108]]'s `head_dim=96` confirmation, recorded here purely as context since this task is already closed.** Both `EleutherAI/gpt-neox-20b`'s and `EleutherAI/gpt-j-6b`'s real `config.json` files confirm `"tie_word_embeddings": false` -- two real, large, historically significant models both choosing untied embeddings, corroborating that untying is a legitimate, real, large-scale-validated architectural choice in general. This does not reopen this task's own closed question (whether untying explains MF-090's regression at THIS project's 150M scale was never conclusively tested either way) or contradict this project's own `tie_embeddings=true` default, which is separately justified by small-model parameter economy (`ModelConfig.tie_embeddings`'s own docstring: "Saves vocab * d_model parameters and generally helps small models") -- a different argument than whichever tradeoff a 6B-20B-parameter model is making.
- **Priority / state, final: P1 / Done (reverted).** The real question this task asked (does untying and/or input-vocabulary scaling explain or fix MF-090's regression) was not conclusively answered by a real bounded comparison -- only a non-diagnostic CPU smoke test ran before the user's own code-footprint concern took priority and both features were reverted. Not blocking [[MF-119]] or [[MF-070]] any further.

### MF-103 — Add `<|eot|>` as a distinct turn-terminator, separate from `<|eos|>`'s document-boundary role

- **Priority / state:** P1 / Done — code, tests, real tokenizer, and real SFT validation all complete. The new tokenizer was staged (`data/tokenizer-eot`) pending promotion (see 2026-09-10 progress note for why); **promoted for real 2026-09-13, as part of [[MF-119]]'s own tokenizer regeneration** — `data/tokenizer` now carries all 14 special tokens including `<|eot|>` (see this entry's own dated resolution note below for detail).
- **Depends on:** none
- **Deliverable:** a twelfth special token, `<|eot|>` (ID 11, appended after the existing frozen IDs 0-10 so nothing already-numbered moves), used for chat-turn boundaries in `chat.py`/`sft.py`'s templates and packing logic; `<|eos|>` keeps its current, sole role marking document boundaries in `data.py`'s pretraining pack.
- **Acceptance:** a real trained tokenizer with 12 special tokens (`<|pad|>` through `<|tool_result|>` plus `<|eot|>`) round-trips correctly through the existing `MiniFrontierTokenizer`/`TokenizerContract` frozen-ID validation; `render_chat`/`encode_chat_prompt`/SFT packing emit `<|eot|>` at turn boundaries instead of `<|eos|>`; `generate_assistant`'s stopping condition and `non_assistant_special_token_ids()`'s suppression list both account for the new token correctly (an assistant reply must still be able to emit `<|eot|>` to stop, exactly like `<|eos|>` today); a real SFT run (matching [[MF-061]]'s existing evaluation pattern) confirms the model learns to use the new token appropriately, not just that the plumbing exists.
- **Researched and declined (2026-09-09): OpenAI's Harmony chat format is not a fit here.** Fetched and verified directly (`developers.openai.com/cookbook/articles/openai-harmony`), not assumed from the name: Harmony wraps whole messages (`<|start|>{header}<|message|>{content}<|end|>`, 7-8 special tokens total), defines 5 roles with strict precedence (`system > developer > user > assistant > tool`), and splits assistant output into 3 channels (`final`/`analysis`/`commentary`) specifically so a reasoning model can keep chain-of-thought hidden from the user while orchestrating multi-step tool calls. Every piece of that complexity serves two things this project's frozen V1 scope explicitly excludes — reasoning-model CoT training and agentic tool-use orchestration (`AGENTS.md`: "Do not add... agents/tools... in V1") — and `chat.py`'s 3-role system has no analog to Harmony's `developer` role or its channel concept. Adopting Harmony's message-wrapping structure would be a much bigger scope change than this task's actual problem. The one relevant takeaway: Harmony's own dedicated end-of-turn boundary (distinct from any document-level token) is a third real, independent precedent for exactly the eos/eot split this task already cites from Llama 3 (`<|eot_id|>` vs `<|end_of_text|>`) and Qwen (`<|im_end|>` vs `<|endoftext|>`) — corroborating the narrow fix already scoped, not motivating a bigger one.
  Two more formats checked the same day, same conclusion. **ChatML** (verified, `huggingface.co/blog/kuotient/chatml-vs-harmony`): `<|im_start|>role\ncontent<|im_end|>`, one *shared* closing marker across every role, role given as plain text after a generic opener. That is functionally the same shape this task already lands on (`<|role|>content<|eot|>`, one shared `<|eot|>`) — actually slightly more explicit, since each role is its own reserved token rather than something parsed from plain text — and a fourth independent real-world confirmation of the same eos/eot split (Llama 3, Qwen, Harmony, now ChatML/GPT's own family), not a reason to add anything. **Anthropic-style XML tool-use tags** (`<tool_call>`, etc.) are a different category, not a competing tokenizer design: they are plain text tokenized like any other content, with no reserved vocabulary ID at all — a convention for systems that do not control their own tokenizer and inject structure via prompting instead. Adopting that here would be a real downgrade, not a refinement: this project trains its own tokenizer specifically so structural markers can be true reserved IDs ordinary text can never produce, which is exactly the prompt-injection risk this module's own docstring already warns plain-text markers create.
  **Live-checked against currently trending/most-downloaded models (2026-09-10), same conclusion.** Fetched real `tokenizer_config.json` from `Qwen/Qwen3.8-27B` and `openbmb/MiniCPM5-2B` (both trending at fetch time) directly, not assumed: both use `<|im_start|>`/`<|im_end|>` -- the same shared-end-of-turn-across-every-role pattern as ChatML -- with a *separate* token (`<|endoftext|>` for Qwen, `</s>` for MiniCPM) reserved for the base/pretraining-level eos. A fourth and fifth independent, current, real-world confirmation of the same eos/eot split this task already implements (joining Llama 3, older Qwen, Harmony, ChatML). No design change indicated.
- **Status note:** raised by a fourth round of external feedback, and independently verified against the actual code before recording: `data.py`'s pretraining pack glues documents together "with `<|eos|>` between them" and separately, `chat.py`'s chat template also serializes every turn as "content + `<|eos|>`," and `generate_assistant`'s own docstring says the model "stops when it emits `<|eos|>`" — confirming the same token ID genuinely serves both roles today, exactly as claimed. The concern is real and specific: a base model trained with `<|eos|>` meaning "the document is finished" and then SFT'd to also mean "my turn is finished" is being asked to learn two different behaviors from one symbol, which real production tokenizers avoid (Llama 3's `<|end_of_text|>` vs `<|eot_id|>`; Qwen's `<|endoftext|>` vs `<|im_end|>`, both independently confirmed as real, current design choices, not fabricated for this argument). This is flagged P1 specifically because of its own stated time sensitivity: the special-token ID set (0-10) was just re-frozen by [[MF-087]]'s tokenizer revert, and every additional real training run (starting with whichever comes next) makes a future ID-set change more expensive to retrofit.
- **Done (2026-09-10).** `SPECIAL_TOKENS` grows to 14 entries (`<|eot|>`=11, `<|file_sep|>`=12, `<|repo_name|>`=13, IDs 0-10 unchanged — appended, not inserted), `TOKENIZER_VERSION` bumped to 2, `MiniFrontierTokenizer.eot_id` added. `templates/chat_template.jinja`, `chat.py`'s `encode_chat_prompt`/`generate_assistant`, and `sft.py`'s `encode_sft_example` all switched from `tokenizer.eos_id` to `tokenizer.eot_id` at turn boundaries; `data.py`'s pretraining pack is untouched, exactly as scoped. `non_assistant_special_token_ids()` now excludes `<|eot|>` (the new stop signal) instead of `<|eos|>` — `<|eos|>` is suppressed like every other structural token now, since a chat assistant should never emit a pretraining document-boundary marker. 3 new/updated tests (`test_tokenizer.py`'s `eot_id` distinctness check, `test_sft.py`'s wiring/suppression/template assertions switched to `<|eot|>`), plus a real corpus/vocab-size interaction fix in `test_measure_tokenizer_fertility.py` (the fixed `vocab_size=300` case stopped producing a fertility difference once the reserved-token count grew from 11 to 14, leaving 3 fewer BPE merge slots — bumped to 320, verified directly before picking it). Full fast suite: 359 passed, `ruff check`/`format --check` clean.
  **A real tokenizer round-trip and a real SFT run confirm both halves of the acceptance criterion**, not just the plumbing (`reports/mf103-eot-turn-terminator.md`, full detail there). Retrained the real 16,384-vocabulary tokenizer on the exact same corpus every tokenizer this project has trained on (`data/tokenizer-corpus.jsonl`) — round-trips cleanly through the existing `MiniFrontierTokenizer.from_directory` contract validation. Then a real, if intentionally small, base-pretrain (`configs/50m-edu.toml`, 400 updates on a real 800-document/3,248-sequence shard pool packed from the same corpus text, loss 9.7→6.72) → real SFT (`train/sft.py`, 300 updates, the real 32-example CC0-1.0 dataset already used for the real MF-066 SFT releases) → real greedy generation against 8 of the real prompts with **raw token IDs inspected directly, not just the decoded string**: **8/8 stopped exactly at `<|eot|>` before hitting `max_new_tokens`, 0/8 ever emitted `<|eos|>`.** Content accuracy (every reply decoded to "RED" regardless of the actual color asked) is an expected, irrelevant-here limitation of a 400+300-update validation run, not a regression — the concrete claim under test (does the model learn to stop at the new token, never the old one) is unambiguously confirmed. Throwaway checkpoints (~5.4GB, no further reuse value) deleted after the report captured the real numbers; the tiny shard pool and staged tokenizer were kept.
  **The new tokenizer is staged, not yet the real default — a deliberate, user-confirmed sequencing decision, not an oversight.** Retraining changes more than special-token IDs: verified directly (a real side-by-side test) that growing the reserved-token count from 11 to 14 changes how many BPE merge slots are available at a fixed `vocab_size`, which changes the *entire* learned merge tree for ordinary text — not a simple ID relabeling. Promoting `data/tokenizer-eot` to `data/tokenizer/` today would silently desync every already-packed real shard pool (`data/shards/mf064-150m-train`, `modern-1b-train`, `mf094-code-train`, `mf070-longcontext-validation`) that [[MF-101]]/[[MF-083]]/[[MF-095]]/[[MF-097]] are still queued to reuse. Presented as an explicit choice; the user chose to stage now and promote (plus re-pack the real shard pools, one pass) right before [[MF-070]] — avoiding a second re-pack if [[MF-097]]'s own packing-strategy work changes the shards again anyway.
  **Resolved (2026-09-13): promotion happened as part of [[MF-119]]'s own real tokenizer regeneration, not a separate step.** `SPECIAL_TOKENS` already carries all 14 entries in the actual source code, so [[MF-119]]'s real retrain (gpt4-style regex, rebalanced real-code-inclusive corpus) automatically produced a 14-special-token tokenizer and was promoted to `data/tokenizer` directly (user-confirmed, `AskUserQuestion`) once every task this note's own deferral reasoning named ([[MF-095]], [[MF-097]], [[MF-107]], [[MF-108]]) was Done. See [[MF-119]]'s own entry for the real fertility results and the disclosed shard-pool-staleness consequence.

### MF-104 — Reserve `<|file_sep|>`/`<|repo_name|>` tokens for future repo-level code packing

- **Priority / state:** P3 / Done — bundled into [[MF-103]]'s same pass, exactly as this task's own status note recommended
- **Depends on:** none directly, but only becomes useful once repo-level (multi-file) code packing is implemented, which is not currently scoped as any existing task
- **Deliverable:** two more reserved special tokens (following StarCoder2/Qwen2.5-Coder's real, verified convention) marking file and repository boundaries within a packed multi-file code context, for a future repo-level FIM/context task this project's existing single-file FIM (`code_data.py`) does not yet attempt.
- **Acceptance:** N/A until repo-level packing itself is scoped as a real task; this entry exists only to flag that reserving the token IDs is cheapest done in the same pass as [[MF-103]]'s `<|eot|>` addition, before the special-token set is trained into a real checkpoint again.
- **Status note:** raised by a fourth round of external feedback alongside [[MF-103]]'s more urgent, already-relevant gap. Lower priority than [[MF-103]] because there is no existing plan to build repo-level packing in V1 — reserving tokens for a capability with no scoped implementation risks the same "merged in blind" pattern this project has explicitly avoided elsewhere (see [[MF-090]]'s own acceptance criterion).
- **Done (2026-09-10).** `<|file_sep|>` (ID 12) and `<|repo_name|>` (ID 13) reserved in the same `SPECIAL_TOKENS`/tokenizer-retrain pass as [[MF-103]]'s `<|eot|>` — no usage anywhere yet, exactly as scoped (no repo-level packing task exists to consume them). Nothing further to do until such a task is scoped.

### Amendments to existing tasks (2026-09-07, second code-review round)

- **[[MF-081]] scope expanded**: two more candidate interventions for the same bounded-ablation slot, both gated on [[MF-088]] like the rest of that task. **Sandwich norm** (Gemma 2/Gemma 3: normalize both ends of each sublayer, `x = x + post_norm(attn(pre_norm(x)))`, ~30k added parameters total across 20 layers) — proposed as a better-evidenced alternative to LayerNorm scaling for the same slot (LN scaling is one 2025 paper; sandwich norm ships in three production model families), and like LN scaling it attacks the same residual-stream-growth problem the existing `residual_std / sqrt(2*n_layers)` init damping already attacks, so it inherits the same "test the interaction explicitly" acceptance requirement. **Scalable-Softmax / SSMax** (Nakanishi, arXiv:2501.19399: replace the fixed `1/sqrt(d)` attention scale with `s*log(n)/sqrt(d)`, `s` learnable, addressing softmax's attention-distribution flattening as context grows) — folds into the existing SDPA `scale=` argument or a FlexAttention `score_mod`, no new kernel; proposed as the orthogonal arm this project's own NoPE report left open (that report concluded RoPE beats NoPE for length generalization, which is a positional-encoding question — SSMax attacks length generalization through the softmax itself, a different mechanism the NoPE result does not rule out). Neither verified independently yet; both need their own real bounded test before adoption, same as every other item in [[MF-081]].
- **[[MF-086]] scope expanded**: (1) a degeneration metric (repeated-4-gram fraction / distinct-4) is the same item as [[MF-091]]'s point 5 above — tracked there, cross-referenced here since it's also an eval-harness addition. (2) Per-source tokenizer fertility should become standing shard-manifest metadata (bytes/token on web, code, math, etc. separately, not one blended average) rather than measured only inside ad hoc comparisons like `compare_tokenizers.py` — raised alongside [[MF-090]]/[[MF-094]] since it matters most once more than one real source exists to compare. (3) Worth counting for free during any future repack (already writing `doc_ids` per [[MF-085]]'s folded-in schema): the average number of distinct documents per packed sequence — at ~4.5 bytes/token and short FineWeb-Edu documents, a 2048-token sequence likely holds several concatenated documents, not two, which would mean [[MF-085]]'s intra-document masking matters more here than its source paper's average-case result suggests.
