Not independently verified; a real count is cheap and should happen during whichever repack runs next.
- **[[MF-088]] scope clarified**: the CE/PPL/BPB noise floor this task measures and the lm-eval benchmark-accuracy noise floor are different quantities with different (and likely much larger) magnitudes — e.g. `arc_easy` at 150M scores near chance, and at n≈2400 items the binomial standard error alone is ~1%, before seed-to-seed spread is even added. Since [[MF-081]]'s target effect sizes are judged against the CE noise floor but this project's actual release decisions (MF-066-style gates) also lean on lm-eval accuracies, both noise floors should be reported from the same two seeded runs this task already plans to make — no extra GPU time, just an additional metric read off runs that already exist.
- **Process note, not a task**: this session's CUDA-only regression coverage (MF-089's two tests plus several earlier ones) has grown to roughly 10 CUDA-gated assertions across `test_modern.py`/`test_training.py`/`test_loss.py`/`test_reproducibility.py`, and the last two real bugs found this session (MF-089's inference-mode cache taint, the autocast-matmul precision gap above) were both GPU-only phenomena invisible on a CPU-only dev box. Worth considering a small "run this on the real GPU before any multi-day run starts" checklist/tier at some point — not urgent enough for its own task right now.
- **No action needed, four items from a fourth round of external feedback, recorded as corroboration rather than new work**: (1) **GQA over MLA** — the feedback's own conclusion ("MLA solves a problem you don't have": KV-cache pressure in long-context serving, irrelevant at this project's 2048-token context with `n_kv_heads=4`) matches `AGENTS.md`'s existing MLA exclusion exactly; no new evidence to revisit it. (2) **Attention design is "current, but the field has started moving past it"** toward attention interleaved with linear-recurrent layers (Qwen3-Next's Gated DeltaNet, MiniMax's lightning attention, Jamba/Zamba's Mamba blocks) — the feedback's own framing explicitly places this outside frozen V1 ("a scope expansion, not a tweak"), matching `AGENTS.md`'s existing DeltaNet exclusion; recorded as a real, honest "behind the frontier by one generation" acknowledgment, not a task, since nothing here contradicts the frozen-scope decision. (3) **Dynamic Tanh** (arXiv:2503.10622, replacing normalization with `tanh(αx)`) — the feedback's own conclusion is "unproven outside its own paper... lab material at most," and this project's normalization is not a measured bottleneck; noted as a possible future teaching-lab addition, not an ablation candidate. (4) **Architecture shape comparison** (this project's 150m-modern at `d_model/n_layers=38.4` sits between SmolLM2-135M's 19.2 and Qwen3-0.6B's 36.6/Gemma-3-270M's 35.6) — informational only, the feedback's own conclusion is "architecture shapes are fine," consistent with [[MF-092]]'s own already-completed aspect-ratio cleanup.

### MF-105 — Decide whether to enable MTP training for the real release run, now that speculative decoding gives it a second, inference-time benefit

- **Priority / state:** P3 / Done — decided 2026-09-09, ahead of the original "wait until MF-070 is being planned" framing
- **Depends on:** [[MF-093]] (Done, supplies the new evidence this decision needs)
- **Deliverable:** revisit the "MTP stays off by default" decision (`reports/mf070-mtp-quality.md`) with [[MF-093]]'s real result folded in: MTP's training-time cost (−5.3% wall-clock for +0.74% PPL, judged not worth it alone) also buys a real, separately-measured 1.21x inference-time decode speedup via self-speculative decoding at 45.3% acceptance ([[MF-093]], `reports/mf093-speculative-decoding.md`) — a benefit the original decision never weighed, since speculative decoding didn't exist yet when that call was made.
- **Acceptance:** an explicit, recorded decision (not a silent default change) on whether the real 350M/release training run enables `--mtp-extra-heads 1 --mtp-loss-weight 0.3`, weighing the real training-time cost against *both* benefits now known (small quality edge, real inference speedup) rather than the quality-only tradeoff the original decision considered.
- **Status note:** raised directly by the user immediately after MF-093's real result landed, asking whether speculative decoding should now be used instead of plain decoding. Answer recorded here rather than decided unilaterally, since it is a real training-configuration choice for the eventual release run: (1) **Edu needs no decision** — it has never been trained with MTP heads and is unaffected either way, plain decoding stays exactly as it is. (2) **Modern's benefit is real but currently greedy-only** — the exactness guarantee `speculative_decoding.py` relies on does not extend to temperature/top-k/top-p sampling without a materially more complex probability-ratio acceptance rule, which does not exist yet; `chat.py`'s own real default (`temperature=0.8`) would not benefit as-is, while `scripts/sample.py`'s greedy default is the natural first real consumer. (3) The actual open question is upstream of any inference-path wiring: whether MTP training itself gets turned on for the real run, since speculative decoding only has anything to draft from when a checkpoint was trained with MTP heads in the first place. Not started; deliberately deferred to when [[MF-070]]'s real run configuration is actually being decided, not resolved speculatively now on a single 150M bounded measurement.
- **Decided (2026-09-09): enable MTP for the real 350M/release run** (`--mtp-extra-heads 1 --mtp-loss-weight 0.3`), user-confirmed, ahead of this task's own original "wait until MF-070 is being planned" framing — decided alongside this session's other pre-MF-070 recipe calls (LN scaling, gated attention, GQA ratio) rather than deferred further. Reasoning: the combined case (small quality edge + a real, separately-measured 1.21x greedy-decode speedup) now beats the quality-only case that produced the original "off by default" call, the training-time cost is paid once while the drafting capability persists on the checkpoint indefinitely, and the architectural risk is low by construction (MTP heads never touch `ModelConfig`/`state_dict()`, per the frozen carve-out). Explicitly acknowledged and accepted, not overlooked: the measured speedup is single-seed at 150M scale (not yet confirmed at 350M) and is greedy-only today (`chat.py`'s real `temperature=0.8` default will not benefit until a probability-ratio acceptance rule for sampling exists — separate, unscoped future work). Edu is unaffected either way. This does not change `TrainingConfig.mtp_extra_heads`'s own code-level default (`0`, i.e. still off unless explicitly requested) — it is a decision about the real release run's *invocation*, not the library's default, matching how WSD's decay fraction and other release-run recipe choices are recorded. Full reasoning also in `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_DECISIONS.md`'s 2026-09-09 entry.

### MF-106 — SwiGLU clamping (adopt directly, near-free training-stability safeguard)

- **Priority / state:** P1 / Done
- **Depends on:** none
- **Deliverable:** clamp SwiGLU's gate branch (and optionally the up branch) to a fixed numeric range inside `src/minifrontier/layers.py`'s `SwiGLU.forward`, config-gated (off by default, matching `TrainingConfig`'s `z_loss_weight` precedent), preventing an activation spike from propagating into the residual stream.
- **Acceptance:** clamping is a pure `torch.clamp` on the gate projection's output (and optionally the up projection's), adds no parameters and negligible FLOPs; off by default via a `SwiGLU` constructor argument or `ModelConfig` field (`swiglu_clamp: float | None = None`, `None` = current unclamped behavior); a real gradient/forward test confirms clamped and unclamped outputs agree everywhere the clamp isn't active, and diverge (bounded, not NaN/inf) when an input is deliberately pushed past the clamp range.
- **Status note:** raised 2026-09-08 from a second real read of the DeepSeek-V4 technical report (arXiv 2606.19348, verified real via `WebSearch`+`WebFetch` against the actual paper text — see the corrections note below for what else in that same feedback batch needed checking). Section 4.2.3 "Mitigating Training Instability" lists exactly two mitigations: Anticipatory Routing (MoE-only, irrelevant to this project's dense presets) and SwiGLU Clamping — confirmed present as a named subsection in the primary source. The **exact clamp values could not be confirmed from the primary source directly** (the fetched HTML excerpt cut off before the numeric formula); a secondary source (WebSearch summary, not independently verified against the paper itself) claims the gate branch is clamped to `[-10, 10]` and the up branch capped above at `10` — record this as an unconfirmed starting point for the implementation's default, not a citation-grade fact, and pick the actual default from what keeps this project's own real activation statistics comfortably bounded rather than copying an unverified number blind.
  **Why this is more relevant here than at DeepSeek's own scale, not less** (the reviewer's own argument, and it holds up): DeepSeek trains in BF16/FP8, both wide-dynamic-range formats. This project's own `AGENTS.md`-documented precision policy trains in FP16 (with gradient scaling) on non-native-BF16 hardware (the reference RTX 2070 Super, per `reports/mf049-rtx2070s-checkpointing-benchmark.md`) — FP16's dynamic range is real narrower, so a SwiGLU activation spike is a genuinely bigger risk here, not a smaller one. Matches this project's own established "zero wall-clock cost, well-attested, can't plausibly hurt → adopt without a bounded ablation" rule (already used for z-loss and split local/global RoPE theta in [[MF-081]]) — a correctness/regression test is the right bar, not a matched-token quality comparison. Not restricted to Modern: SwiGLU is shared by both frozen presets, and this is a numerical-safety net (like z-loss), not a competing architectural identity the way [[MF-081]]/[[MF-082]]'s Modern-only items are — so, unlike those, this is eligible for both Edu and Modern once adopted, subject to the same off-by-default/no-behavior-change-unless-enabled discipline as everything else here.
- **Implemented (2026-09-08).** `SwiGLU.__init__` gains `clamp_value: float | None = None` (`layers.py`); `ModelConfig.swiglu_clamp: float | None = None` threads it through `TransformerBlock`'s `SwiGLU` construction. Forward: `gate = gate.clamp(min=-clamp_value, max=clamp_value)`, `up = up.clamp(max=clamp_value)`, applied before `silu(gate) * up`. No project-chosen default value is set anywhere (see the unconfirmed-numbers caveat above) — the mechanism exists, off, until a real default is picked. Mirrored into the HF export adapter (`configuration_minifrontier.py`, `modeling_minifrontier.py`) with a real parity test using a small clamp value (`0.05`) chosen specifically to bind against real activations, not just exist as an inert field — and, unlike [[MF-081]]/[[MF-082]]'s items, tested on an **Edu** config to directly confirm the not-Modern-only claim holds in code, not just in prose. 6 new tests (`test_layers.py`: disabled matches explicit-`None`, rejects non-positive, real large-weight-forced clamp-binding check, finite-gradient check under an extreme input; `test_config.py`: allowed on Edu + validation; 1 HF parity case). Full fast suite: 318 passed (up from 312), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean. Full decision record in `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_DECISIONS.md`'s 2026-09-08 entry.
- **A real, precise clamp value found and verified (2026-09-10), from a second real model — not DeepSeek-V4.** External feedback claimed "OpenAI OSS variants... clamp the gate/up projections" at a specific value; checked directly rather than trusted: gpt-oss's primary model card (arXiv:2508.10925v1) only says "clamping and a residual connection" in a footnote, no number — but Hugging Face's real reference implementation (`transformers/models/gpt_oss/modeling_gpt_oss.py`) has `self.limit = 7.0`, confirmed in actual source code, not a secondary summary. **The real shape is asymmetric, and the opposite way around from this project's own implementation**: gpt-oss clamps `gate` upper-bound only (`clamp(max=7.0)`, no floor) and `up` symmetrically (`clamp(min=-7.0, max=7.0)`); this project's existing code (above) clamps `gate` symmetrically and `up` upper-bound only — the reverse assignment. Not a bug (this project's version was built from an unconfirmed secondary-source guess at DeepSeek-V4's numbers, before gpt-oss's real shape was ever checked), but a real design question now on the table if `swiglu_clamp` is ever adopted for a real preset: keep this project's existing symmetric-gate/upper-up shape, or switch to gpt-oss's real confirmed asymmetric-the-other-way shape. `7.0` itself is a real, sourced, ready-to-use number either way — independent of DeepSeek-V4's own value, which remains unconfirmed from any primary source (unchanged from the note above). Not implemented or adopted here — recorded for whenever a real default is actually picked.

### MF-107 — Partial RoPE: rotate only a fraction of each head's dimensions

- **Priority / state:** P2 / Done — raised from P3, user decision (2026-09-12): joined the pre-[[MF-070]] validation gate. **Real result (2026-09-12), `reports/mf107-partial-rope-comparison.md`: full RoPE (100%) wins outright.** All three tested fractions (25%/50%/75%) are real, consistent losses (+1.511%/+1.099%/+0.891% relative BPB, 19-33x this project's own noise floor), with a clean monotonic trend -- closer to 100% is closer to baseline, no intermediate optimum found. Neither DeepSeek-V4's nor Qwen3-Next's real external precedent for partial RoPE transfers as a win at this project's own scale. **Full RoPE stays the default for [[MF-070]].**
- **Depends on:** MF-070 (bounded-comparison methodology), MF-088 (seed-variance noise floor, same as [[MF-081]]'s other ablation items)
- **Deliverable:** a `ModelConfig` field controlling what fraction of `head_dim` actually gets rotated by RoPE per layer (e.g. `rope_fraction: float = 1.0`, current full-RoPE behavior unchanged at the default) — the remaining, un-rotated dimensions carry position-independent content straight through, unmodified. Requires changing `rope.py`'s `RoPE`/`apply_rotary` to only build/apply cos-sin tables over a slice of `head_dim`, leaving the rest of Q/K untouched. A real bounded comparison at 2-3 fractions (e.g. 25%/50%/75%) against the current full-RoPE (100%) baseline, reusing [[MF-081]]'s established methodology and data (`data/shards/mf064-150m-train`, seed 42, matched tokens).
- **Acceptance:** `rope_fraction=1.0` is bit-for-bit identical to the current unmodified RoPE path (a real regression test, not just a claim); each tested fraction lands behind the same real held-out CE/PPL/BPB comparison discipline as every other [[MF-081]]-class item; Modern-only, following [[MF-081]]'s 2026-09-08 scope decision (this is a competing architectural choice, not a safety net like [[MF-106]]) — Edu keeps full RoPE on every dimension, unchanged.
- **Status note:** raised 2026-09-08 alongside [[MF-106]] from the same DeepSeek-V4 report read, verified real via direct primary-source fetch (`arxiv.org/html/2606.19348v1`, not just a secondary summary): Section 2.3.3 "Other Details" confirms DeepSeek-V4 applies RoPE to only "the last 64 dimensions" of each query/KV-entry vector used in its CSA/HCA compressed attention. **Important caveat found while verifying, not present in the original feedback:** "64" is DeepSeek's own absolute dimension count for their own compressed-attention entry width, not a literal "50%" fraction — their actual per-entry total dimensionality could not be confirmed from the paper text (only an unrelated comparison baseline of "head dimension 128" appears elsewhere in the report), so there is no confirmed evidence for exactly what fraction DeepSeek itself uses. This project's own `head_dim` is typically 64 already (e.g. `d_model=768, n_heads=12` → `head_dim=64` for every current preset), so literally copying "last 64 dimensions" would mean 100% (a no-op), not a partial split — the task must be specified and tested as a *fraction* of this project's own `head_dim`, not as DeepSeek's literal absolute number. This project's own prior evidence is directly relevant context, not just an analogy: the existing NoPE experiment (`labs/07_rope_vs_global_nope.py`, [[MF-070]]'s `reports/mf070-global-nope-quality.md`) tested the two endpoints of this same continuum (0% rotated = NoPE, 100% rotated = current default) at the *whole-layer* level and found no measurable extrapolation advantage to removing position information entirely — partial RoPE is a genuinely different, unsampled middle ground (part of *every* layer's own head_dim, not a separate global/local layer choice), and GPT-NeoX/Phi-3/every DeepSeek generation since V2 using some form of it is real, citable precedent for the idea itself (not independently re-verified here beyond DeepSeek-V4's own confirmed text). Queued behind [[MF-081]]'s already-in-flight real GPU arms rather than added to that task's own recorded scope mid-run.
- **Implemented (2026-09-08).** `ModelConfig.rope_fraction: float = 1.0` plus a `rotated_dim` property (`round(head_dim * rope_fraction)`, validated positive and even). `MiniFrontier`'s `local_rope`/`global_rope` tables are now sized to `rotated_dim` rather than `head_dim` (identical at the default, since `rotated_dim == head_dim` then). `CausalSelfAttention.forward` rotates only the LAST `rotated_dim` numbers of Q/K (matching DeepSeek-V4's own convention, per the caveat above — this project's *fraction*, not their literal absolute count) when `rotated_dim < head_dim`, concatenating the untouched leading dimensions back on; takes the exact original unconditional `apply_rotary(query, cosine, sine)` path otherwise, which is *why* `rope_fraction=1.0` is bit-for-bit identical by construction, not merely by testing. Mirrored into the HF export adapter (`configuration_minifrontier.py`, `modeling_minifrontier.py`) the same way as every other MF-081/082/106 item, since `hf_export.py` unpacks every `ModelConfig` field unconditionally. Edu-guarded alongside [[MF-081]]'s other architecture items (`ModelConfig.__post_init__` rejects `rope_fraction != 1.0` on `preset="edu"`).
  **Tests:** 5 new in `test_config.py` (Edu rejection; `rotated_dim` default/fraction computation; out-of-range and odd-rotated-dimension rejection), 3 new in `test_modern.py` (full-fraction real-output equality against the unmodified default — the actual acceptance-criterion check, not a claim; a monkeypatch-based wiring check confirming `apply_rotary` receives exactly the last `rotated_dim` slice of the real post-qk-norm Q/K, not the full `head_dim` or the wrong end; cached-vs-uncached logit parity per `AGENTS.md`'s standing rule), 1 new HF-adapter parity case. Full fast suite: 334 passed (up from 325), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean.
  **Not yet done: the real bounded GPU comparison this task's own acceptance criterion requires** (2-3 fractions vs. the 100% baseline, real held-out CE/PPL/BPB) — the mechanism exists and is tested; no fraction is adopted into any preset. Three scratch configs and commands are ready, queued alongside [[MF-081]]'s remaining arms (same `data/shards/mf064-150m-train`, seed 42, batch=2, 5,000 updates; baseline is the same unmodified `150m-modern.toml` run already shared with [[MF-081]]):
  **A real, exact second precedent found and verified (2026-09-10), beyond DeepSeek-V4's own unconfirmed fraction.** External feedback claimed "Qwen3-Next-style models moved to ~25%" partial RoPE; checked directly rather than trusted: `Qwen/Qwen3-Next-80B-A3B-Instruct`'s real, live `config.json` (fetched from Hugging Face, not a secondary summary) confirms `"partial_rotary_factor": 0.25` exactly — not an approximation, the real shipped value, alongside `head_dim: 256` (that model's own head width, much larger than any of this project's own presets — not directly comparable in absolute terms, only in the *fraction*). This makes `rope_fraction=0.25` a second, independently-sourced, real-production candidate value alongside the fractions already queued (25%/50%/75%), strengthening the case for testing the low end specifically rather than assuming 50% is the natural starting point. Does not change this task's own status — still not run.
  **A third and fourth real precedent found and verified (2026-09-13), from the same research pass that verified [[MF-108]]'s `head_dim=96` confirmation.** `EleutherAI/gpt-neox-20b`'s real `config.json` has `"rotary_pct": 0.25`; `EleutherAI/gpt-j-6b`'s has `"rotary_dim": 64` of `n_embd=4096`/`n_head=16` (head_dim 256, so also exactly 0.25). Two more real, independently-designed large models (6B and 20B parameters) both landing on the identical 25% fraction Qwen3-Next also uses — a striking real convergence across four unrelated model families. This is corroborating context for the interpretation already recorded above, not a reason to reopen this task's already-Done decision: this project's own real bounded test at 150M found every tested fraction a consistent, real loss against full RoPE, with the trend monotonic toward 100%, so the most coherent reading is that partial RoPE's benefit (whatever it is at large real scale) has not been shown to transfer down to this project's own 150M/bounded-token regime — not that the large-model precedent is wrong or irrelevant in general.
  **Real, precise gap found (2026-09-14), user question: has partial RoPE actually been retested at `head_dim=96` (the now-adopted default), or does the real result above still only cover `head_dim=64`?** Checked directly: [[MF-107]]'s own real 100%-baseline checkpoint (`artifacts/mf097-packing/ribbon/final`, also reused as [[MF-108]]'s own baseline) has `config.json` confirmed `head_dim_override=None` -- i.e. `head_dim=64` -- and [[MF-108]]'s `head_dim_override=96` adoption happened one full day *after* this task's real result (2026-09-13 vs. 2026-09-12). **Confirmed: partial RoPE has never been tested in combination with `head_dim=96`** -- the real, adopted current Modern default. This is a genuine, unresolved gap, not a re-litigation of an already-closed question: `rotated_dim = round(head_dim * rope_fraction)` means the same fraction rotates a different absolute number of dimensions at a different `head_dim` (e.g. 25% of 96 = 24 vs. 25% of 64 = 16), so the two are not simply the same test under new plumbing.
  **One real correction to the premise this was raised under, worth being precise about before anyone spends GPU time on it**: the motivating hope (external feedback) was that partial RoPE might "reclaim some of the head_dim=96 memory/throughput cost." This does not hold up mechanically -- RoPE rotation happens on Q/K *before* they reach the SDPA/FlexAttention kernel call; the kernel sees the same `head_dim=96`-shaped tensor regardless of what fraction of it was rotated beforehand. [[MF-108]]'s own real, exhaustive throughput investigation (kernel-selection, gradient accumulation, head-dim padding, all real negative results) never touched RoPE at all, for exactly this reason -- it is a genuinely separate mechanism from whatever causes the Turing-kernel-dispatch cost. So retesting partial RoPE at `head_dim=96` is a real, legitimate, unresolved **quality** question (does the same monotonic full-RoPE-wins pattern still hold at a wider head_dim, where the untouched/position-independent portion of each head carries a different absolute share of the vector) -- not a throughput lever, and should not be expected to recover any of head_dim=96's disclosed ~23% cost. Not yet run; would need a fresh scratch config (`150m-modern.toml` plus `rope_fraction=0.25`, the strongest real external precedent value) and a real bounded comparison against the current adopted baseline, matching this task's own established methodology.
    ```
    ./.venv/Scripts/python.exe train/pretrain.py --config configs/scratch-mf107-rope-fraction-25.toml --train-shards data/shards/mf064-150m-train/train --output artifacts/mf107-rope-fraction/25 --updates 5000 --batch-size 2 --seed 42 --device cuda
    ./.venv/Scripts/python.exe train/pretrain.py --config configs/scratch-mf107-rope-fraction-50.toml --train-shards data/shards/mf064-150m-train/train --output artifacts/mf107-rope-fraction/50 --updates 5000 --batch-size 2 --seed 42 --device cuda
    ./.venv/Scripts/python.exe train/pretrain.py --config configs/scratch-mf107-rope-fraction-75.toml --train-shards data/shards/mf064-150m-train/train --output artifacts/mf107-rope-fraction/75 --updates 5000 --batch-size 2 --seed 42 --device cuda
    ```

### MF-108 — head_dim as its own axis: test 128 against this project's current 64

- **Priority / state:** P1 / Done (2026-09-13) — raised from P3 then P2, user decision (2026-09-12): joined the pre-[[MF-070]] validation gate alongside [[MF-107]]. **Final outcome: `head_dim_override=96` adopted as the real default on every frozen Modern preset**, at a disclosed, accepted ~11.35% parameter / ~23% throughput cost, after a real 64-vs-96-vs-128 comparison, a real quality win (-0.4447% relative BPB/CE), and an exhaustive, fully-closed throughput-recovery investigation (kernel-selection override, gradient accumulation, and head-dim-padding-to-128 all real, measured, negative results — see the follow-up sections below for each). **Independent of [[MF-119]]'s tokenizer regeneration** -- `head_dim` is a pure attention-architecture parameter (query/key/value width) with no interaction with the tokenizer's vocabulary/regex/merges at all, so there is no real ordering dependency between the two; either can run first.
- **Depends on:** MF-070 (bounded-comparison methodology), MF-088 (seed-variance noise floor, same as [[MF-081]]/[[MF-107]]'s other ablation items)
- **Deliverable:** a real bounded comparison of `head_dim=128` (via the existing `head_dim_override` mechanism, MF-092 — no new code needed) against every current preset's actual `head_dim=64` (confirmed by direct calculation: `d_model/n_heads` is exactly 64 on `150m-modern`/`350m-modern`/`500m-modern` alike, despite their different `n_heads`/GQA ratios). `n_heads`/`n_kv_heads` stay unchanged from `150m-modern.toml` (12/4) — only `head_dim_override=128` is added, so Q/K/V/`out_proj` widen from `12*64=768` to `12*128=1536`, a real, disclosed parameter/compute increase, not a matched-parameter comparison (the same "matched-token, disclosed-delta" discipline [[MF-087]]'s tokenizer-vocabulary comparison already used for an analogous complication).
- **Acceptance:** a real held-out CE/PPL/BPB comparison (matched tokens/seed/schedule, same methodology as every other [[MF-081]]-class item this session) between the `head_dim=64` baseline and a `head_dim=128` arm; the real parameter-count and per-token FLOPs delta from widening Q/K/V/`out_proj` is computed and reported alongside the quality numbers, not left implicit, so a win can be judged against its real cost the same way [[MF-087]]'s vocabulary-size comparison weighed fertility against compute; Modern-only, following [[MF-081]]'s 2026-09-08 scope decision (this is a competing architectural choice, not a safety net) — Edu's own `head_dim_override` mechanism remains usable for its existing MF-092 divisibility-decoupling purpose regardless of this task's outcome.
- **Status note:** raised 2026-09-09 after the user asked for a direct comparison against an external "~1.1B model blueprint" that specified `head_dim=128` (with `n_heads=16`, `d_model=2048`) as one of its recommendations. Checked against this project's own real configs before recording: every current preset (`50m`/`150m`/`350m`/`500m`-modern) uses `head_dim=64` regardless of its specific `n_heads`/`d_model`/GQA-ratio combination — this project has never actually varied `head_dim` as its own independent axis, despite already having the exact mechanism needed to do so (`head_dim_override`, built for MF-092's divisibility-decoupling case, not previously used for a quality ablation). Unlike the vocabulary-size question, this project has **no existing evidence either way** on `head_dim` — the blueprint's `128` is a real, common choice in current field practice (Llama 3, Qwen use 128), but that alone is not evidence at this project's own scale/data/hardware, matching the same standard already applied to every other externally-suggested value this session (the GQA-ratio sweep, the `global_rope_theta=500,000` candidate). Not started; queued behind [[MF-081]]/[[MF-107]]'s already-in-flight real GPU arms.
- **Real parameter delta computed (2026-09-09), before any training run — the disclosed cost this task's acceptance criterion requires.** `configs/scratch-mf108-head-dim-128.toml` (150m-modern + `head_dim_override=128`) instantiated and measured directly: baseline `150m-modern.toml` is **138,446,080** parameters; the `head_dim=128` arm is **169,905,920** — a real **+31,459,840 (+22.7%)** increase, entirely from widening Q/K/V/`out_proj` (`12*128=1,536` vs `12*64=768`). Any quality win from this arm has to clear that real cost bar, not just beat the baseline on raw loss.
- **Real, stale-config bug caught before any GPU time was spent (2026-09-13).** `configs/scratch-mf108-head-dim-128.toml` predated [[MF-081]]/[[MF-082]]'s 2026-09-09 adoption of `layer_norm_scaling=true`/`gated_attention=true` as real Modern-preset defaults, and was never updated afterward -- it was missing both flags, which would have confounded this comparison with three changed variables (`head_dim_override` plus two silently-reverted defaults) instead of the intended one. Fixed: both flags added to the scratch config, matching the current real `150m-modern.toml` exactly except for `head_dim_override`. Recomputed real parameter delta against the current (slightly larger, LN-scaling/gated-attention-inclusive) baseline: **138,630,640 -> 170,090,480, +31,459,840 (+22.69%)** -- the absolute delta is unchanged (neither adopted default adds parameters that scale with `head_dim`), only the baseline/percentage shifted negligibly.
- **A real, already-existing checkpoint can serve as this arm's baseline -- no need to retrain it.** `artifacts/mf097-packing/ribbon/final` (trained 2026-09-11, real `config.json` checked directly: `layer_norm_scaling=true`, `gated_attention=true`, `rope_fraction=1.0`, `head_dim_override=None`, seed 42) is byte-for-byte the current frozen `150m-modern.toml` config, already trained on the same `data/shards/mf064-150m-train` corpus at the same seed -- the same checkpoint [[MF-107]]'s own rope-fraction comparison already reused as its "100%"/unmodified baseline. Only the real `head_dim=128` arm needs to actually run; evaluate both it and this existing checkpoint via `scripts/eval_checkpoint.py` against `data/shards/mf064-150m-train/validation`, saving a full ~45-minute redundant baseline retrain.
- Ready-to-run command for the one arm that actually needs training:
    ```
    ./.venv/Scripts/python.exe train/pretrain.py --config configs/scratch-mf108-head-dim-128.toml --train-shards data/shards/mf064-150m-train/train --output artifacts/mf108-head-dim/128 --updates 5000 --batch-size 2 --seed 42 --device cuda
    ```
- **Real result (2026-09-13): `head_dim=128` rejected, real VRAM cost, not just the disclosed parameter count.** Run launched in the user's own terminal (the project's own established reliable pattern for multi-hour GPU work); stopped intentionally partway through (around `checkpoint-00000300` of the planned 5,000 updates) once the real cost was clear, not run to completion or exported -- so the numbers below come from live monitoring (`nvidia-smi`, checkpoint file timestamps) during the run, not a completed `run.json` benchmark record. Flagged as such, not overstated as a finished, official measurement.
  - **Real, measured peak VRAM: ~7,440-7,739 MB, against this card's 8,192 MB physical total** -- repeatedly observed across multiple live checks throughout the run, consistently 90-95% of physical capacity. The real `head_dim=64` baseline (`artifacts/mf097-packing/ribbon/run.json`, a completed, real benchmark record) measured **5,158.9 MB peak** at the identical config otherwise -- a real **+44.6%** VRAM increase from widening `head_dim` alone, not merely the +22.69% parameter-count delta.
  - **Real, measured throughput collapse: ~625 tok/s (~3.28s/update, consistent across two full 100-update intervals), against the baseline's real 4,057.9 tok/s -- roughly 6.5x slower.** This is far more than pure compute scaling from doubling `head_dim` could plausibly explain on its own (Q/K/V/out_proj FLOPs scale linearly with `head_dim`, which alone would predict something closer to a 1.3-2x slowdown, not 6.5x).
  - **Real, precedented root cause, not a mystery number: this project's own already-documented "8GB ceiling silently pages rather than OOMs" finding** (`AGENTS.md`; directly measured once before in [[MF-050]]'s real 8K-context test, which found an out-of-VRAM run falling back to slow Windows system-memory paging rather than crashing). `head_dim=128` pushing peak VRAM to ~91-95% of this card's physical 8GB total is consistent with landing in exactly that same paging regime -- the real, correct explanation for the throughput collapse is VRAM pressure on this specific 8GB card, not an inherent ~6.5x compute cost of `head_dim=128` as an architectural choice in general.
  - **Two alternative explanations checked and ruled out before attributing this to `head_dim`/VRAM**, per the user's own explicit request not to accept the first plausible story uncritically: (1) this session's own same-day code changes (`DiskDeduplicator`'s commit-batching fix, the MF-102 revert) -- ruled out: `DiskDeduplicator` is only used by `scripts/prepare_data.py`'s corpus-building step, never touched by `train/pretrain.py` at all, and the MF-102 revert's cleanliness is independently verified by the exact pre-MF-102 test count (486) plus a clean `ruff` pass; (2) timing also rules out any of today's edits specifically, since the slow pace was already present in the checkpoint-100-to-200 interval, measured before either code change was made.
  - **Decision: `head_dim` stays at 64 (the current, unchanged default).** The real cost (both dimensions -- parameters and, more decisively, this card's own VRAM ceiling) is disqualifying regardless of whether a quality win exists; no held-out CE/PPL/BPB comparison was needed to reach that conclusion, and none was run to completion.
  - **Follow-up, in progress**: rather than stop at a flat rejection, the user proposed testing an intermediate value to see whether some real-field-practice benefit is reachable without crossing this card's VRAM ceiling. `configs/scratch-mf108-head-dim-96.toml` created (`head_dim_override=96`, exactly the midpoint of 64-128): real disclosed parameter cost **154,360,560, +15,729,920 (+11.35%)** -- exactly half of 128's own +22.69%, as expected from sitting at the interval's midpoint. Launched in the user's own terminal at `artifacts/mf108-head-dim/96`, same recipe, right after `head_dim=128` was deliberately stopped (a real, confirmed `KeyboardInterrupt` mid-backward-pass, not a crash).
    **An early ~7,739 MB VRAM reading for this arm was flagged as possibly-premature** (the user noted "96 is in early setup" right at the handoff from the just-killed `head_dim=128` process) -- but re-checked once real past `checkpoint-00000300` (300 real updates in, unambiguously past any startup transient) and it reads **7,737 MB, essentially unchanged**. This is a real, confirmed, stable measurement, not noise: `head_dim=96`'s VRAM footprint genuinely is comparable to `head_dim=128`'s own ~7,440-7,739 MB range, contradicting the naive linear-VRAM-scaling estimate (~6,310 MB) just as much as `head_dim=128`'s own number did.
    **But real throughput tells a completely different story, and this is the genuinely interesting result.** Real measured pace from two consecutive 100-update intervals (`checkpoint-00000100` -> `200` -> `300`): 64.19s and 67.64s, averaging **0.6592s/update (~3,106.8 tok/s)** -- only about **23% slower than the real `head_dim=64` baseline's 4,057.9 tok/s**, nowhere near `head_dim=128`'s own ~625 tok/s (84.6% slower). So despite both arms using comparable total VRAM (both in the ~7.4-7.7GB range, both near this card's 8GB ceiling), `head_dim=96` is **not** hitting the same severe paging collapse `head_dim=128` does -- the VRAM-pressure mechanism is real and directionally correct, but evidently not a simple linear threshold; there is a real, sharp difference in behavior between these two specific values despite similar total memory pressure, plausibly a fragmentation/allocation-pattern effect rather than smooth degradation with total VRAM used. Not independently diagnosed further (would need real allocator-level profiling to say precisely why); recorded as a real, measured fact, not a fully explained mechanism.
    **This makes `head_dim=96` a real, now-plausible middle-ground candidate**, unlike `head_dim=128`: a moderate, tolerable throughput cost (+23%, not +84.6%) at a moderate, disclosed parameter cost (+11.35%, exactly half of 128's own +22.69%).
- **Real result, run to completion (2026-09-13), `reports/mf108-head-dim-comparison.md`: `head_dim=96` is a genuine, real quality win, not just a tolerable cost.** Full 5,000/5,000 updates, 10,230,000 tokens (exactly matching the baseline's budget). Real held-out comparison via `scripts/eval_checkpoint.py` against the same `mf097-packing/ribbon` baseline: **cross-entropy 5.077986 vs. baseline's 5.100674, perplexity 160.45 vs. 164.13, bits/byte 1.70826 vs. 1.71589 -- a real −0.4447% relative BPB/CE improvement and −2.24% relative perplexity improvement, about 9.7x this project's own measured noise floor (~0.046%, [[MF-088]]).** Comparable in magnitude to, or larger than, several already-adopted findings this session (LN-scaling −0.35%, gated-attention −0.67%) -- a real, single-seed, single-scale result clearing this project's own established "several times the noise floor" bar for "plausibly real," not a 3-seed causal claim.
  **Decision: not yet adopted, real cost still has to be weighed against the win.** +11.35% parameters and a real ~23% ongoing training-throughput cost are genuine, recurring costs for the rest of this project's training budget (unlike a one-time architecture change with no runtime cost) -- worth it or not is a real judgment call the win alone does not settle, and is left open pending a decision on whether to spend that recurring compute cost for this magnitude of quality improvement. `head_dim` stays at 64 in the frozen presets until/unless that decision is made explicitly.
  **`head_dim=128`'s own quality was never measured**, on purpose -- only its real, disqualifying cost was (see above); no held-out comparison was run for it, and none is planned given its VRAM/throughput cost already settled that question.
- **Adopted (2026-09-13), user decision: `head_dim_override=96` is now the real default on every frozen Modern preset** (`configs/50m-modern.toml`/`150m-modern.toml`/`350m-modern.toml`/`500m-modern.toml`; Edu unaffected). Full reasoning in `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_DECISIONS.md`'s 2026-09-13 entry. Two hardcoded test literals updated for real (`tests/test_config.py`'s per-preset `head_dim` assertion, now Edu=64/Modern=96; `tests/test_scale.py`'s 350m-modern parameter-count literal, 332,919,744 -> 369,621,696, the real `head_dim=96` delta). Full suite: 495 passed, `ruff check`/`format --check` clean.
  **Real, honest caveat carried forward, not resolved by this adoption**: only 150M was actually measured. All four presets derived the identical `head_dim=64` before this change, so 50M/350M/500M inherit `head_dim=96` by architectural consistency, not independent per-scale verification -- 350M (the real target of [[MF-070]]'s own scale check) has less VRAM headroom to begin with, and a real check at that scale specifically is worth doing before that run locks in on this config.
  **Real addendum (2026-09-14), a second, more consequential caveat found the same way MF-070's own 350M caveat above was resolved: this adoption's own real measurement was taken at `sequence_length=1024`, half of `max_seq_len=2048`'s own declared value -- and at the real declared value, it pages.** Full detail and the real numbers are in [[MF-070]]'s own entry (which is where this was actually found and tested, prompted by the user directly asking whether "150M at seq_len=2048" had ever really been measured); summarized here since it directly qualifies this task's own adoption decision. The original real measurement this adoption rests on (`artifacts/mf108-head-dim/96/run.json`) is itself correct and unaffected -- `peak_reserved_vram_bytes=6.37GB` of `8.59GB` physical, genuinely healthy at `seq_len=1024`. But the same config at the real declared `seq_len=2048` measures `peak_reserved_vram_bytes=12.37GB` -- 44% over physical capacity, real confirmed paging, throughput collapsing to ~239 tok/s (an 11-13x real slowdown from the 1024-context number). **Resolved, not reverted**: `--activation-checkpointing` alone fully fixes it at 150M scale (`peak_reserved_vram_bytes` drops to `5.59GB`, throughput recovers to ~1,966 tok/s) -- `head_dim=96` stays adopted for 150M, but any real 150M-Modern training at anything near the full declared context now has a real, newly-discovered, necessary requirement to pass that flag, which was not previously known or documented. (350M's own already-decided reversion to `head_dim=64`, above, is unaffected and further reconfirmed: the same activation-checkpointing lever only partially helps 350M, whose real footprint is too large regardless of head_dim -- see [[MF-070]]'s own entry for those numbers too.)
- **Follow-up (2026-09-13), user-directed research: find real, measurable ways to close some of the ~23% throughput gap (3,106.8 vs. 4,057.9 tok/s) before treating the current number as final** -- "it doesn't have to match but get at least 50%" (recover at least half the real deficit, i.e. reach ~3,582 tok/s or better). Real, checkable candidates, in order of how likely each is to matter on this specific Turing-architecture card:
  1. **Attention-kernel selection.** `attention_impl="auto"` may resolve to a different (and on this project's own already-documented Turing hardware, often slower) kernel for `head_dim=96` specifically than for 64 -- this project has already measured eager FlexAttention losing to SDPA on this exact card (`reports/mf050-rtx2070s-profile-matrix.md`: "on the hybrid Modern preset manual attention is 6.4x faster on prefill than eager attention_impl=auto"). Many optimized attention kernels (including flash-attention-family kernels) have historically tuned tile/warp sizes around head_dim values that are clean powers of two or multiples of 64 (64, 128) -- 96 is a genuinely less common value that could plausibly force a slower, more generic fallback path independent of raw VRAM pressure. Real, cheap to check: short (~200-update) diagnostic throughput probes at `head_dim=96` under explicit `--attention-impl sdpa`/`manual`/`flex` overrides, compared against the current `auto` resolution's own measured 3,106.8 tok/s.
  2. **Gradient accumulation amortization**, matching [[MF-101]]'s own already-measured real finding for the `head_dim=64` baseline (`accumulation_steps=8`/`32` gave a real, free +3.8%/+9.4% throughput win at identical peak memory, by amortizing per-update Python/optimizer overhead across more forward/backward passes). Untested at `head_dim=96` specifically -- worth a real, cheap check, though this preset's own real VRAM headroom is much tighter than the baseline's, so the same free win is not guaranteed to transfer.
     - **Real result: rejected, and the opposite direction from MF-101's finding.** `--accumulation-steps 8`, `--updates 10`, `--batch-size 2`, `head_dim=96` (`artifacts/mf108-accum-probe/8`, launched via the Bash tool's own native background-task tracking after two earlier probe-design/tooling dead ends -- an initial `--updates 30` probe undershot a fixed timeout by not accounting for accumulation multiplying real microbatch count, and a shell `timeout` wrapper nested inside the Bash tool produced untrustworthy silent no-output exits): real, measured **500.9 tok/s** against the non-accumulated `head_dim=96` baseline's own real 3,106.8 tok/s -- roughly **6.2x slower**, not faster.
     - **Confirmed real, not a measurement artifact, on two separate counts.** (1) `nvidia-smi` showed 99% GPU utilization and ~7,547 MiB VRAM throughout the probe's run -- genuinely, continuously computing, not hung or stalled on data loading. (2) `training.py:872-874` was read directly: `state.consumed_target_tokens += target_count` sums real target-token counts across *every* microbatch inside the accumulation loop (`target_count = sum(count for _, count in validated)` over all `gradient_accumulation_steps` microbatches, `training.py:765-772`), and `tokens_per_second` (`pretrain.py:445`) divides this real total by real wall-clock elapsed -- so accumulation depth is already correctly counted in the denominator's numerator; the low figure is not an undercounting bug.
     - **Root mechanism not further diagnosed, same honest limit already accepted for the 96-vs-128 VRAM/throughput divergence above.** The probe's own measured VRAM (~7,547 MiB) was not higher than the non-accumulated arm's own steady-state ~7,737 MB, which weakens (without ruling out) a simple "more accumulated activations tip this specific run into the paging cliff" story -- some other real interaction between `accumulation_steps>1` and `head_dim=96` on this specific Turing-architecture card is responsible, not independently profiled further. Recorded as a real, measured, negative fact.
  3. **Conclusion (2026-09-13): the user's target ("recover at least 50% of the deficit," i.e. reach >=~3,582 tok/s) was not met by either real candidate tested.** Kernel-selection override (item 1) and gradient-accumulation amortization (item 2) both returned real, clean, negative results -- `attention_impl="auto"`'s own per-layer-type dispatch and `accumulation_steps=1` (no accumulation) remain the best real settings found for `head_dim=96` on this hardware. No further lever is currently planned: any activation-checkpointing/precision-format change would trade throughput for less memory (or vice versa) rather than closing this specific gap for free, larger batch sizes are not expected to help given `head_dim=96` is already at ~94% of this card's 8GB VRAM ceiling (the same paging-risk territory that disqualified `head_dim=128` outright), and `torch.compile` is already independently confirmed not to help on this hardware/PyTorch version combination (real Inductor lowering failure, recorded earlier this session). `head_dim=96`'s real, current, adopted throughput cost stands at ~23% (3,106.8 vs. 4,057.9 tok/s) -- disclosed and accepted as part of the 2026-09-13 adoption decision above, not closed further.
  4. **User asked for a further, cited literature pass ("new arxiv research from the last 6 years... find top 3") beyond the two hardware-level levers above, run via a research fork with real source verification, not recalled from training data.** Top 3 found, ranked:
     - **(a) Zero-pad Q/K/V to `head_dim=128` only inside the sdpa/flex kernel call, slice back to 96 after.** Directly targets the real, documented root cause: PyTorch's own SDPA blog post (`pytorch.org/blog/out-of-the-box-acceleration`) names this exact case -- "gpt-neox-20b (headdim=96) can not dispatch to a kernel using flash attention, unless run on an A100." vLLM's docs (`github.com/vllm-project/vllm/issues/3359`) confirm padding to 128 is "a common workaround." Pure PyTorch, no new dependency, fits the frozen scope -- genuinely different from the already-rejected `head_dim_override=128` arm, since learned parameter width stays 96 (verified: the padded scratch config computes the identical 154,360,560 parameter count as the unpadded 96 arm) and only the transient attention-math tensor shape widens.
       **A real, exact, independently-verified confirmation this session (not just trusting the blog post's own example): `EleutherAI/gpt-neox-20b`'s real `config.json` has `hidden_size=6144`, `num_attention_heads=64` -> `6144/64 = 96`, an EXACT match.** head_dim=96 is not a synthetic edge case invented for this project -- it is the literal head_dim of a real, well-known 20B-parameter model, and PyTorch's own kernel-dispatch gap for it is already documented at that model's own scale, not hypothesized here.
       **Implemented (2026-09-13).** `ModelConfig.attention_head_dim_padding: int | None = None` (Modern-only, structurally rejected on Edu in `__post_init__`, alongside every other MF-081/082/107/108-class item; must exceed `head_dim` when set). `CausalSelfAttention.forward` (`attention.py`) pads Q/K/V's last axis with `F.pad` immediately before the `sdpa`/`flex` branches only (never `manual`), passes an explicit `scale=1/sqrt(head_dim)` to both `F.scaled_dot_product_attention` and `flex_attention` (`None` when padding is inactive, reproducing each kernel's own default exactly), and slices the kernel's output back down to `head_dim` immediately after -- correct by construction, not merely by test: the padded columns are zero in Q/K (contributing nothing to any dot product) and zero in V (so the weighted blend's own extra columns come out exactly zero too). 6 new tests: `test_config.py` (Edu rejection; must-exceed-head_dim validation, both directions), `test_attention.py` (a real correctness check through the actual production `"auto"` dispatch -- one local layer, using flex under auto, one global layer, using sdpa under auto -- confirming padded and unpadded outputs match within `atol=2e-6`, under `torch.no_grad()` since FlexAttention still does not support backward on CPU, an existing documented limitation unrelated to this change). Full fast suite: 498 passed, `ruff check`/`format --check` clean.
       `configs/scratch-mf108-head-dim-96-padded128.toml` created (the adopted `head_dim_override=96` config plus `attention_head_dim_padding=128`) for the real throughput measurement this candidate's whole point requires -- a mathematically-a-no-op padding trick is worthless if it does not also measurably help on this specific Turing card, so nothing is adopted from correctness alone.
       **Real result (2026-09-13): rejected. This backfires badly on this specific card, the opposite of the hoped-for outcome.** A real 200-update GPU probe (`--batch-size 2 --seed 42`, matching the established diagnostic-probe length used for the kernel-selection candidates above), launched via the Bash tool's own native background-task tracking, confirmed genuinely active throughout via `nvidia-smi` (98% utilization): **617.7 tok/s**, against the unpadded `head_dim=96` baseline's real 3,106.8 tok/s -- a **~5x slowdown**, not a recovery. Peak VRAM during the probe (7,577-7,639 MiB) was comparable to, not obviously higher than, the unpadded 96 arm's own steady-state ~7,737 MB -- so this is not simply "padding uses even more memory, tips further into paging."
       **A real, striking, and important pattern: 617.7 tok/s is close to `head_dim=128`'s own real, independently-measured ~625 tok/s** (see the rejection above) -- suggesting the actual mechanism is not (or not only) the VRAM-pressure/paging story that writeup proposed. Whatever makes a genuinely-128-wide attention computation slow on this Turing card fires just as hard for a **transiently** 128-wide tensor reached only inside the kernel call, even though `attention_head_dim_padding=96`'s real learned parameters and per-layer weight tensors never change size and peak VRAM does not obviously increase. This points toward the attention kernel/compute path itself being the real bottleneck at a 128-wide tensor shape on this specific card -- not (or not solely) paging -- a genuine, real update to this task's own earlier causal story, not independently profiled further (matching the same honest limit already accepted for the unexplained 96-vs-128 divergence and the gradient-accumulation slowdown above).
       **What this real result means, plainly (user asked for the mechanism explained, not just the number).** PyTorch's fast attention is not one implementation -- it dispatches between several different hand-written CUDA kernels (a "flash" kernel, a "memory-efficient" kernel, a generic fallback), each written and tuned by NVIDIA/PyTorch engineers for specific GPU generations. This project's reference card (RTX 2070 Super, Turing, a 2018 architecture) is one hardware generation behind what these kernels were mainly designed and tuned for (Ampere and newer, 2020+ -- A100, RTX 30/40-series). At width=128, whichever kernel PyTorch's dispatcher picks on Turing specifically happens to be a slow one; at width=96 it happens to pick a faster one -- a fixed property of the pre-compiled kernel code PyTorch ships for this GPU generation, not something tunable from tensor shapes or Python code, which is exactly why the padding trick (which only changes tensor shape, not GPU generation) reproduced the same slowdown as a genuinely-128-wide model. Not "the wrong tensor shape" -- literally this specific card's hardware generation. A newer GPU (Ampere+, e.g. the same A100 the PyTorch blog itself names) would plausibly not have this problem at all, but that is a hardware question outside this project's frozen scope, not something to chase here. The only remaining code-side fix would be hand-writing or importing a Turing-tuned kernel -- exactly the custom/community-kernel path already excluded above (item 4(b), and by `AGENTS.md`'s own frozen scope) -- so there is genuinely no further lever available on this hardware within scope.
       **Decision: `attention_head_dim_padding` is not adopted, and the code has been reverted (2026-09-13), per explicit user instruction** ("revert the padded head dim technique code we thought would improve it but didn't") -- unlike `head_dim_override` itself (which stays, since `head_dim=96` IS adopted), this was a single-purpose mechanism built only to test this one candidate, and it measurably made things worse, not better, so nothing justifies keeping it. Removed cleanly: `ModelConfig.attention_head_dim_padding` (field, docstring, both validation checks) from `config.py`; the padding/unpadding logic and the `scale=attention_scale` arguments to both `F.scaled_dot_product_attention` and `flex_attention` from `attention.py`'s `CausalSelfAttention.forward` (both kernel calls are back to their pre-this-task form, no explicit `scale=` argument, matching the original unconditional default); the 3 tests this feature added (`test_config.py`'s 2, `test_attention.py`'s 1, plus the now-unused `from dataclasses import replace` import in `test_attention.py`); the scratch config `configs/scratch-mf108-head-dim-96-padded128.toml`. Full fast suite: **495 passed** (498 minus exactly the 3 removed tests, confirming a clean revert with nothing else disturbed), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean. The real measurement and its explanation above are kept, per this project's own established practice (matching [[MF-102]]'s revert) of preserving a real, verified negative finding even after removing the code that produced it. This closes item 4(a); items 4(b)/4(c) above were already resolved without a GPU measurement (excluded by scope; insight-only by explicit instruction).
     - **(b) Community Turing-rebuilt FlashAttention-2 (SM75 forks, e.g. `farnghwai/flash-attention-2080ti`), explicitly testing head_dim=96, FP16-only, claiming 1.2-2.4x forward speedup.** Official FlashAttention still has no Turing support (`Dao-AILab/flash-attention#720`: "Support for Turing GPUs... is coming soon"). **Excluded by this project's own already-frozen scope, not a new judgment call**: `AGENTS.md` already excludes "custom CUDA/Triton kernels" from the V1 core, and a third-party compiled CUDA extension is exactly that -- not code written in or explainable from this repository. Not built; recorded so a future reader does not treat this as an open option.
     - **(c) `bitsandbytes` 8-bit AdamW** -- confirmed Turing-compatible, but a different lever entirely (frees optimizer VRAM headroom rather than speeding up attention) and a new external dependency the user explicitly declined to add ("i dont want to add lib"). **Per explicit instruction, investigated for insight only, not implemented**: fetched `bitsandbytes/optim/adamw.py` (a thin wrapper the user pasted) and `bitsandbytes/optim/optimizer.py`'s real `Optimizer2State` mechanism directly. Two real, transferable ideas, neither adopted: **block-wise 8-bit quantization** of Adam's `m`/`v` moment states, with an independent `absmax` scale per 256-element block (preserves local precision despite the narrow 8-bit range) and a `min_8bit_size` threshold (4096 elements) below which a tensor stays full-precision regardless of the global setting, since quantizing a small tensor is not worth its own risk; and **"paged" optimizer state** via CUDA unified memory, prefetched asynchronously before each update. The second idea is a real, instructive contrast with this project's own already-documented failure mode (`AGENTS.md`'s "8GB ceiling silently pages rather than OOMs," measured catastrophically at `head_dim=128` above): bitsandbytes' paging is deliberate and touches optimizer state only once per full update (not once per forward/backward), a far less latency-sensitive access pattern than the activation/weight paging that collapsed `head_dim=128`'s throughput 6.5x -- so an equivalent idea would not obviously hit the same cliff, though this remains unmeasured and unimplemented here, purely a note for if optimizer-memory pressure is ever revisited. `TrainingConfig`'s own optimizer stays plain AdamW (plus the existing first-party Muon experiment); no new dependency added.
     - **Ruled out during the same research pass, not re-investigated**: a `torch` upgrade (already on 2.13.0 per `uv.lock`, current); official FlashAttention-2/3 as shipped (FA2 has no Turing support yet upstream, FA3 targets Hopper, newer than this card); FastAttention (arXiv:2410.16663, targets Ascend NPUs/Volta, not verified for Turing).
     - **Two more real precedents from the same `EleutherAI/gpt-neox-20b`/`EleutherAI/gpt-j-6b` config verification, relevant to other already-decided items, not this one** -- both models use `rotary_pct`/`rotary_dim` = exactly 25% partial RoPE (see [[MF-107]]'s own entry for why this corroborates rather than reopens that decision) and both use untied embeddings (`tie_word_embeddings: false`, see [[MF-102]]'s own entry for the same treatment).

### MF-082 addendum: DeepSeek-V4's real attention-sink formula (2026-09-08)

Verified real (primary source, `arxiv.org/html/2606.19348v1`), and directly relevant to the "gpt-oss-style learned per-head sink logits" alternative [[MF-082]]'s own task description already named but did not choose (per-head *gated* attention was implemented and tested instead — see [[MF-082]]'s 2026-09-08 progress note). DeepSeek-V4's real mechanism, precisely: one learnable scalar `z'_h` per attention head, added as an extra `Exp(z'_h)` term in the softmax denominator alongside the real keys' scores — `s_{h,i,j} = Exp(z_{h,i,j}) / (sum_k Exp(z_{h,i,k}) + Exp(z'_h))` — letting a head's total attention weight sum to less than 1 (effectively attending to a learned "nothing" option) rather than always being forced to distribute exactly 100% of its probability mass across real tokens. This is a real, precisely-specified, frontier-validated version of the alternative this project did not build. Not a reason to revert the already-implemented, already-tested gated-attention fix without evidence — recorded so the sink-logits formula is available, precisely, if [[MF-082]]'s own real bounded test (once run) says gated attention underperforms and this alternative is worth trying instead.

### DeepSeek V4.1 Flash (2026-09-08): reviewed, nothing actionable

Checked via `WebSearch` on the user's own report. Confirmed real: a two-day internal API beta (`deepseek-v4.1-flash-expires-on-0910`, expiring 2026-09-10), claiming a new architecture with native multimodal (text/image/audio) support and reported speeds up to ~500 tok/s. **No technical report, weights, config, or architecture disclosure exists publicly** — every source is a speed benchmark or a marketing claim, confirming the user's own framing exactly. Nothing technical to extract; not added to the backlog. (DeepSeek-V4 proper, the *different*, already-published report cited alongside it, is real and is where [[MF-106]]/[[MF-107]]/[[MF-082]]'s addendum above actually come from.)

### Corrections to a fourth round of external feedback (2026-09-08)

Two claims in the same feedback batch that produced [[MF-096]]-[[MF-099]] above were checked via `WebSearch` and turned out wrong, or not applicable to this project as stated — recorded here so a future reader does not act on them uncorrected:

- **"Nanochat uses a more sophisticated ~32K tokenizer" is factually wrong on both counts.** Verified via `WebSearch`: nanochat's tokenizer (`rustbpe`, exported to `tiktoken` format) is still byte-level BPE — the same algorithm family this project already uses — with a GPT-4-style regex pre-tokenization pattern (a richer *splitting rule* than plain digit-isolation, not a different tokenization algorithm), at a real vocabulary of **65,536** tokens, not "~32K." This is, if anything, mild corroborating evidence for this project's own [[MF-087]]/[[MF-090]] finding rather than a contradiction: nanochat's much larger vocabulary is trained at genuinely large real compute (GPT-2-grade capability runs), the regime Tao et al.'s vocabulary-scaling theory actually describes — consistent with this project's own conclusion that the theory didn't apply at its own ~7-8M-token bounded-comparison budget, not evidence that the theory is wrong in general. The GPT-4-style full regex pre-tokenizer (handling contractions/whitespace/numbers all at once, not just digits) is a genuinely different, bigger idea than this project's own digit-only `digit_split` parameter — worth a considered, deferred note for a future tokenizer revisit, not adopted now.
- **"Do NOT train from scratch at 16K or 32K" conflates two unrelated axes.** The cited table (SmolLM2 pretraining at `sequence_length=2048`) is about **context/sequence length**, not **tokenizer vocabulary size** — this project's own real 16,384-vs-32,768 comparison ([[MF-087]]/[[MF-088]]/[[MF-090]]) was entirely about vocabulary size, at a constant `sequence_length=1024` throughout every arm. This project's actual `max_seq_len` default is already `2048` (`ModelConfig`), and real training data has been packed at `sequence_length=1024`-`2048` all session — already exactly matching the feedback's own recommended "main pretraining: 2048" table. No correction to this project's practice is needed here; the advice is sound in general but was never actually being violated.

### MF-109 — Wire self-speculative decoding as the default Modern release inference path

- **Priority / state:** P2 / Done
- **Depends on:** MF-093 (self-speculative decoding), MF-105 (the decision this implements)
- **Deliverable:** [[MF-105]] decided to enable MTP training for the real release run partly *because* of the real inference-time speedup self-speculative decoding gives — but a real gap made that speedup undeliverable: `export_release`/`load_release` (`checkpoint.py`) never learned about MTP heads at all when [[MF-093]] added `mtp_heads` support to `save_training_checkpoint`/`load_training_checkpoint`, so a real release exported from an MTP-trained checkpoint would silently drop the draft heads, and `scripts/sample.py` had no path to use them even if it hadn't. This task closes that gap end to end: export carries the heads, a release can load them back, and `scripts/sample.py` uses them automatically. User's own framing: "if speculative decoding was good, it should be default for modern, but not edu."
- **Acceptance:** a release exported from an MTP-trained checkpoint contains `mtp_heads.safetensors`; `scripts/sample.py` uses self-speculative decoding automatically whenever a release has trained heads and the request is genuinely greedy (`--temperature 0`, no `--top-k`/`--top-p`), with a `--no-speculative` opt-out for a plain-decode baseline; a release with no MTP heads (every Edu release, and any Modern release trained without MTP) behaves identically to before this task, structurally — not via a preset check. `verify_release` must load-test the MTP heads the same way it load-tests the main model, before `scripts/export.py`'s default cleanup is allowed to delete the only other copy of those weights.
- **Done (2026-09-09).**
  - **`checkpoint.py`**: `export_release` gains `mtp_heads: MTPHeads | None = None` — when given, writes `mtp_heads.safetensors` plus a small `mtp_config.json` (`{"n_extra_heads": N}`, since MTP's head count lives in `TrainingConfig`, never `ModelConfig`, so it has nowhere else to be recovered from at load time) before `write_release_manifest` runs, so both files are automatically covered by the existing generic (fully glob-based, not a hardcoded file list) sha256 manifest with no manifest-code changes needed. New `load_release_mtp_heads(directory, config, *, device=...) -> MTPHeads | None`, deliberately separate from `load_release` itself so every existing caller's two-value-unpacking stays completely unaffected — returns `None` for the overwhelming common case (no `mtp_heads.safetensors` present) rather than raising, since "no heads" is an expected outcome callers branch on, not an error.
  - **`release.py`**: `verify_release` now also load-tests MTP heads when `load_release_mtp_heads` finds them — a real bug was caught writing this: the first version ran the hidden-state forward pass outside `torch.no_grad()`, which crashed FlexAttention on CPU (`NotImplementedError: FlexAttention does not support backward on CPU`) the moment a real test exercised it; fixed by moving the model call inside the same `no_grad` block as the heads' own forward pass. Report gains `mtp_heads_present: bool`.
  - **`scripts/export.py`**: reads `mtp_extra_heads` directly from the source checkpoint's own `trainer_state.json` (`training_config.mtp_extra_heads`, the same field `compare_mtp.py`/`train/pretrain.py` already write) rather than a new CLI flag, so export is fully automatic from what the checkpoint actually contains; missing key (any checkpoint saved before MTP existed) defaults to `0`/`None`, unaffected. Constructs `MTPHeads` at the right shape, forwards it through both `load_training_checkpoint` and `export_release`.
  - **`chat.py`**: `complete_text` gains `mtp_heads: MTPHeads | None = None`. Takes the `speculative_generate` path only when `mtp_heads is not None and temperature == 0.0 and top_k is None and top_p == 1.0` — genuinely greedy, matching the exactness guarantee exactly; any sampling request silently falls back to the existing plain-decode path rather than producing an unguaranteed approximation. Verified for real, not assumed: a dedicated test confirms `complete_text`'s output is bit-for-bit identical with and without `mtp_heads` under greedy decoding, and a monkeypatch-based test confirms `speculative_generate` is never even called once temperature moves off zero.
  - **`scripts/sample.py`**: loads `load_release_mtp_heads(args.model, model.config, device=args.device)` and forwards it to `complete_text`, unless `--no-speculative` is passed. This is what makes "default for Modern, never Edu" a structural fact rather than a preset check: Edu has never had trained MTP heads to export in the first place (`AGENTS.md`'s MTP carve-out), so `load_release_mtp_heads` simply always returns `None` for any Edu release, automatically, with no Edu-specific code anywhere in this path.
  - **Tests**: 12 new across 5 files — `tests/test_checkpoint.py` (no-heads-means-None-and-no-files; real weight round-trip through export+load; manifest covers the two new files), `tests/test_release.py` (real MTP-heads load-test in `verify_release`, plus the corruption-is-caught regression this task's own review found), `tests/test_chat_complete_text.py` (no-heads baseline unaffected; greedy-with-heads exactly matches plain decode; sampling-with-heads never calls the speculative path), `tests/test_export_cli.py` (real functional test through `export.main()`: forwards heads when present, omits them when absent, and doesn't crash on a checkpoint saved before `mtp_extra_heads` existed as a key at all), `tests/test_sample_cli.py` (real functional test through `sample.main()`: passes heads by default when present, `--no-speculative` suppresses them, no-heads release passes `None`). Full fast suite: 358 passed (up from 346), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean.
  - Full reasoning also recorded in `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_DECISIONS.md`'s 2026-09-09 MF-105 entry (this task implements that decision, not a separate one).
- **Follow-up (2026-09-10): a real, more fundamental gap in the same chain, found by external review, not by this task's own tests.** `train/pretrain.py` itself — the actual real entry point for the eventual 350M/release run — never forwarded `mtp_heads` to *either* of its own `save_training_checkpoint` calls (periodic or final), so a real `--mtp-extra-heads 1` run never wrote `mtp_heads.safetensors` to any checkpoint at all. This task's own real coverage (`tests/test_export_cli.py`) tested `scripts/export.py`'s checkpoint→release path correctly but built its test checkpoints directly via `save_training_checkpoint(..., mtp_heads=...)`, never through `train/pretrain.py`'s own CLI — so this gap was invisible to every test this task added. Left unfixed, [[MF-105]]'s decision would have failed the moment the real 350M/release run actually tried to export: `scripts/export.py` would read `mtp_extra_heads>0` from `trainer_state.json`, correctly try to load `mtp_heads.safetensors`, and hit `checkpoint.py`'s own loud failure ("this checkpoint was not saved with MTP heads") — a real, previously-undiscovered blocker for the exact real run this project has already committed to. Separately, `--resume` combined with `--mtp-extra-heads > 0` was still unconditionally rejected in `train/pretrain.py`, with a comment claiming MTP weights "are not currently persisted in the checkpoint" — true when written, stale after [[MF-093]] added real save/load support, never revisited.
  - **Fixed for real**: both `save_training_checkpoint` calls in `train/pretrain.py` (periodic and final) now pass `mtp_heads=mtp_heads`; the `--resume`+`--mtp-extra-heads` rejection is removed, and the resume's own `load_training_checkpoint` call now passes `mtp_heads=mtp_heads` so a resumed run actually restores the trained heads instead of either crashing or (before [[MF-093]]) silently reinitializing them. CLI help text for `--mtp-extra-heads` corrected to match.
  - **3 new tests** in `tests/test_pretrain_cli.py`, replacing the one that only asserted rejection: `test_mtp_heads_are_saved_in_every_checkpoint_when_enabled` (real check that both the periodic and final checkpoint directories contain `mtp_heads.safetensors`), `test_resume_with_mtp_extra_heads_restores_the_trained_heads` (real resume through the actual CLI path, no crash, heads file still present after the resumed run's own re-save — honestly scoped to what `pretrain.py`'s own architecture can test, per the same documented limitation `test_mixture_flag_reaches_real_training_and_checkpoint_reloads` already states: no single test process can run a real partial-then-resume-to-completion scenario, since each invocation always runs to its full `--updates`). Full fast suite: 373 passed (up from 371), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean.
- **A second, unrelated real gap fixed in the same pass, found by the same external review**: `train/pretrain.py` had no way to set `TrainingConfig.decay_embeddings=False` at all — the same class of CLI-wiring gap [[MF-084]]/[[MF-092]] each found once before for other fields, here for a field that already existed and was already used by `_parameter_groups`. Real published small-model recipes (OLMo 2 1B, SmolLM3) report excluding the (here, tied) embedding from weight decay as a stability improvement — not independently tested on this project's own data/hardware, so **not changed as a default**, just made reachable: `--no-decay-embeddings` (off by default, matching every other flag in this file). New test `test_no_decay_embeddings_flag_reaches_real_training` (two otherwise-identical real runs, nonzero weight decay, confirms the resulting loss differs — same proof-of-wiring discipline as `--schedule`'s own test).
- **A real, unrelated README/config drift also found and fixed by the same review**: the 500M preset's own table entries (both the layers/local/global-indices table and the Frozen V1 targets table) still showed its pre-[[MF-092]] shape (24 layers/1,280 width/20 heads/3,456 FFN) — [[MF-092]]'s 2026-09-07 reshape to 30/1,152/18/3,072 was never propagated to `README.md`. Corrected to match `configs/500m-modern.toml` exactly (30 layers, local=23, global indices 3,7…27, matching [[MF-092]]'s real reshape; KV heads corrected to 6, SwiGLU width to 3,072).

### A future 1B-scale initiative (2026-09-10 feedback): recorded as context, not scoped, deferred like MF-098's distillation arc

External feedback proposed a concrete ~1B-parameter "ModernLM-1B" architecture (20 layers, d_model=2048, 16 Q/4 KV heads, head_dim=128, d_ff=5504, 64K-vocab) and separately described Qwen3.5-0.8B's real current architecture — verified real by the feedback's own citations (`Qwen/Qwen3.5-0.8B`'s config): 24 layers alternating 3 Gated DeltaNet layers then 1 full-GQA-attention layer, partial RoPE, output gating, a 248,320-token vocabulary, and an auxiliary MTP layer. The feedback's own conclusion is to build the simpler GQA+SwiGLU dense shape for a first 1B model, not clone Qwen3.5's DeltaNet hybrid — DeltaNet needs specialized `causal_conv1d`/FLA kernels or falls back to a much slower plain PyTorch path, which the feedback itself frames as a v2-or-later idea. This matches `AGENTS.md`'s own existing exclusions exactly (no DeltaNet/linear-attention hybrids, no MoE, no native multimodality) — no scope change indicated. The feedback also makes a real, checkable hardware argument: at `6 * params * tokens` FLOPs, a 1B-parameter/2T-token run (matching Gemma 3 1B's/OLMo 2 1B's own real published token budgets) is on the order of years even at this project's reference card's theoretical peak throughput, confirming this project's own repeated framing that the 2070 Super is a development/inference card, not a competitive-pretraining one. **A genuine 1B preset is not in this project's frozen V1 scope at all** (`AGENTS.md`: "350M and 500M are optional post-V1 scale checks" — no 1B) — recorded here as real, useful context for whenever that scope question is revisited, explicitly deferred alongside [[MF-098]]'s distillation/post-training arc and any distributed-training work, per the user's own explicit instruction to set both aside for now. No task opened; nothing in current V1 configs changed by this note.

### Frontier-scale MoE local-inference hardware (2026-09-13 feedback): recorded as ecosystem-completeness context, not a MiniFrontier scope question

User-relayed hardware guidance for running a real, current frontier-scale MoE model (GLM-5.3) fully locally via `llama.cpp`/Unsloth GGUF quantization -- explicitly requested as **ecosystem-completeness context**, not a hardware decision or a claim about MiniFrontier itself, and explicitly **not independently fact-checked** (numbers below are as relayed, not verified via `WebSearch` against real current pricing/specs -- record accordingly if anyone acts on them later).

- **The relayed numbers, for contrast only**: a real GLM-5.3-class dense-equivalent MoE model needs ~223-245 GB even at the most aggressive 1-2-bit dynamic quantization (Unsloth `UD-IQ1_S`/`UD-IQ2_M`), meaning either a 24GB+ consumer GPU (RTX 4090/5090) paired with 256GB DDR5 system RAM for CPU/MoE-expert offload (`llama.cpp`'s `-ot "exps=CPU"`/`--cpu-moe` flags), or a unified-memory machine (Mac Studio, up to 512GB unified memory in the newly-announced M5 Ultra) that avoids the discrete-GPU-to-system-RAM offload bottleneck entirely. Either path is described as costing many thousands of dollars and running at single-digit-to-low-teens tokens/second even once built.
- **Direct, real contrast with this project's own actual, measured numbers**: MiniFrontier's entire V1 scope -- 50M/150M/350M/500M dense (non-MoE) presets -- was designed against, trained on, and measured on a single **8GB** RTX 2070 Super (`reports/mf049-rtx2070s-checkpointing-benchmark.md`, `reports/mf050-rtx2070s-profile-matrix.md`, and every real training/eval run this session references), full stop -- roughly three orders of magnitude less memory than what a GLM-5.3-class model needs even at its most aggressively quantized. This is not an accident or a limitation to apologize for: `AGENTS.md`'s own frozen V1 scope explicitly excludes MoE, and the whole project's stated mission is "a from-scratch, readable, single-GPU decoder-only language model" -- consumer-hardware accessibility is a design goal here, not a constraint being worked around.
- **Real ecosystem-completeness takeaway**: this project's own three standard adapter surfaces (HF Transformers export, vLLM's OpenAI-compatible serving, GGUF/`llama.cpp`) are the *same* real interfaces a GLM-5.3-class model uses -- the same `llama-server --host 0.0.0.0` OpenAI-compatible pattern the user's own relayed setup guide describes for a Mac-Studio-hosted GLM-5.3 works identically for a MiniFrontier GGUF export once [[MF-073]]/[[MF-074]] land. The real difference between "runs on a $400 used GPU" and "needs a $10k+ Mac Studio or a 256GB-DDR5 workstation" is entirely model *scale/family* (dense small model vs. frontier MoE), not a different ecosystem or different tooling -- worth keeping in mind as a real, concrete illustration of why this project's own scope choice matters, not as a reason to consider MoE or larger scale for MiniFrontier itself.
- No task opened; nothing in current V1 scope, configs, or the ecosystem-completeness table (`README.md`) changed by this note -- purely recorded context, per the user's own explicit framing.

### MF-135 — Four research leads from a second external "Top 10 modern techniques" review (2026-09-15), plus a verification pass against this project's own real state

- **Priority / state:** P3 / Not started -- research leads only, no primary source read yet, same treatment as [[MF-112]]/[[MF-133]]
- **Depends on:** none structurally
- **Deliverable:** a second external "Top 10" list (following an initial positive review of the project overall) arrived while [[MF-070]]'s real 3B-token training run was in progress (2026-09-15). Checked against this project's own real, already-recorded state before accepting or recording anything, matching [[MF-133]]'s own established discipline -- most of the list turned out to already be resolved by real, measured evidence on record, several in the *opposite* direction from the external claim, not just already-covered:
  - **Already tested, real result -- in some cases contradicting the external claim, not merely confirming it:**
