The naive version (chunking to `max_seq_length` alone) silently truncates the generation instead -- worth reusing this exact reasoning if MiniFrontier ever runs a local teacher to generate synthetic training data.
  3. **A real, confirmed gap in text cleaning, not previously known -- checked directly against `data.py`/`shards.py`, not assumed from the fork's own report alone.** `raw_text.py`'s `_TextCharTable` strips a specific, deliberately-scoped set of invisible/junk Unicode code points (soft hyphen, zero-width space, bidi embedding/override/isolate controls, BOM, interlinear annotation marks, Unicode noncharacters) while explicitly keeping real format characters (ZWJ, ZWNJ, emoji tags). **Verified this project's own pipeline has no equivalent anywhere**: `shards.py`'s only text-quality gate (line ~193) checks for empty text and a literal null byte (`"\x00" in document.text`) -- nothing else. `data.py`'s `_CHARACTER_BOUNDS` (the `character_bounds` admission-rejection reason seen in every real `metadata.json` this session, e.g. [[MF-070]]'s own github-code prep) is a length check, not a junk-character filter -- confirmed by reading it directly, not assumed from the name. This means genuinely junk invisible characters (zero-width spaces, bidi controls, BOM) currently pass straight through admission into every real training shard this project has ever built, undetected. Real, cheap, worth a small fix -- the specific character list above is the useful part to borrow (the idea, not Unsloth's code, per this project's standing no-import rule), not a large engineering effort.
  - **Considered and correctly not pursued further**: stride-overlap chunking for arbitrary raw `.txt` files (`RawTextDataLoader.smart_chunk_text`) solves a different problem than MiniFrontier's own ribbon packing (retrofitting ad hoc raw text files a user hands you, vs. this project's own streaming multi-source document pipeline) -- not clearly needed given MiniFrontier has no such "point me at a folder of files" ingestion path today, noted for completeness only. `TextPreprocessor.validate_dataset`'s own exact-match-only duplicate/quality checks are already exceeded by MiniFrontier's real simhash-based near-dedup. RL/GRPO reward-function data formatting (`unsloth/models/rl_replacements.py`) is out of scope per `AGENTS.md`'s existing RL ban, not investigated further. No vision/multimodal data handling found anywhere under `unsloth/dataprep/`.
- **Acceptance:** item 3 (Unicode noise-stripping) is a real, scoped, small fix: add a similar deliberately-scoped junk-character strip to MiniFrontier's own admission path (likely alongside or before the existing `_CHARACTER_BOUNDS` check in `data.py`), with a real regression test confirming a document containing zero-width spaces/BOM/bidi controls is cleaned rather than either silently admitted with junk intact or wrongly rejected outright. Items 1-2 are N/A until [[MF-098]] itself is scoped.
- **Status note:** not scheduled; recorded per `AGENTS.md`'s "add newly discovered work to the backlog instead of hiding it" rule, at the user's own direct follow-up request. Item 3 is real and independent of any pretraining decision or the live [[MF-070]] run -- could be picked up any time without needing GPU time, though it would only affect *future* data-prep runs, not retroactively clean shards already built.

### MF-146 — Real `transformers` insights: SIGTERM checkpoint safety, adaptive LR, entropy-adaptive sampling, more WSD decay shapes

- **Priority / state:** P2 / item 1 Done (2026-09-18); items 2-5 Not started
- **Depends on:** none structurally; item 1 touches `checkpoint.py`/`train/pretrain.py` (builds on [[MF-079]]/[[MF-130]]'s existing atomic-write machinery); item 4 touches `WarmupStableDecaySchedule` ([[MF-083]]); item 3 touches `generation.py` ([[MF-091]])
- **Deliverable:** an earlier pass this session mined `reference/transformers-main.zip` only for `PretrainedConfig`/`PreTrainedModel` HF-ecosystem-compatibility conventions ([[MF-137]]/[[MF-142]]). At the user's own direct follow-up request, a second pass went deep on `Trainer`, `generation/`, and tokenizer internals -- real code read directly (`training_args.py`, `trainer.py`, `trainer_jit_checkpoint.py`, `optimization.py`, `logits_process.py`), cross-checked against MiniFrontier's own `checkpoint.py`/`generation.py`/`training.py`.
  1. **JIT checkpoint on SIGTERM -- directly relevant to a real incident this project has already had, not a hypothetical.** `trainer_jit_checkpoint.py`'s `CheckpointManager` registers a `SIGTERM` handler; on receipt, it waits a short grace period (default 3s, to distinguish a genuine termination request from an imminent `SIGKILL`), writes a sentinel "checkpoint is incomplete" marker, forces an immediate save, then deletes the sentinel on success. **This is complementary to, not redundant with, MiniFrontier's own [[MF-079]]/[[MF-130]] atomic-write fix** -- that work makes *whatever checkpoint gets written* safe to resume from; this idea minimizes *how much work gets lost before a checkpoint is written at all*. [[MF-070]]'s own real 1B-token pre-work run lost ~21,000 updates (~3 hours) to an external kill specifically *because* the run was mid-interval, before the atomic-write fix even existed to protect a partial write -- a SIGTERM-triggered emergency checkpoint would have caught that kill at whatever update it actually happened on, not at the next scheduled interval. **Real, disclosed platform caveat**: this project trains on Windows, and Python's signal handling there is more limited than POSIX (`SIGTERM` delivery/handling differs; `SIGBREAK`/`CTRL_BREAK_EVENT` may be the more portable real equivalent) -- needs a real, verified implementation on the actual target platform, not assumed to port unchanged from a POSIX-first reference.
  2. **`save_total_limit`'s real "best + recent" retention semantics -- a real, cheap idea our own checkpoint pruning doesn't have.** `training_args.py`: combined with a validation metric, `transformers` keeps the *N-1 most recent plus the single best-scoring checkpoint*, not just the N most recent. MiniFrontier's own `prune_old_checkpoints` (`checkpoint.py`) is confirmed pure-recency-only -- no best-checkpoint-retention concept exists at all, so a real run's single best validation checkpoint could get pruned away by a later, worse one. Now has a real prerequisite it didn't before [[MF-136]]: periodic in-loop validation exists to rank checkpoints by, making this idea newly actionable rather than only theoretical.
  3. **`TopHLogitsWarper` -- a real, recent (NeurIPS 2025, arXiv:2509.02510), citable sampling technique MiniFrontier's own toolkit doesn't have.** Entropy-adaptive nucleus selection: keeps tokens until cumulative entropy exceeds `τ = H(p) × top_h` -- the cutoff width adapts per-token to the distribution's actual peakiness, unlike top-k's fixed count, top-p's fixed mass, or min-p's fixed floor (all already in `generation.py` per [[MF-030]]/[[MF-091]]). A real, not-yet-considered addition to that same toolkit.
  4. **`get_wsd_schedule`'s real decay-shape options exceed MiniFrontier's own WSD implementation.** `transformers` supports `decay_type` in `{"linear", "cosine", "1-sqrt"}`; `WarmupStableDecaySchedule` ([[MF-083]]) only implements cosine-shaped decay. A real, cheap, untested axis -- a different decay curve shape, not a different schedule family. Also real, useful validation: `transformers` now ships WSD as a standard, named scheduler -- independent confirmation this project's own MF-083 adoption tracks a real, now-mainstream convention, not a one-off choice.
  5. **`GreedyLR`** (`optimization.py`, arXiv:2512.14527) -- a real, primary-sourced adaptive LR scheduler that raises/lowers LR in response to actually measured training-loss plateaus, rather than following any predetermined shape. Genuinely orthogonal to both `WarmupCosineSchedule` and WSD, which never look at real training dynamics at all. A real, distinct third schedule family worth knowing exists, lower priority than item 4's cheaper decay-shape tweak.
  - **Already matched, not a gap**: `max_grad_norm` defaults to `1.0` in `transformers`, exactly matching MiniFrontier's own `gradient_clip=1.0` default.
  - **Explicitly out of scope**: `Trainer` itself stays banned per `AGENTS.md`; the Kubernetes/Slurm-oriented framing around `enable_jit_checkpoint` (`terminationGracePeriodSeconds`) is cluster-specific -- only the underlying signal-handler *idea* (item 1) transfers to a single-machine run, not that orchestration layer.
- **Acceptance:** N/A until scoped -- item 1 needs a real, verified Windows-specific implementation (confirm the actual real signal MiniFrontier can catch on this project's own target platform) plus a real interruption test before being trusted on a multi-day run; item 2 needs [[MF-136]]'s validation wired into a real run first to have a metric to rank against; items 3-5 each need the same real primary-source-then-bounded-test discipline as every other sampling/schedule item already in this backlog.
- **Status note:** items 2-5 not scheduled; recorded per `AGENTS.md`'s "add newly discovered work to the backlog instead of hiding it" rule. Item 1 implemented and tested (2026-09-18), at the user's own direct request, **deliberately without touching the live [[MF-070]] training process** -- editing source files never affects an already-running Python process; this only takes effect on the *next* real launch (a fresh start or a future `--resume`).
  - **Real implementation**: new `src/minifrontier/termination.py` (`TerminationRequestTracker`, `GracefulTerminationRequested`). Registers handlers for `SIGTERM`, `SIGINT`, and (Windows-only) `SIGBREAK` -- confirmed via real signal delivery on this project's own actual Windows machine (`signal.raise_signal`, not `os.kill`, since `os.kill(pid, signal.SIGTERM)` on Windows calls `TerminateProcess` directly for any non-`CTRL_*` signal value, bypassing any registered handler and killing the calling process outright -- a real, documented platform trap avoided here, not guessed around). **Real, disclosed limit, stated plainly in the module's own docstring, not glossed over**: this catches a *requested, graceful* stop only -- Ctrl+C/Ctrl+Break in the same console, or a real POSIX SIGTERM. A hard force-kill (`Stop-Process -Force`, `taskkill /F`, Task Manager's "End task", real `SIGKILL`) can never be caught by any process on any platform; this narrows the real gap (a graceful request landing mid-interval), it does not close it, and never can.
  - **Wired into `train/pretrain.py`**: `run()` installs the tracker before calling `train_updates`, wrapped in `try/finally` so handlers are always restored regardless of how the run ends (normal completion, a graceful stop, or an unrelated real crash) -- real, tested concern, since `pretrain.run()` is called repeatedly within the same process by the test suite, and a leaked handler would otherwise contaminate whatever runs next. `update_progress_and_checkpoints` checks the tracker every update (independent of `--checkpoint-interval`) and, if triggered, forces an emergency checkpoint at the update that just completed -- not the next scheduled interval boundary -- reusing the exact same `save_training_checkpoint` call shape (and directory naming) as a periodic save, factored into one shared `save_checkpoint_and_prune` helper to avoid duplicating that call twice. `--no-checkpoint` is respected: a termination request under that flag stops immediately without writing anything, matching that flag's own existing "nothing kept" contract. `GracefulTerminationRequested` propagates out of `run()` uncaught (not swallowed into a same-shape return value) so `main()` can print a clear message and exit non-zero -- signaling "did not complete," the correct outcome for `run_mf070_150m_release.ps1`'s own `$LASTEXITCODE` step-success check, same as a real crash would.
  - **Real, cheap, deliberate scope decision**: only `train/pretrain.py` was touched, not `train/sft.py` -- SFT runs are already described as "small and fast... minutes rather than days" (per `train/sft.py`'s own module docstring), so the real risk this fix addresses (losing hours of multi-day progress to an external kill) doesn't apply there the same way. Not implemented for SFT; a real, separate follow-up if that ever changes.
  - **Tests**: 8 new in `tests/test_termination.py` (real signal delivery via `signal.raise_signal` for SIGTERM/SIGINT/SIGBREAK -- confirmed genuinely catchable on this actual machine, not just asserted from documentation; install/restore round-trip; double-install rejection; off-main-thread failure leaves nothing partially registered, verified by a real `threading.Thread` attempt); 3 new in `tests/test_pretrain_cli.py`, using a deterministic fake tracker (becomes "requested" on its Nth *read* of `.requested`, since the real callback reads it exactly once per completed update) rather than a racy real concurrent signal mid-test -- a real functional run confirms an emergency checkpoint lands at the exact update the request arrived on, `GracefulTerminationRequested` propagates with the checkpoint path in its message, no `final/` gets written (a graceful early stop is not a completed run), and handlers are restored afterward; a second test confirms `--no-checkpoint` stops cleanly with nothing written; a third confirms handlers are restored even when an unrelated real exception crashes the run. Full fast suite: 573 passed (up from 562), `ruff check`/`format --check` clean on all four changed/new files.

### MF-147 — Benchmarking against 11 real educational/small-model LLM repos: introduction.md gaps, code-quality audit, and real reference implementations for existing backlog leads

- **Priority / state:** P3 mostly Not started (real, sourced findings); one item Done (tokenizer.py cleanup); one item resolved as a user decision (script docstring convention)
- **Depends on:** none structurally; several items feed directly into already-open leads ([[MF-112]], [[MF-135]] item 4, the Muon-vs-AdamW result)
- **Deliverable:** user-requested benchmark against 11 real, local `reference/` archives (`LLMs-from-scratch-main.zip`, `reasoning-from-scratch-main.zip`, `minimind-master.zip`, `miniGPT-main.zip`, `train-a-model-from-scratch-master.zip`, `llm-350m-master.zip`, `modded-nanogpt-master.zip`, `nanoGPT-master (1).zip`, `llm.c-master (1).zip`, `beyond-nanogpt-main.zip`, `modern-llm-notebook-main.zip`) -- five parallel research passes (four grouped by theme across the 11 repos, one auditing MiniFrontier's own `src/`/`train/`/`labs/`/`scripts/` directly), explicitly framed as "no mega refactor, most code already works" -- a targeted-improvement search, not a rewrite.
  1. **Real, direct verdict on the "professional-lab-standard" question, from a dedicated code audit (not self-assessment): the codebase already meets the bar it was measured against.** Tensor-shape documentation at every reshape/transpose/cache/attention boundary is consistently present and genuinely explanatory; comments are overwhelmingly WHY-focused (citing papers, prior MF-NNN ablations, real measured tradeoffs), not restatements; naming has no cross-file synonym drift; the hardest non-obvious-logic cases (`cache.py`'s ring-buffer rollback, `checkpoint.py`'s double-rename atomicity, `training.py`'s FP16-scaler-vs-BF16-isfinite split, `muon.py`'s Newton-Schulz intuition) are all clearly explained at the right level. Only two real findings survived scrutiny out of a full read of every core `src/` file plus samples from `scripts/`/`labs/`:
     - **Done (2026-09-18): a stale, restating comment block in `tokenizer.py` (was lines 366-373, now deleted)** -- the one place in the codebase found violating the project's own WHY-not-WHAT convention (it restated *what* a pre-tokenizer does, duplicating content the file already covers better elsewhere). Zero-risk deletion; full tokenizer test suite (35 tests) re-confirmed passing, `ruff` clean.
     - **Resolved as a real user decision (2026-09-18): scripts should keep `--help` terse, with the fuller why/how rationale living in a comment block below the one-line docstring, not inside the docstring itself.** A sampled check of 4 of the real 38 `scripts/` files found this split inconsistently applied (`eval_checkpoint.py` puts the full rationale in its docstring; `sample.py`/`prepare_data.py` keep the docstring terse and the rationale in a comment) -- the terse-docstring pattern is now the one project-wide convention. **Not yet applied**: a real pass across all 38 scripts to find every file not yet following it (only `eval_checkpoint.py` is confirmed, from the sampled 4, to need its rationale moved out of the docstring) -- real, bounded work, not urgent, not scoped as its own task yet given the small sample size so far.
  2. **Real, genuinely new introduction.md gaps** (beyond what's already covered this session -- do not re-flag anything already added):
     - **DeepSeek Sparse Attention (DSA)**, real and named, from `LLMs-from-scratch`'s own `ch04/09_dsa/gpt_with_kv_dsa.py`: a "Lightning Indexer" scores every past token per query via a gated sum of `ReLU(q*k)` dot products, then keeps only the top-K highest-scoring tokens. Architecturally distinct from MiniFrontier's own static, position-based local/global windowing -- this is **learned, per-query dynamic sparsity**, a genuinely different mechanism.
     - **Inference-time scaling as a distinct axis from RL training** (`reasoning-from-scratch`'s `ch04`-`ch05`): self-consistency (sample N, majority-vote), best-of-N, self-refinement, chain-of-thought prompting -- all real, named, zero-training-change techniques. More directly relevant to MiniFrontier's own actual `generation.py` sampling code than RLVR/GRPO is, since these need no training stage at all (RLVR is already correctly excluded from the core).
     - **YaRN RoPE scaling now has a real, concrete, readable reference implementation** (minimind's `model/model_minimind.py`, `precompute_freqs_cis`'s `rope_scaling` branch -- beta_fast/beta_slow/factor/original_max_position_embeddings, the real "linear ramp" calculation) -- closes the "no primary source" gap on this already-flagged-missing introduction.md item from an earlier round this session.
  3. **Real, concrete code-quality/structural patterns worth adopting for future `labs/` additions (not a rewrite of existing labs)**:
     - **Matched baseline+modified file pairs**: `LLMs-from-scratch`'s advanced-technique subdirectories ship the unmodified baseline file directly alongside the modified one (e.g. `gpt_with_kv_mha.py` next to `gpt_with_kv_gqa.py`), so a reader can `diff` the two to see exactly what one technique changes.
     - **Explicit diff-region markers plus a "why" prose block**: `beyond-nanogpt`'s `train_mtp.py` marks `## MTP CHANGES HERE ##`/`## MTP CHANGES FINISH ##` around the exact delta from its own baseline `train_naive.py`, with a substantial top-of-file explanation of the real reasoning (e.g. why N separate heads rather than one head over N×V -- a real memory argument).
     - **Staged capability builds with real measured numbers at each stage**: `beyond-nanogpt`'s `dataloaders/dataloader{0,1,2}.py` + `bench_dataloader.py` progression (naive -> increasingly parallel, each independently runnable and benchmarked) -- the same ablation discipline MiniFrontier's own architecture work already uses, applied to systems code specifically.
     - Neither pattern requires touching existing labs -- both are real, adoptable conventions for whenever a new lab gets added.
  4. **Real, concrete hardware-profiling gap, worth a future backlog task**: `train-a-model-from-scratch` ships a single `benchmark.py` sweeping training speed/inference speed/VRAM across all its own configurations, plus a real GPU-to-recommended-profile-and-batch-size lookup table in its README (RTX 2060/8GB, RTX 3090/24GB, RTX 4090/24GB, each with a real matching config+batch-size). **MiniFrontier has no equivalent** -- real hardware numbers exist, but scattered across many separate `reports/*.md` files and `AGENTS.md` prose, all for one card (the RTX 2070 Super), with no single sweep script or single "which config for which card" table. Real, concrete, not urgent -- not yet scoped as its own MF-number.
  5. **Real reference implementations now exist for two backlog leads that previously had none**:
     - **4-bit KV-cache quantization**: `train-a-model-from-scratch`'s `PolarQuantizer`/`TurboQuantKVCache` (`model.py:196-244`) -- decomposes each cached K/V vector into a norm and a unit direction, quantizes each separately. Directly relevant to [[MF-135]] item 4 (quantized/paged KV cache), which previously only had a landscape summary, no concrete implementation to reference.
     - **Hyper-Connections**: the same file's `sinkhorn`/`MHCResidual`/`MHCExpand`/`MHCCollapse` (lines 41-77) -- a Sinkhorn-normalized doubly-stochastic mixing matrix over N parallel residual streams. Directly relevant to [[MF-112]] (Manifold-constrained Hyper-Connections), previously just a name with no local reference implementation.
  6. **Muon has evolved past this project's own tested comparison -- a real caveat on an existing result, not a call to re-test now.** `modded-nanogpt`'s current `train_gpt.py` implements **NorMuon** (a second per-row-variance-reduction momentum buffer, plus scheduled momentum 0.85->0.95 over 300 steps then cooled) and replaces Newton-Schulz with **Polar Express** orthogonalization (arXiv:2505.16932) -- both real, primary-sourced, and postdating the vanilla-Muon-vs-AdamW comparison this project already ran and recorded (AdamW won on wall-clock-matched quality, [[MF-057]]/[[MF-070]]'s pre-work). This doesn't invalidate that result -- it's a real, honest caveat that the comparison was against vanilla Muon specifically, and a current, more sophisticated Muon variant hasn't been tested. Not scoped as a real bounded-ablation task now -- would need real GPU time this project's current priority ([[MF-070]]'s live run) doesn't have to spare.
  7. **Confirmed non-issues, real verification, not assumed**:
     - **The `torch.load`/`weights_only` PyTorch 2.6+ default-change gotcha (a real, documented pain point in `llm-350m`'s own `RETROSPECTIVE.md` -- their checkpoint loading silently broke) does not apply to MiniFrontier.** Verified directly: there is exactly **one** `torch.load` call in the entire `src/`+`train/` tree (`checkpoint.py:268`), and it already explicitly sets `weights_only=False` behind a guarded, documented `trusted_local_state` flag -- never relying on an implicit, version-dependent default. Directly relevant given the live [[MF-070]] run's own `--resume` path depends on exactly this code.
     - **RoPE convention cross-check**: `llm.c`'s real Llama3 implementation (`train_llama3.py:104`) uses the **interleaved** convention (adjacent-pair rotation), the opposite of MiniFrontier's own confirmed rotate-half/NEOX convention. Neither is a bug -- both are real, self-consistent, production conventions -- good primary-source grounding for a fact already stated in introduction.md, not a new correction.
     - Tensor-shape-comment convention, MTP/GQA/RoPE/RMSNorm/SwiGLU/KV-cache coverage, and general code cleanliness were all independently confirmed to already match or exceed these 11 real comparable repos -- several (`minimind`'s language-model cluster, `beyond-nanogpt`'s own simpler transformer) were found to implement a *less* sophisticated version of what MiniFrontier already has (plain learned positions instead of RoPE, plain LayerNorm instead of RMSNorm, no GQA).
- **Acceptance:** N/A until any individual item is scoped -- items 2-6 each need the same real primary-source-then-bounded-test discipline as every other lead already in this backlog; item 1's script-convention pass needs a real tally across all 38 scripts before applying broadly.
- **Status note:** the code-audit verdict (item 1's overall finding) and the two confirmed non-issues (item 7) are the most load-bearing results here -- real, direct evidence the codebase already reaches the standard it was being benchmarked against, not just an aspiration. Everything else is real, sourced, and recorded per `AGENTS.md`'s "add newly discovered work instead of hiding it" rule; [[MF-070]]'s live run remains the current priority.

### MF-148 — Benchmarking against 6 real full-stack open-model releases (OLMo 3, Nemotron 3 Super, Pythia, LLM360 Amber/CrystalCoder, BigScience BLOOM, SmolLM2): real leads for `training.py`, `shards.py`, eval, and data governance

- **Priority / state:** P3, all items Not started
- **Depends on:** none structurally; several items feed existing open leads ([[MF-095]]'s data-curriculum question, [[MF-107]]'s partial-RoPE precedent list) or existing memories (`modded-nanogpt-muon-followup.md`)
- **Deliverable:** user-requested benchmark against `reference/stack-training-ref/` (`OLMo-core-main.zip` + `OLMo-main.zip`, `Nemotron-main.zip` + `RL-main.zip`, `pythia-main.zip` + `amber-data-prep-main.zip` + `amber-train-main.zip` + `crystalcoder-data-prep-master.zip` + `crystalcoder-train-master.zip`, `data-preparation-main.zip` + `multilingual-modeling-master.zip` + `tokenizers-main.zip`) -- four parallel research forks, one per group, explicitly framed as "no mega refactor, most code already works," same discipline as [[MF-147]]. SmolLM2 was not re-researched here -- already deep-dived earlier this session (its alignment-handbook SFT/DPO recipes + smollm pretraining repo), reflected throughout the data-mixture/tokenizer-fertility entries already in this backlog.
  1. **Real, concrete gap: no statistical spike/outlier detection on loss or grad norm.** AI2's OLMo-core (`core/src/olmo_core/optim/skip_step_optimizer.py`) tracks a rolling window (default 128 steps) of loss/grad-norm, computes mean/std, and **completely voids** the optimizer step (not just clips it) when the current value exceeds `sigma_factor` (default 6) standard deviations above the rolling mean. MiniFrontier's `training.py` only has plain gradient clipping (`gradient_clip: float = 1.0`) -- confirmed via grep, no rolling-window/z-score/skip-step mechanism exists anywhere. A real, different, complementary mechanism (clipping softens a large gradient; this refuses an anomalous step outright), not redundant with what's already there.
  2. **Cheaper first step toward item 1, worth doing before it regardless of whether item 1 itself gets adopted:** OLMo-core's `StabilityMonitorCallback` (`train/callbacks/stability_monitor.py`) uses the identical rolling z-score idea but only *observes* -- logs a `spike/SpikeScore` metric, takes no action. Instrumenting this first would show how often MiniFrontier's own real training runs (including the live [[MF-070]] run, retroactively from its logged loss values) would have triggered a skip, before deciding whether acting on it is worth the complexity.
  3. **Real, lower-urgency gap: `MixtureBatchProvider`/`shards.py` has no explicit repetition/consumption safety cap.** OLMo-core's `SourceMixtureConfig` (`core/src/olmo_core/data/source_mixture.py`) validates `max_repetition_ratio` (caps how much a source can be upsampled) and `max_source_fraction` (caps how much of a source's total data may be consumed) -- confirmed absent from `shards.py` via grep. Not urgent for the current, already-fixed, already-real [[MF-070]] mixture, but a real, cheap addition worth doing before any future mixture-ratio experiment, since nothing today stops an accidentally-tiny source from being silently over-repeated.
  4. **Real, confirmed gap, low urgency: code eval is single-sample pass/fail, not pass@k.** Flagged by the Nemotron/RL fork (NeMo RL's own `EvalConfig` has `num_tests_per_prompt`/`k_value` fields for exactly this), then independently verified directly: `src/minifrontier/evaluation/code.py`'s `score_python`/`score_fixture_predictions` and `scripts/eval_code.py` both operate on exactly one prediction string per fixture id, read from a pre-generated predictions file -- no multi-sample generation, no pass@k estimator anywhere. Real gap relative to standard code-eval practice (HumanEval-style), but likely genuinely low-urgency given this project deliberately uses a small set of *original* fixtures (not a large public benchmark) specifically to avoid contamination -- pass@k's value is highest exactly where sample noise matters most, i.e. large benchmark suites this project intentionally doesn't use.
  5. **Real, cheap, additive feature: dense early-training checkpoints.** Pythia's own real training configs (`pythia-main/models/70M/pythia-70m.yml`) set `checkpoint-factor: 1000` plus `extra-save-iters: [0,1,2,4,8,16,32,64,128,256,512]` -- regular periodic checkpoints *plus* a deliberate power-of-2 dense schedule right at the start, specifically to make early learning dynamics observable. MiniFrontier's `--checkpoint-interval` (`train/pretrain.py`) only supports one fixed interval. Not urgent (this project's goal is a usable model, not an interpretability suite like Pythia's), but cheap if ever wanted -- e.g. an `--extra-checkpoint-updates` list.
  6. **Real, distinct, uninvestigated curriculum hypothesis -- explicitly not a re-litigation of [[MF-095]].** CrystalCoder's real published training phases (`crystalcoder-data-prep-master`/`crystalcoder-train-master`'s `pretrain/params/{phase1,phase2,phase3}`) withhold code entirely from phases 1-2, introducing it (plus FIM augmentation) only in phase 3. This is a different curriculum axis than [[MF-095]] (which tested reweighting an *already-uniform* mixture near the end of training and found no benefit) -- CrystalCoder withholds an entire domain until a later phase, rather than reweighting existing sources throughout. Genuinely untested by this project either way; not itself independently ablated by CrystalCoder either (Cerebras's own house recipe, not a result they proved in isolation) -- a real, separate hypothesis worth recording, not a proven technique to adopt.
  7. **Real, low-priority gap, honestly scoped: no PII pattern-matching for natural-language prose.** BigScience/BLOOM's real ROOTS pipeline (`preprocessing/training/02_pii/bigscience_pii_detect_redact.py`) has a dedicated PII-detect-and-redact stage; MiniFrontier's `code_data.py` has real, equivalent protection for *code* (`_SECRET_PATTERNS`, `_VENDOR_PARTS`, `_GENERATED_MARKERS`, matching `docs/DATA_GOVERNANCE.md`'s MF-051 claims, verified not just claimed) but nothing analogous exists anywhere in `src/` for names/emails/phone numbers in prose. Low priority today since every current real prose source (FineWeb-Edu, DCLM-Edu, Cosmopedia V2, FineMath) is itself the already-filtered output of its publisher's own pipeline -- would become real if a future raw/uncurated prose source is ever added. `docs/DATA_GOVERNANCE.md`'s "approved personal-data policy" bullet is aspirational for the prose path specifically until then.
  8. **Two citations worth adding to already-existing content, both now done (2026-09-19, same session as this benchmarking pass):** `match_rms_adamw`'s real published precedent is the Kimi/Moonlight paper (arXiv:2502.16982) -- OLMo-core's own `MuonConfig.adjust_lr`'s `rms_norm` strategy implements the identical technique, cited the same way; added to `introduction.md`. A second, independently-cited "NorMuon" exists too: OLMo-core's `NorMuonConfig` cites arXiv:2510.05491, distinct from modded-nanogpt's arXiv:2505.16932 already in `modded-nanogpt-muon-followup.md` -- that memory now records both. Pythia's own real config unties embeddings even at 70M (`no-weight-tying: true`) and uses partial RoPE at every scale (`rotary-pct: 0.25`, predating DeepSeek-V4/Qwen3-Next) -- `introduction.md`'s tied-vs-untied paragraph and `docs/IMPLEMENTATION_DECISIONS.md`'s MF-107 entry were both corrected/extended with these real counter-examples/precedents.
  9. **Confirmed non-issues and corroborations, real verification not assumption:**
     - **BPE trainer determinism, actually verified, not assumed a risk.** The real Rust `tokenizers` library's `BpeTrainer` uses `AHashMap` (no iteration-order guarantee) and parallel pair-counting, which looks like a nondeterminism risk on the surface -- verified it isn't: `Merge::cmp` breaks count-ties by comparing the pair's own content, not hashmap/iteration order, so the chosen merge sequence is genuinely deterministic given the same corpus regardless of parallelism. Directly relevant to this project's own "deterministic training" claims.
     - **BPB as the cross-tokenizer eval metric**: OLMo's real eval suite (`eval/task_groups.py`) uses BPB-suffixed task variants throughout -- independent confirmation this project's own choice to report bits-per-byte (not just raw CE/perplexity) matches current frontier-lab practice, not just this project's own reasoning.
     - **Muon and chunked cross-entropy, independently adopted elsewhere.** NeMo RL's own v0.6.0 release notes list "Muon Optimizer" and "Chunked Cross Entropy Loss" as new supported features -- the same two real, from-scratch techniques this project already has, adopted independently by a large NVIDIA production library around the same time.
     - **Checkpoint mechanism, eval breadth, and distributed/cloud infra all correctly out of scope, not incomplete.** OLMo-core's `Checkpointer` is built entirely around `torch.distributed.checkpoint` multi-rank/remote-URL coordination -- a fundamentally different problem than MiniFrontier's single-process atomic-rename guarantee (`checkpoint.py`), not comparable or adoptable. Its dozens of BPB-based generative eval tasks suit 1B-32B commercial scale, not a gap at 50-500M educational scale. The large majority of `olmo_core`'s surface area (`distributed/`, `float8/`, `kernels/`, `launch/`, cloud-logging callbacks) and all of NeMo RL's Ray/PPO/GRPO/DPO machinery are exactly what `AGENTS.md`'s frozen scope already excludes -- confirms the scope discipline is sound, not that anything is missing.
     - **`multilingual-modeling-master.zip` confirmed genuinely inapplicable** (BLOOM+1 cross-lingual PEFT/LoRA/MAD-X adapters for an already-multilingual model) rather than assumed so -- MiniFrontier is single-language and PEFT-free by frozen scope.
     - **Amber's packed-data provenance is coarser than MiniFrontier's own `Document`/`RawDocument` schema** (a `source` string per packed 2049-token sample vs. `source`/`revision`/`license`/`content_hash`/`parent_content_hash` per original document) -- no gap, already stronger here.
     - **`src/minifrontier/tokenizer.py` reviewed against the real `tokenizers` library source directly -- no gap found.** Its regex patterns were already verified against tiktoken's actual source (an earlier session caught a misquoted digit clause from secondhand web results), it documents a real workaround for an actual `tokenizers`-library parsing bug (possessive quantifiers mis-parsed as repeated groups), and its lack of a `Normalizer` is the correct convention for byte-level BPE, not an oversight.
- **Acceptance:** N/A until any individual item is scoped -- items 1-7 each need their own real bounded test or implementation before being called done; item 8's citations/corrections are already applied as part of this entry, not pending.
- **Status note:** items 1-3 (OLMo-core) are the most concretely actionable of the not-yet-started items -- a real, different training-stability mechanism this project doesn't have, with a cheap diagnostic-only first step already identified. Item 7 (PII) is honestly low-priority given current data sources are pre-filtered upstream. [[MF-070]]'s live run remains the current priority; nothing here blocks or should interrupt it.
